## 身份 / Identity（14）

### 1. 🔴 Agent ontology spec (agent_spec / ability_spec)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The descriptor fields in AbilityManifest (name/description/input_schema/output_schema/version/visibility/AccessPolicy) and AgentSpec.name re-implement, in CLI-local Rust, the SAME contract Axon already defines in proto: CapabilityDescriptor (ability_id/name/version/tags), MCPToolSpec/MCPToolSpecSummary and A2ASkillSpec (description/input_schema/output_schema — types.proto:1050-1141), and AgentIdentity (ura/profile — types.proto:314). Two different-language nodes must AGREE on this descriptor to interoperate, so it is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon; a hand-rolled CLI copy with no codegen link is a silent protocol fork (the exact R3-style "silent divergence" the interface review warns about — CLI already links easynet-axon but does NOT derive these types from it). The executor half (AbilityExec variants, ShellExec argv templating, RuntimeKind, timeout-to-Duration boundary, dispatch-router from_qualified_name) is correctly CLI — it is how this device runs/orchestrates, must NOT sink into the neutral protocol.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the descriptor half (agent name, ability name/description/io-schema/visibility/access-policy) is exactly the URA-addressable, governable contract the endgame needs (owner device advertises an ability addressed by URA, governed by Axon, auditable). It serves the value. But it serves it from the wrong place: today it is a CLI-local Rust ontology that no other-language node can agree on, so it cannot yet anchor cross-language interop. The executor half (AbilityExec shell/http/eal/mcp, RuntimeKind) genuinely serves "how this device runs the ability" and belongs where it is.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the EXECUTOR layer: AbilityExec (shell/http/eal/mcp configs), RuntimeKind, argv minijinja templating, timeout-seconds-to-Duration boundary, AbilityKind::from_qualified_name dispatch routing, agent.toml/ability.toml on-disk TOML serde and validate(). CLI should derive the DESCRIPTOR fields (name/description/io-schema/visibility) from Axon's generated types (it already links easynet-axon at Cargo.toml:137) rather than re-declaring them, projecting agent.toml -> Axon CapabilityDescriptor/MCPToolSpec at the publish boundary. Net: CLI authors and runs; Axon owns the descriptor shape.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the agent/ability DESCRIPTOR contract as the neutral cross-language shape: AgentIdentity (URA, already present), CapabilityDescriptor + MCPToolSpec/A2ASkillSpec carrying name/description/input_schema/output_schema (already present). Axon should absorb the remaining CLI-only descriptor semantics that are genuinely contract — Visibility tier (self/device/public) and AccessPolicy (allow_callers/deny_callers, deny-wins ordering) are governance fields that two nodes must agree on to enforce permission consistently, and are NOT yet in the proto. Per F-01 sec7, Axon absorbs this ontology back so CLI stops being the de-facto source of truth.
- **现状：** forked. Descriptor model lives in CLI at EasyNet-Cli/src/core/ability_spec.rs (88KB: AbilityManifest name/description/input_schema/output_schema/Visibility/AccessPolicy) and agent_spec.rs (35KB: AgentSpec.name/RuntimeKind). The overlapping contract already exists independently in EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto (AgentIdentity:314, CapabilityDescriptor:746, MCPToolSpec:1067, A2ASkillSpec:1135). CLI links easynet-axon (Cargo.toml:137) but does NOT derive these descriptor types from it. The interface review docs/cli-axon-interface-review-2026-05-30.md R4 logs the broader CLI-local-copy migration as a controlled transitional state with no named owner/deadline; R3 names "silent fork" as the most dangerous redundancy class. Visibility/AccessPolicy governance fields are MISSING from Axon proto entirely.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption. Split the verdict and act on the contract half only: (1) draft an Axon RFC that promotes the descriptor + governance fields (Visibility tier, AccessPolicy allow/deny + deny-wins ordering) into CapabilityDescriptor/AgentIdentity so they become the neutral cross-language shape — this is Axon-repo work, and per RFC-003 spec §0 the CLI may NOT modify the axon proto unilaterally, so it must go through an Axon RFC (confirmed by Cargo.toml:371 and the interface-review political-reality note). (2) Once Axon lands it, make CLI derive descriptor fields from the generated easynet-axon types and keep ONLY the executor layer (AbilityExec/RuntimeKind/templating) local. (3) Assign the R4 "migration-completion owner + deadline" the interface review flags as missing, so the fork does not become permanent. Do NOT push AbilityExec/RuntimeKind into Axon — that would over-sink runtime into the neutral protocol.

### 2. 🔴 AgentId/agent_spec absorbed back into Axon as neutral contract

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — the identity NAMING SHAPE must be agreed cross-language, so by the boundary criterion it is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon, which already has it. But CLI runs a divergent shape: CLI src/core/agent_id.rs uses `<tenant>/<name>` (ontology §6.4, self-described as NOT a URA parser, URA is a future L3 layer), while Axon sdk/rust/src/ura.rs:208 + proto AgentIdentity (types.proto:314) already provide `/r/<realm>/agent/<user-id>.<agent-id>` with typed parse/build in Rust/Go/Python. Two incompatible agent-identity surfaces = protocol fork. NOTE: the feature premise (AgentId not in Axon / none-missing) is inaccurate — Axon HAS the agent URA contract; the gap is CLI not adopting it. Separately, agent_spec.rs (agent.toml: runtime driver claude-code/codex, on-disk projection) and ability_spec.rs (exec binding shell/http/mcp, cost) are CORRECT-as-CLI runtime/executor ontology — absorbing THOSE into Axon would be product/runtime over-sink.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — agent identity is the spine of the endgame: the user owns agents, abilities are addressed by a URA that two different-language nodes must resolve identically. If CLI's `<tenant>/<name>` and Axon's `/r/<realm>/agent/<user>.<agent>` never converge, a Python node and a Rust node cannot agree on who they are addressing, breaking addressable + governable. Convergence on the Axon URA shape directly serves owner-identity + URA-addressing.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the RUNTIME/EXECUTOR ontology it correctly owns: AgentSpec (how a device drives an agent process — runtime driver, knobs, on-disk .mcp.json/config.toml projection) and AbilitySpec.exec (how an ability actually runs). CLI should STOP defining its own logical-identity shape (AgentId = `<tenant>/<name>`) and instead consume Axon's URA agent type (Ura::agent / AgentIdentity) as the canonical addressable identity, mapping its `<tenant>/<name>` surface sugar onto the Axon URA at the boundary rather than as a parallel ontology.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already provides the neutral contract: AgentIdentity proto (types.proto:314 — URA + profile + reserved key_id/rotation_epoch) and typed cross-language URA builders/parsers (sdk/rust/src/ura.rs Ura::agent / ParsedURA / URAKind::Agent / AgentMissingTail|AgentBadShape; sdk/go/easynet/ura.go; python). Axon should NOT absorb agent_spec/ability_spec runtime ontology. Remaining Axon-side gap: a contract-blessed mapping/normalization note for CLI `<tenant>/<name>` ↔ Axon `/r/<realm>/agent/<user>.<agent>`, and deterministic profile handling of the CLI default-tenant shorthand.
- **现状：** forked — Axon: AgentIdentity URA shape SHIPPED (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:314; sdk/rust/src/ura.rs:208,117; sdk/go/easynet/ura.go). CLI: divergent AgentId `<tenant>/<name>` SHIPPED and pervasive (EasyNet-Cli/src/core/agent_id.rs:75, used across src/eal/* and src/runtime/* — 30+ files), explicitly disclaiming URA at agent_id.rs:18-19. agent_spec.rs:141 and ability_spec.rs (exec/cost) are CLI-only and correctly so. Net: identity-shape = forked; runtime ontology = correctly CLI, not missing from Axon.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption (de-fork, not new-absorb): contract already lives in Axon, so the work is CLI-side convergence — make CLI AgentId a thin surface over Axon's URA agent type instead of a parallel ontology, with a boundary mapper for `<tenant>/<name>` shorthand. Step 1: CLI decision doc reconciling AGENT_IDENTITY.md §6.4 `<tenant>/<name>` vs Axon URA `/r/<realm>/agent/<user>.<agent>`, pick one canonical shape (bias: Axon URA wins, per boundary criterion + F-01 sec7 Axon-absorbs-ontology-back). Step 2 (Axon-repo): extend/clarify AgentIdentity profile handling for CLI shorthand and publish the normalization note — do NOT add a second agent identity message. Do NOT sink agent_spec/ability_spec into Axon (violates runtime/executor boundary). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is contract-touching Axon work and MUST enter via the sec0 spec gate, not a silent SDK edit.

### 3. 🔴 BootstrapSelfIdentity (runtime.bootstrap_self_identity)

- **裁决：** `FORKED — the ability `runtime.bootstrap_self_identity` exists in TWO repos with incompatible semantics. Axon serves the REAL handler (runtime_admin.rs:339) that decodes the ed25519 key, validates 32 bytes, and writes node_key_materials/node_keys with first-writer-wins + append-on-new-key — exactly what its own verify_easynet_subject_key_binding gate (security.rs:537) requires. The CLI carries a SECOND handler (federation_wrappers.rs:431 handle_bootstrap_self_identity) that ignores the key and returns {ack:true} as a no-op, and the daemon dispatch table (daemon_invocation_service.rs:980) intercepts the ability name BEFORE it reaches the embedded Axon LocalRuntime — so the CLI fork shadows the contract and drops the key. By the boundary criterion this is a protocol fork: two nodes must AGREE the key is actually registered to interoperate, yet the CLI keeps a divergent copy of the handler. NOTE: the CALLER side + key derivation (publish.rs:403, derive_owner_public_key_b64 / derive_hub_public_key_b64) is correctly CLI (runtime: how this device makes its own identity reachable). Only the daemon-side no-op HANDLER is the misplaced fork.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — cold-start identity provisioning is the precondition for the whole endgame: until the daemon's own verifying key is pinned in the runtime's trusted-key table, every signed Invoke fails AXON_EASYNET_SUBJECT_KEY_UNREGISTERED (Axon security.rs:537,606), so no URA is reachable, no ability returns a Surface, and easynet-verify has nothing to audit. It is the bootstrap that makes "user owns identity, ability addressed by URA, governed by Axon" physically possible on a fresh device.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the CALLER role only: at boot/heartbeat/join (boot.rs:1191, start.rs:520, heartbeat.rs:355) derive the deterministic agent key and hub key (publish.rs:431,448) and invoke runtime.bootstrap_self_identity against the runtime, plus write the hub (uri,pubkey,role=hub) trust row from join's hub_public_key_b64 (join.rs:51-59) into realm-trust.toml. CLI should DELETE its own handle_bootstrap_self_identity no-op (federation_wrappers.rs:431) and remove the ABILITY_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAP_SELF_IDENTITY arm from the daemon dispatch table (daemon_invocation_service.rs:980) so the call falls through to the embedded Axon LocalRuntime that actually registers the key. This is RUNTIME (identity-key provisioning + reachability), legitimately CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the contract: the runtime.bootstrap_self_identity runtime-admin ability that mutates the trusted-key table (runtime_admin.rs:339) and the verify_easynet_subject_key_binding gate that consumes it (security.rs:537). Axon must also publish the BootstrapSelfIdentityArgs shape (tenant_id, node_id, owner_id, public_key_b64) as a neutral cross-language SDK builder/descriptor so any-language daemon agrees on the wire shape, since P5-rewrite-13 deleted the typed register_node RPC and left only this loosely-typed admin ability. The first-writer-wins + append-on-new-key semantics (so the agent key and hub key coexist under one node_id) are contract behaviour and stay in Axon.
- **现状：** forked — REAL handler shipped in Axon (EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/runtime_admin.rs:339; gate at runtime/security.rs:537). DUPLICATE no-op handler shipped in CLI (EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs:431) and wired into the daemon dispatch table at daemon_invocation_service.rs:980, shadowing Axon. Caller side shipped in CLI (publish.rs:403; invoked from boot.rs:1191, cli/start.rs:520, cli/heartbeat.rs:355). Join cold-start hub-key plumbing shipped (cli/join.rs:51-59). Note: Axon proto/SDK has NO BootstrapSelfIdentity message — only unrelated ReloadBootstrapConfig (admin.proto:176); the ability is JSON-args only, so the cross-language contract shape is under-specified.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup — remove the CLI's forked no-op: delete handle_bootstrap_self_identity (federation_wrappers.rs:431-435), its request/response structs if unused elsewhere, the ABILITY_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAP_SELF_IDENTITY dispatch arm (daemon_invocation_service.rs:980-982) and dispatch_runtime_bootstrap_self_identity (1350-1358), so the boot-time invoke falls through to Axon's real runtime_admin handler that actually registers the key. Then F-07 ontology-absorption on the Axon repo: promote BootstrapSelfIdentityArgs from loose JSON to a typed proto message + SDK builder so the cross-language contract shape is pinned (the register_node it replaced was typed; this regressed to untyped). RFC-003 sec0 note: Axon-repo work — the typed-message promotion is a wire-compat surface and must land coordinated with PR-4 schema-compat fixtures (federation_wrappers.rs:194-196 warns FEDERATION_ABILITIES order is a PR-4 fixture dependency); file under AXON-RFC-001 ontology absorption, not a CLI-local change.

### 4. 🔴 device join (redeem pairing token -> credentials)

- **裁决：** `FORKED — the runtime/UX half is correctly CLI, but the credential-provisioning CONTRACT is hand-mirrored across two languages with no neutral owner. The DeviceResp wire shape (node_id, credential_token, hub_endpoint, realm/tenant_id, deploy_signature, device_public_key hex, auth_binding, key_status) is defined twice — Go types in EasyNet backend and Rust structs in join.rs/config.rs — and both must AGREE byte-for-byte to interoperate. Per the boundary criterion that makes the wire shape a CONTRACT (belongs in Axon as the neutral identity-provisioning shape), and the dual hand-maintained definitions are a protocol fork. Note: this is NOT over-sink — the act of redeeming/writing creds is device runtime (stays CLI) and the act of minting is Hub (stays EasyNet); only the SHAPE of what crosses the wire is the forked contract. Axon today owns federation.join (realm-level pairing_secret) but has zero coverage of the device-credential-redemption shape.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the bootstrap of "user owns identity+state": it is the one act that lands a verifiable device identity (node_id + credential_token + device Ed25519 pubkey registered as hub trust anchor) on the owner device, making the URA addressable/online. Without join there is no credentials.json, no daemon identity, no reachable URA. Directly serves the endgame's identity+audit anchor.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the whole runtime/front-door half: the `easynet join` verb, token-format validation (fail-fast on typo), sysinfo collection, the preflight→validate→save_credentials state machine (run_join_stages, join.rs:198-237), being the SOLE writer of ~/.easynet/credentials.json, deriving the device Ed25519 keypair, autostart of the daemon, and username backfill from auth session. This is "how this device enrolls and lands identity locally" — correctly CLI EXECUTOR/RUNTIME/FRONT-DOOR. CLI should consume the Axon-defined credential shape instead of hand-rolling Rust structs.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should own the NEUTRAL device-credential-provisioning shape — the DeviceResp/credential envelope (node_id, credential_token, hub_endpoint, realm, deploy_signature, device_public_key, auth_binding, key_status) and the validate/preflight request/response types — as a cross-language contract (proto + generated Rust/Go/Python), exactly as it already owns federation.join. This stops the Go-vs-Rust hand-mirror drift (the file already documents one such drift: empty-username fallback handled ad hoc in both repos). Axon should NOT run the redemption, write the file, or mint tokens — only define the shape both sides agree on.
- **现状：** forked. CLI side shipped: EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/join.rs:128-237 (run_join_stages, preflight_pairing_token:591, validate_pairing_token:629), credential struct EasyNet-Cli/src/persistence/config.rs:389-409, sole writer save_credentials:237. Hub side shipped: EasyNet/backend/internal/logic/device/validatePairingLogic.go (ValidatePairing, DeviceResp build:243-260) + preflightPairingLogic.go. Axon: no device-credential contract — grep of Axon sdk/core finds only realm-level federation.join `pairing_secret` (dendrite_bridge.rs:657-670), an unrelated concept. The shared wire shape exists only as two hand-maintained mirrors.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: lift the device-credential-provisioning shape into Axon as a neutral contract (proto for ValidatePairing/Preflight req+resp and the credential envelope), generate Rust+Go bindings, then have join.rs and validatePairingLogic.go consume the generated types instead of hand-mirrored structs — same pattern as F-01 sec7 (CLI ran ahead, Axon absorbs the ontology back). Do NOT move the redemption/write/mint behavior; only the shape moves. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work — the new identity-provisioning contract must land via an RFC-003 sec0 contract entry before either repo's hand-rolled types are deleted, so the Go and Rust sides cut over to the generated shape in lockstep and avoid a transient double-fork.

### 5. ✅ AgentId / AbilityName / NodeId typed identity model

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI L2 runtime) — Applying the boundary criterion: the thing two different-language nodes must AGREE on to interoperate is the URA wire form (easynet:///r/.../agent.<user>.<agent>/abilities/<name>), and that contract ALREADY lives in Axon (sdk/rust/src/ura.rs parse + AgentBadShape/AbilityBadShape, mirrored in sdk/go|node|python). agent_id.rs is the device-local L2 runtime type that Rust code/IR/dispatcher hold between L1 EAL surface and L3 URA — its own AGENT_IDENTITY.md §1 names it the L2 layer and forbids string-equivalence with L3 (Constraint 1). It deliberately diverges from URA (ASCII-lowercase-only, full-form Display, default tenant, AbilityName allows '.', AgentId forbids '.'), so it is NOT a protocol fork of URA — it is the runtime resolution layer, which is exactly CLI's job. Pushing this struct into Axon would over-sink a runtime convenience into the neutral protocol.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — INV-3 ("ability is a method on an agent, no global registry") is the load-bearing rule for the endgame where ability lives on the owner device and is addressed by URA, not matched by a global provider directory. The typed L2 model encodes exactly that: AbilityName is deliberately NOT a peer of AgentId (no tenant, no cross-owner equality, not a registry key). It is the runtime-internal guard that keeps the system off the service-registry path the endgame rejects.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Owns the L2 runtime identity types (AgentId/AbilityName/NodeId) plus AgentId::parse / Display for L1-EAL-surface ↔ L2 transitions, as it does today. Should additionally own the typed L2↔L3 converter (AgentId -> URA owner-id and back) so the dispatcher never reaches L3 via string-formatting — AGENT_IDENTITY.md §2 Constraint 1 forbids the format! shortcut and the converter is the sanctioned path. The converter consumes Axon's URA parser; it does not re-implement URA validation.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Owns the L3 cross-language identity/addressing contract: URA shape and parse/validation (user_id / agent_id / ability_id, AgentBadShape/AbilityBadShape, agent_id-must-not-contain-'.') in sdk/rust/src/ura.rs with language-SDK parity, plus the proto-level tenant_id/node_id/principal fields. Axon does NOT need a typed AgentId message — the wire carries plain strings and the URA parser is the canonical agreement point. Per F-01 sec7, Axon's role is to keep URA as the absorbed ontology that CLI's L2 maps onto, not to mirror CLI's struct.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI L2): EasyNet-Cli/src/core/agent_id.rs:75 (AgentId), :191 (AbilityName), :271 (NodeId), spec at EasyNet-Cli/docs/AGENT_IDENTITY.md. shipped (Axon L3): EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/ura.rs:194/389/451-465 (URA parse + ability/agent shape validation), tenant in core/proto/axon/v1/identity.proto:219-273. partial: the L2↔L3 typed converter is explicitly deferred ("when L3 arrives", AGENT_IDENTITY.md L2-line "for L1↔L2 today"); no production caller of NodeId::as_str/into_string yet (both #[allow(dead_code)], agent_id.rs:321,330) — boundaries still pass &str.
- **行动：** Keep agent_id.rs in CLI as-is; do NOT migrate it to Axon. Next step (F-07 ontology-absorption): add the typed L2↔L3 converter in CLI (AgentId <-> Axon URA owner-id) that delegates to Axon's ura.rs parser, replacing any string-formatted L3 access and lifting the NodeId #[allow(dead_code)] once boundaries carry the typed value end-to-end. No Axon-repo change required for this feature, so no RFC-003 sec0 entry — Axon's URA contract already covers the cross-language agreement; confirm the converter's char-class divergence (L2 ASCII-lowercase vs L3 NFC) is documented at the converter, not re-litigated.

### 6. ✅ auth login / register (user session identity)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — this is "what the user sees / hub routing": product account login (email+password → product JWT), owned by EasyNet hub (backend/internal/logic/auth/loginLogic.go), with CLI as a thin HTTP front-door client (the ssh/gh/kubectl pattern). It is NOT a cross-language interop contract: two foreign-language nodes do not need to agree on EasyNet's email/password login shape to interoperate — they agree on Axon's principal/token contract (identity.proto IssueToken/WhoAmI/principal_type) which is separate and untouched here. So it is not a protocol fork. The one watch-item: the CLI hand-rolls AuthResp{token,user{id,nickname,username}} as a bespoke JSON struct (auth.rs:144-159) coupled to the hub's ad-hoc /api/v1/auth shape — that is product/transport coupling within the EasyNet product, acceptable for a front door, not a neutral-protocol concern.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it is the front door to "user owns identity": logging into the EasyNet account that anchors who the human is. But the endgame identity that matters to the protocol (URA / device signing key / principal) is the device keyring identity (src/runtime/keyring/*, config::load_credentials), not this hub JWT. The hub account session is a product-account convenience that gives whoami an email and a user_id; it does not by itself make a URA reachable or sign a Receipt. So it serves the product layer of identity, not the protocol layer.
- **CLI 预期实现：** FRONT-DOOR role: provide easynet auth login / logout / whoami verbs that POST to the hub auth endpoints, persist the resulting product JWT + user_id to ~/.easynet/auth.json (mode 0600), and let auth-aware commands pick it up — exactly what auth.rs:161-247 + load_session/save_session do. CLI should NOT invent its own credential cryptography or its own principal/token semantics; the JWT is minted by the hub. CLI correctly keeps this distinct from the runtime device key (keyring). This is shipped and in the right layer.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for this feature — Axon must NOT carry email/password product login. Axon's identity surface (core/proto/axon/v1/identity.proto: IssueToken with principal_type user|node|service, WhoAmI, RevokeToken, tenants) is the neutral cross-language principal/token contract and is the right home for *principal* identity, but the EasyNet product account login is a hub product concern, not protocol. Sinking email/password login into Axon would be over-sink / product-into-protocol leak.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI: EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/auth.rs:161-247 (run_login, post_login, post_register, http_post_json) + AuthSession persistence at auth.rs:48-112; whoami at auth.rs:292+. Hub: EasyNet/backend/api/easynet.api auth group (Register/Login/SocialLogin/Refresh) with logic in backend/internal/logic/auth/loginLogic.go minting its own JWT. The "cli+axon" location label is misleading: the actual login implementation is cli+hub(EasyNet); Axon's identity.proto is imported by the hub for node/principal identity but is NOT in this login path. No real Axon dependency in the login flow.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — keep login as CLI front-door + EasyNet hub. Frame F-03 (surface): correct the accounting label from "cli+axon" to "cli+easynet"; this feature does not touch Axon. Optional hardening, F-05 (cleanup): the bespoke AuthResp struct (auth.rs:144-159) and the /api/v1/auth path strings are duplicated CLI-side knowledge of the hub's product API shape — if/when an EasyNet product-API client crate emerges, fold these into it so the front-door stops hand-coding the hub JSON contract. Not an Axon-repo change, so no RFC-003 sec0 note required.

### 7. ✅ auth logout

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (for the local half) but with a MISSING contract half. Tearing down ~/.easynet/auth.json is purely "how this device stops holding the identity key" = CLI runtime, correctly placed. However server-side token invalidation is a thing two different-language nodes must agree on (issuer revokes, every verifier must honor) — that is CONTRACT and already exists as Axon identity.proto RevokeTokenRequest (reason="logout"). CLI's logout does NOT call it, so the contract half is unwired, not forked. Net: local placement correct; the revoke leg is MISSING from the CLI flow.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — clearing the local session so this device stops asserting the user identity is part of "user owns identity+state on owner device." But the endgame's auditable, governable identity also requires the bearer token to actually stop being valid network-wide. Today logout only removes the local file; the JWT remains live server-side until expiry, so a leaked token survives "logout." That undercuts the owns-identity guarantee.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI SHOULD keep clear_session() as the device-runtime teardown (delete auth.json, mode-0600 file), AND before deleting, fire the Axon RevokeToken contract with the cached JWT and reason="logout" (best-effort, tolerate offline). It is the front-door verb (`easynet auth logout`) plus the runtime actor that holds the token and is the only party that can present it for revocation.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD provide (and already declares) the neutral RevokeToken contract: identity.proto RevokeTokenRequest{access_token, reason} / RevokeTokenResponse{revoked} with reason already enumerating "logout". This is the cross-language agreement the CLI imports and calls; Axon must not grow a local "logout file path" notion — that is device runtime, stays in CLI.
- **现状：** partial. Local teardown shipped: EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/auth.rs:254 run_logout → clear_session() at auth.rs:105 (removes auth_session_path()). Contract exists but unwired: EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/identity.proto:199 RevokeTokenRequest (reason "logout" at :202); grep of EasyNet-Cli/src finds no RevokeToken call (only an unrelated CredentialCheck::Revoked test mock at start.rs:992).
- **行动：** Wire run_logout to call Axon RevokeToken(access_token=session.token, reason="logout") best-effort before clear_session(); keep local teardown as-is. Frame F-05 cleanup (close the runtime gap; no protocol change needed since the contract already exists). RFC-003 sec0: no Axon-repo change required — RevokeTokenRequest already landed in identity.proto, so this is CLI-side consumption only, not a contract edit.

### 8. ✅ auth pair (mint pairing token)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (split CLI front-door + EasyNet hub backend). Minting a single-use, hashed, TTL'd, per-user-quota bearer token is hub identity provisioning, not a cross-language wire contract — two different-language nodes do NOT need to agree on the token-mint algorithm to interoperate (only the device-side bearer presentation matters, which is plain HTTP). So it correctly lives in EasyNet (createPairingLogic.go) with a thin CLI front-door verb. The "cli+axon" label is a mislabel: NO Axon contract code participates — identity.proto governs trust-root/key management (the SHAPE of trust), not pairing-token workflow; the Endpoint/NodeID/Realm fields merely echo the hub's Axon coordinates. Sinking the mint logic into Axon would be product/runtime over-sink into the neutral protocol (WRONG); it is correctly out of Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — pairing is the on-ramp by which a user device acquires its identity binding (device_pairings.user_id FK) so it can later run an owner-addressable URA. It directly serves "user owns identity" by minting the one-time credential that bootstraps device→user trust.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI provides the FRONT-DOOR verb only: `easynet auth pair` authenticates with the cached user JWT, POSTs /api/v1/devices/pairing, parses PairingResp, and prints the token (with --quiet for piping into `device join`). This is correct CLI responsibility — a human/agent-facing front door over a thin HTTP call, holding no mint logic. Shipped at auth.rs:391-441.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a cross-language contract. Axon's identity.proto correctly owns trust-root governance and signature verification (the SHAPE of identity), but the pairing-token mint is a hub-local provisioning workflow with a hashed-bearer DB record; it must not be absorbed into the neutral protocol. The only Axon-adjacent contract here is that the eventual paired device's URA/key is governed by Axon identity — which already exists.
- **现状：** shipped, correctly split. CLI front-door: EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/auth.rs:391-441 (PairArgs/PairingResp/run_pair). Hub mint: EasyNet/backend/internal/logic/device/createPairingLogic.go:58-142 (crypto/rand 128-bit token, SHA-256 hash-before-store, 5-min TTL, max-10 pending quota). No Axon participation (verified: no pairing-token contract in core/proto or sdk; identity.proto covers only trust-root/keys).
- **行动：** No code move needed — placement is already correct (CLI front-door + EasyNet hub). Frame F-03 surface: correct the "Where today: cli+axon" accounting entry to "cli+easynet" — the Axon attribution is spurious (only echoed endpoint coordinates, no contract code). One-line audit note: confirm the device-side bearer presentation in join.rs (POST /pairing/{token}/validate) is the only cross-node touchpoint and stays plain-HTTP, not promoted into an Axon RPC. No RFC-003 sec0 Axon-repo work required.

### 9. ✅ auth whoami (layered identity answer)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). Applying the boundary criterion: run_whoami reads two CLI-LOCAL stores — load_session() (interactive auth session) and load_credentials() (device pairing in credentials.json, struct persistence/config.rs:388) — and renders a precedence (session > device > neither). That is "how THIS device reports the identity it currently holds to a human," i.e. FRONT-DOOR + RUNTIME state, not a cross-language contract. No protocol is forked into CLI: every URA string comes from Axon builders via the src/ura.rs re-export of easynet_axon::ura (no raw format! construction). Note Axon already owns a WhoAmIRequest/WhoAmIResponse (identity.proto:211-225, manifest identity.whoami) but that is single-principal TOKEN introspection from the hub's view (principal_id/tenant/trust_level/scopes/expiry) — a different artifact from the local layered device answer; keeping the local-layered rendering in CLI is not a fork of that.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame requires the user to own identity+state on their device; "who am I to EasyNet from this host right now" (which user session, which device URA, which hub) is the front-door question that makes URA addressing legible to the human. It directly serves "user owns identity, ability lives on owner device addressed by URA."
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns the command: read local session + credentials, resolve device/hub/user URAs via Axon builders, render the layered human answer with precedence and next-step hints (auth.rs:292-366). When a session exists and a hub round-trip is wanted to confirm the token is still live, CLI should be the caller of Axon's identity.whoami — but the local two-layer rendering stays CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns (and already ships) the URA grammar/builders (client-sdk/src/domain/ura.rs) that CLI re-exports, and the identity.whoami contract for hub-side token/principal introspection (identity.proto:211-225). It should NOT grow a "layered local device answer" message — that would over-sink CLI runtime state into the neutral protocol.
- **现状：** shipped — EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/auth.rs:292-366 (run_whoami), wired at src/cli/groups/auth.rs:91; URA builders sourced from Axon via src/ura.rs (re-export of easynet_axon::ura, builders in EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/ura.rs:314-336); Session/Credentials are local config (persistence/config.rs:388, load_credentials:584).
- **行动：** No layer move needed — feature is correctly placed in CLI. F-05 cleanup only: (1) the session branch (auth.rs:307-321) uses raw field strings for hub while the device branch (334-350) goes through ura::hub_ura — make the session branch also surface hub via the Axon hub_ura builder so both layers print the same URA form; (2) optional follow-up — when a session is present, call Axon identity.whoami to confirm token liveness and show trust_level/expiry, keeping the local layered rendering in CLI. No Axon-repo change, so no RFC-003 sec0 note required.

### 10. ✅ CLI URA facade (re-export only, no parallel grammar)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — URA is the canonical example of cross-language contract: two different-language nodes MUST agree on the exact `easynet:///r/<realm>/...` grammar to interoperate, so the grammar belongs in Axon. The CLI deliberately holds zero grammar (pure `pub use easynet_axon::ura::*` at ura.rs:37) and enforces the no-fork rule with a guard test, which is exactly the boundary criterion satisfied: contract in Axon, device imports it. No fork, no over-sink, no UI leak.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — URA is the addressing scheme that lets a user own identity+state and have abilities reached by a stable address. A single canonical grammar (no CLI fork) is precisely what makes URA an interoperable addressing layer rather than a per-device dialect; this facade is the mechanism that guarantees the CLI device speaks the same URA as every other node.
- **CLI 预期实现：** A thin re-export facade so existing CLI modules keep using `crate::ura::*` against the Axon source of truth, plus a guard test preventing any `format!`/`strip_prefix`/`starts_with` URA construction outside the facade. This is correctly a CLI runtime/import concern, not a grammar definition. Currently shipped exactly this way.
- **Axon 预期实现：** The full URA grammar: typed builders (`user`/`device`/`agent`/`ability`/`hub`/`resource`/`resource_dot`/...), free-function string builders (`*_ura`), parser (`parse_ura`/`ParsedURA`/`URAKind`), and prefix helpers. All present at EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/ura.rs. Axon remains the neutral cross-language source of truth.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI: EasyNet-Cli/src/ura.rs:37 (`pub use easynet_axon::ura::*`), dep at EasyNet-Cli/Cargo.toml:137; guard at EasyNet-Cli/tests/scripts/test_no_raw_ura_construction.sh; Axon source at EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/ura.rs. Verified: no raw URA construction exists in CLI src (only the two comment lines in ura.rs itself match the prohibited patterns).
- **行动：** No code change needed — facade is correct and enforced (F-05 cleanup, in-spec). Optional hardening: the guard test only scans `src` and only catches the three literal patterns; consider extending its glob to `src/bin` and `tests` fixtures so a fork cannot creep in via the daemon/keyring binaries. Not an Axon-repo change, so no RFC-003 sec0 note required.

### 11. ✅ Device public-key registration (per-device Ed25519)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (distributed, each layer owns the right shape). The thing two languages MUST agree on — the signature-verification contract — already lives in Axon: KeyResolver.Resolve(agentURA)->ed25519.PublicKey plus the RFC-001 §5.2 admission pipeline (VerifySignature + canonical reason strings AXON_CALLER_SIGNATURE_INVALID etc.) with Go/Python/Rust parity. The device-local mechanism (the register-pubkey write ability + realm-trust store) is CLI runtime, correctly in CLI. The user-facing HTTP registration surface is EasyNet, correctly there. The canonical ability name is identity.register_pubkey across CLI and backend; no alias is staged.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — per-device Ed25519 keys are the foundation of "user owns identity": every invoke is a device-signed envelope verified against a registered key, which is exactly what makes a URA decidable and the audit trail (easynet-verify) trustworthy. Without registered device keys, admission falls back to self-asserted identity and the ownership/audit endgame collapses.
- **CLI 预期实现：** RUNTIME/EXECUTOR: own the device-local trust-anchor store (realm-trust.toml via realm_trust_anchor.rs, DEC-EU multi-pubkey-per-URI) and the register_device_pubkey / list_user_pubkeys / revoke_user_pubkey ability handlers in src/services/invocation_transport/. CLI verifies the submitted VerifyingKey, atomically appends to the store, republishes the shared trust-anchor cell, and INSTALLS the resulting mapping into Axon's KeyResolver so §5.2 admission can verify. The write-ability is the device's "how I run/admit" — keep it CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** CONTRACT: the neutral cross-language signature-verification shape only — KeyResolver trait (Resolve(agentURA)->ed25519 pubkey), the §5.2 admission pipeline (ValidateSignatureStructure / VerifySignature / nonce-replay), and the load-bearing rejection-reason strings, all with Go/Python/Rust parity. Axon does NOT store keys, does NOT author the trust set, and does NOT run a daemon.
- **现状：** shipped (correctly split). Axon: sdk/go/easynet/invocation/admission.go:132 VerifySignature + axiom.go:300 KeyResolver/FileKeyResolver, with python/rust parity. CLI: src/services/invocation_transport/register_device_pubkey.rs (handler), src/services/realm_trust_anchor.rs (store, DEC-EU). EasyNet: backend/api/easynet.api:101-103,348-363 (DeviceResp pubkey fields), internal/logic/device/register_device_pubkey.go + key_status.go (fan-out helper + UI fingerprint), handler/user/registerUserSigningKeyHandler.go + handler/device/verifyCredentialHandler.go:129. PARTIAL on the name: ability literal still forked CLI<->backend, rename staged not landed (docs/open-questions/deprecate-self-alias-in-ability-names.md Stage 2).
- **行动：** No structural move — the feature is in the right layers. Keep the mechanism in CLI, keep the HTTP registration surface in EasyNet, and keep Axon limited to neutral admission/signature contracts.

### 12. ✅ Python URA-as-resource_ura context (Client.tenant.ability chain)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — this is the SHAPE of the addressable contract and it must AGREE cross-language to interoperate. Confirmed symmetric in two Axon SDKs: Python client.py:56-72 (`_subject_visibility_from_resource_ura`/`_principal_to_subject_id`) and Rust sdk/rust/src/tool_adapter.rs:132-170 (`parse_resource_visibility`/`principal_to_subject_id`) implement the identical pub|prv|org → `easynet:{vis}:reg:agent.{id}` rule. A Python node and a Rust node derive the same subject from the same URA — that is contract, correctly in Axon. No fork found in CLI: CLI only uses `easynet:prv:reg:agent.<node>` as an HKDF key-derivation namespace (publish.rs:503, join.rs:719, boot.rs:1118) and publish.rs:482 explicitly notes it "Mirrors AxonClient::default_auth_for_subject in the SDK" — it defers the shape to Axon rather than re-deriving visibility-from-URA.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — URA is the addressable spine of the endgame: an ability lives on the owner device and is reached by a `resource_ura`. Threading that opaque URA through the chained builder, and deriving subject visibility (pub/prv/org) + subject-id from it, is exactly how a foreign-language caller addresses an owner's governed ability without inventing its own identity scheme.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a for the contract itself. CLI's correct role is downstream: the runtime imports this Axon contract and uses the derived subject_id as the HKDF namespace to bootstrap/verify the daemon's owned-ability signing key (publish.rs:474-505). CLI should NOT grow its own URA-to-subject mapping; keep mirroring Axon.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns this and already provides it in both SDKs as a chained `Client.tenant(...).ability(resource_ura).principal(...).call(...)` builder plus the visibility/subject derivation. Axon should keep this rule single-sourced (ideally a documented canonical spec the two SDK copies are tested against) so Python and Rust cannot drift on the pub|prv|org default-to-org fallback.
- **现状：** shipped — Python: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/python/easynet_axon/client.py:48-72,189-323 (chained builder + derivation, evidence at :56-75,272 confirmed). Rust counterpart shipped: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/tool_adapter.rs:132-170 and ura.rs:349 (`resource_ura` constructor). CLI consumer shipped and correctly deferring: src/runtime/publish.rs:474-505.
- **行动：** No relayer; placement is correct. Frame F-07 (ontology-absorption / hardening): single-source the pub|prv|org→subject-id rule so the Python (client.py:56-72) and Rust (tool_adapter.rs:132-170) copies are verified against one cross-language conformance fixture, preventing silent drift on the default-to-org fallback and the `easynet:`-already-qualified short-circuit. This is Axon-repo work — per RFC-003 sec0, route through the Axon SDK conformance/test surface, not a CLI change.

### 13. ✅ Self identity keyring + canonical_self_uris (device key custody)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI RUNTIME). Boundary criterion applied strictly: (a) the shapes that two different-language nodes MUST agree on — the URA grammar and the Ed25519 signature/canonical-bytes shape — are NOT forked here. src/ura.rs:37 is a pure `pub use easynet_axon::ura::*` re-export, and signing uses Axon's seed primitive (easynet-axon dep, Cargo.toml:137). (b) What lives in this file is device-local key-at-rest custody: KeyringClient (unix-socket RPC to the keyring daemon), InMemoryVault, seed storage, fresh_seed_hex minting, and canonical_self_uris composing Axon's device_ura+hub_ura into a primary+overlay for THIS host. That is "how this device runs / stays online holding the identity key" = RUNTIME, correctly CLI. Pushing seed custody into Axon would be over-sink (Axon is imported, never runs on a user device, must not hold private keys); it correctly does not.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the literal substrate of "user owns identity+state+audit": the device holds its own Ed25519 seed and signs as itself, so a URA becomes a reachable, self-sovereign principal whose receipts easynet-verify can check. Without local key custody there is no owner-owned identity to address or audit against.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Exactly what is shipped: the RUNTIME that holds the device seed and makes the URA reachable — KeyringClient daemon transport, InMemoryVault/Vault custody, SelfIdentity::{sign,public_key} trait (self_identity.rs:74-84), fresh_seed_hex minting, and canonical_self_uris(realm,node_id) composing the device-primary + hub-overlay placement policy for this host. The primary/overlay composition is a CLI runtime placement decision (device also signs as hub on this backend host), not protocol.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Only the neutral contract pieces, all already present and consumed: the URA grammar builders (sdk/rust/src/ura.rs: device(), hub(), agent(), ability()) that CLI re-exports; the signature primitive over a seed (sdk/rust/src/invocation/axiom.rs:417 signing_key_from_bytes); and the governable key-lifecycle proto (core/proto/axon/v1/identity.proto: RotateNodeKey / RevokeNodeKey / GetTrustRoot / VerifySignature). Axon must NOT hold or store the seed — custody is device-local.
- **现状：** shipped and correctly layered. CLI: src/services/self_identity.rs:106-442 (KeyringClient 106, SelfIdentity trait 74, canonical_self_uris 417, fresh_seed_hex 428); URA re-export src/ura.rs:37; consumed by the join flow src/cli/join.rs:370-389; Axon dep Cargo.toml:137. Axon contract shipped: sdk/rust/src/ura.rs:204/225, axiom.rs:417, identity.proto.
- **行动：** No layer move required — leave key custody in CLI. F-05 cleanup (light): canonical_self_uris hardcodes a single hub-role overlay; this primary+overlay placement policy is undocumented as a CLI runtime decision. Add a one-line note in self_identity.rs:412-423 that the overlay set is CLI placement policy (not protocol) and that any URA role must come from easynet_axon::ura builders, keeping the test_no_raw_ura_construction.sh invariant visible. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 (no-modify-axon) does not bind here.

### 14. ✅ URA grammar + builders + parser (canonical contract)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (placement) but the contract is FORKED into a third language. By the boundary criterion, URA grammar is the canonical thing two different-language nodes must AGREE on to interoperate, so it belongs in Axon — and it does: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/ura.rs is the source-of-truth, and CLI/src/ura.rs is a 58-line pure re-export façade (`pub use easynet_axon::ura::*;`, dep `easynet-axon = { path = "../EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust" }`), NOT a fork. Rust↔CLI is clean. The defect is on the TypeScript edge: EasyNet/Frontend/src/lib/easynet-ura.ts hand-reimplements the grammar (its own parseURA, URAKind, ResourceNamespace, and literal builders emitting `easynet:///r/...`). Its own comment (lines 322-327) admits this is a stopgap until an axon-ts package ships. That is a protocol fork in a third language — the WRONG state — even though the Axon home is right.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — URA IS the addressable primitive of the endgame: "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, returns a Surface, governed by Axon." Every node (owner device runtime, hub router, browser surface, easynet-verify) must agree on how a user/device/agent/ability/resource is named before identity, audit, or routing can mean anything. Without one canonical scheme there is no shared address space, so this is foundational, not peripheral.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a as owner of the contract — CLI must NOT define URA grammar. CLI's only correct role here (already met): re-export the Axon Rust builders/parser via the src/ura.rs façade so runtime/executor/front-door code addresses URAs through the canonical SDK rather than string-concatenating them. Keep it a façade; do not let CLI grow its own grammar.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the canonical grammar, typed builders (user/device/agent/ability/hub/resource_ura), URAKind/ResourceNamespace enums, and parse_ura/kind_from_ura/realm_from_ura as a NEUTRAL cross-language contract — present and correct in the Rust SDK. The missing obligation: Axon must ship the SAME contract for the second interop language (TypeScript), i.e. an axon-ts URA module mirroring ura.rs, so the browser node agrees by import rather than by hand-rolled copy.
- **现状：** partial. Rust contract shipped: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/ura.rs (enums 63-114, builders 325-388, parse_ura:389, kind_from_ura:496, realm_from_ura:500). CLI façade shipped: EasyNet-Cli/src/ura.rs:37 (`pub use easynet_axon::ura::*;`). TypeScript contract forked/missing: EasyNet/Frontend/src/lib/easynet-ura.ts reimplements parseURA (line 45) and builds literals by hand (resourceUraForUserFile:340, stubBrowserSessionUra:359), with comment 322-327 flagging it as a temporary fork pending an axon-ts builder.
- **行动：** Ship an axon-ts URA module in the Axon repo (port ura.rs grammar/enums/builders/parser to TypeScript, fixture-parity tested against the Rust unit tests at ura.rs:740-884), publish it, then migrate EasyNet/Frontend/src/lib/easynet-ura.ts to import it and delete the hand-rolled parser + literal builders. Frame: F-07 ontology-absorption (Axon absorbs the URA contract into the second interop language so the browser node stops forking it). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work — new TypeScript SDK target, additive only; must not modify existing axon/v1 proto or the Rust ura.rs shape (sec0 forbids breaking the ratified Rust contract), and CLI's axon-pb path resolution is untouched.

## 发现 / Discovery（15）

### 15. 🔴 meta.list_abilities / meta.describe

- **裁决：** `FORKED — split verdict. The VERB is CORRECT in CLI: device.meta.{list_abilities,describe} is a device-side handler reading the live runtime registry (meta_ability.rs:169-419) — pure "how this device exposes its own catalog over the Invoke front door", which is CLI RUNTIME/FRONT-DOOR by the boundary criterion. But the RESULT SHAPE it emits — AbilityDescriptor — is an Axon contract that has forked: src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs:1-46 declares itself "the Rust home for the AXON-RFC-001 §1.6 schema" (name, owner_agent_ura, visibility, scope_subjects[], schema_summary{input,output_receipt_body}, hints{...}), while EasyNet-Axon/sdk/go/easynet/ability_lifecycle.go:76-205 independently defines a DIFFERENT AbilityDescriptor (ToolName, CommandTemplate, ReadOnly/Destructive/Idempotent/OpenWorld bools, no visibility/scope, no owner_agent_ura). Two languages that must agree to interoperate carry two incompatible descriptor structs = protocol fork. The neutral §1.6 descriptor + visibility-filter rule must live in Axon and be imported, not re-typed per language.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — discovery is load-bearing for the endgame: a URA-addressed device must answer "what abilities do you own/host" so a peer or agent can decide-then-invoke. meta.describe (cheap identity+summary cache-check) and meta.list_abilities (full AbilityDescriptor catalog) are exactly the "addressed by URA, returns the catalog the owner device hosts" half of owner-device + URA + governed-by-Axon. Verified at meta_ability.rs:51-419.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep BOTH verbs in CLI as the device-side Invoke handlers — they are runtime/front-door and belong here. CLI should construct the descriptor catalog by IMPORTING the Axon §1.6 AbilityDescriptor type rather than maintaining src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs as a hand-rolled Rust copy. CLI retains: live-registry merge (registered-after-Arc::new fix, meta_ability.rs:236-388), the static+synth merge, scope=local|realm hub-cache merge, the canonical-name filtering, and the synth-drop-on-missing-owner honesty. Visibility row-filtering stays at the admission/dispatch gate per the file's own §1.6 note (lines 7-11).
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon must own the neutral AbilityDescriptor §1.6 contract (name, owner_agent_ura, visibility tri-state, scope_subjects[]/scope_agents[], source, schema_summary{input,output_receipt_body}, hints{read_only,destructive,idempotent,streaming_only,bidi_only}, class) and the is_visible_to(...) filter invariant, as one cross-language definition imported by Rust CLI and Go/Python SDKs. The Go SDK's divergent AbilityDescriptor (ability_lifecycle.go:76) must be reconciled to that single shape. Axon does NOT own the meta verbs themselves (those are device runtime) nor the live-registry enumeration (device-local). Note: AbilityDescriptor is the SHAPE returned BY discovery, not a discovery RPC — INV-3 holds (ability is a method, no global registry/provider-matching), so Axon defines the descriptor type, not a catalog service.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI verbs): both abilities registered + dispatchable, full live-registry merge, realm scope, dynamic-manifest synth, multi-agent same-name de-collapse — all covered by tests at meta_ability.rs:548-1137. forked (descriptor shape): CLI Rust AbilityDescriptor at src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs:1-46 vs Axon Go AbilityDescriptor at EasyNet-Axon/sdk/go/easynet/ability_lifecycle.go:76-205, incompatible field sets, no shared Axon source of truth (no AbilityDescriptor message in core/proto/axon/v1/*.proto — capability.proto defines CapabilityDescriptor for package lifecycle, a different concept).
- **行动：** Leave the two meta verbs in CLI untouched (F-05 cleanup: they are correctly placed runtime handlers). Open an Axon-repo task (F-07 ontology-absorption): promote the §1.6 AbilityDescriptor + is_visible_to invariant into Axon as the single neutral contract — per F-01 sec7, CLI ran ahead with the Rust §1.6 shape and Axon now absorbs it back as the canonical type, then reconcile the Go SDK struct to it and have CLI import rather than re-type. ### 17. 🔴 federation heartbeat loop (keep device reachable)

- **裁决：** `FORKED — the CONTRACT piece (the `federation.heartbeat` ability name + empty-body wire shape + SDK `federation_heartbeat()` helper) correctly sits in Axon (EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/dendrite_bridge.rs:606, sdk/python/.../dendrite_bridge.py:628, ABILITY_FEDERATION_HEARTBEAT const). But the ORCHESTRATION loop (cadence/jitter, failure budget, rejection parsing, deregister-on-exhaustion, daemon lifecycle) is a device-runtime concern that exists TWICE and incompatibly: CLI heartbeat.rs is the real one, and Axon NodeAgent (sdk/rust/src/node_agent.rs, sdk/python/easynet_axon/node_agent.py) is a second over-sunk copy with a different 10s cadence, no jitter, and crucially NO `permanent`/`rejected_nodes` rejection parsing (CLI's own test at heartbeat.rs:646 calls this out). - **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — a long-lived device only stays addressable-by-URA / reachable if it keeps advertising liveness; without the heartbeat the runtime sweeper self-revokes the device and its abilities drop off the directory, so the endgame (ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA) collapses.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns the whole heartbeat STATE MACHINE as RUNTIME: cadence + per-device jitter, failure budget (MAX 10), `permanent`/`rejected_nodes` rejection handling, FailuresExhausted -> federation.revoke + credential cleanup, and the long-lived daemon lifecycle (fork, ReconnectingBridge, re-register hook, self-identity bootstrap). It should call the Axon SDK `federation_heartbeat()` helper for the wire, nothing more. This is already correctly shipped in heartbeat.rs.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns ONLY the contract shape: the `federation.heartbeat` ability name, its empty-body request / typed-success-or-rejection response schema (so a Python node and a Rust node agree on what a heartbeat and a `permanent`/`rejected_nodes` rejection look like), and a thin `federation_heartbeat()` bridge helper. It should NOT ship a NodeAgent orchestration loop — cadence/jitter/backoff/failure-budget/daemon-lifecycle are device-runtime, not contract; that is over-sink into the neutral library.

Correct runtime loop shipped in CLI (EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/heartbeat.rs:25-409, with full test suite). Divergent second loop shipped in Axon (EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/node_agent.rs:147-218, sdk/python/easynet_axon/node_agent.py:81-239) lacking rejection parsing and using a different cadence.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup: deprecate/retire the Axon NodeAgent orchestration loop (Rust + Python) so the heartbeat STATE MACHINE lives in exactly one place — CLI. Keep in Axon only the `federation.heartbeat` ability name, its request/response (incl. `permanent`/`rejected_nodes`) schema, and the `federation_heartbeat()` bridge helper. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work removing a runtime concern from the library — file under RFC-003 sec0 as a contract-vs-runtime de-sink; if any other consumer depends on NodeAgent, gate removal behind a deprecation cycle and document the rejection-fields schema first so no node forks the parsing again.

- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption — promote the federation.discover request/response and the DirectoryEntry message (with origin_realm provenance + §2.4 rewrite-chokepoint invariant) into Axon's federation.proto / client-sdk so it becomes the neutral cross-language directory contract; then have CLI import those generated types instead of its private federation_directory.rs serde structs, leaving CLI only the daemon-side projection executor + the `easynet federation discover` front door. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo protocol work — gate it behind an RFC-003 sec0 contract entry (new proto message + ability registration) reviewed for wire/back-compat against the existing FederatedNodeEntry family before CLI re-points its imports.

### 21. 🔴 federation.resolve_key

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — two different-language-capable hubs MUST agree on this wire ability to interoperate (realm A dials realm B's federation.resolve_key), so by the boundary criterion the SHAPE is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon. The neutral contract already exists there (KeyResolver::resolve(caller_ura)->VerifyingKey, RFC-001 §5.4, client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:91). But the over-the-wire ability is implemented twice with INCOMPATIBLE shapes: Axon hub_profile.rs returns {public_key_hex} (hex, RFC-002 §5.1); CLI federation_wrappers.rs returns {public_key_b64} (base64, RFC-001 §5.2) and takes {agent_ura, presented_pubkey_b64}. Same ability name, divergent field names AND encoding = protocol fork.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — cross-realm verifying-key lookup is what lets a forwarded invoke from realm B be admitted on realm A under the same RFC-001 §5.2 4-step verify a local caller gets; without it, federation cannot govern/audit cross-realm identity, which is core to "owner identity addressed by URA, governed by Axon, auditable by easynet-verify."
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI should be the EXECUTOR/RUNTIME landing only: the daemon serves federation.resolve_key over Invoke against its local realm trust anchor, and the FederatedKeyResolver dials a peer hub's resolve_key on a forwarded envelope. The request/response shape it serializes must be the Axon-defined canonical shape, not a CLI-local struct. CLI keeps the trust-anchor lookup + multi-device presented_pubkey disambiguation (device-runtime concern), but emits the contract bytes.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should OWN the wire contract: one canonical ResolveKey request/response message (single field name, single encoding — pick hex or base64 once) in core/proto/axon/v1/federation.proto, generated into every SDK, sitting alongside the existing KeyResolver trait. resolve_key is the transport realization of KeyResolver::resolve, so its envelope must be neutral and cross-language-stable; the multi-device presented_pubkey semantics (DEC-EU) belong in that message too.
- **现状：** forked — CLI handler at EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs:702 (ResolveKeyResponse{public_key_b64}, line 689; const at :119); Axon handler at EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/hub_profile.rs:1192 (hub_resolve_key) returning ResolveKeyReceiptBody{public_key_hex} (:1542), routed at :476, exercised by core/runtime-rs/src/tests/forward_invoke_e2e.rs:473. No resolve_key message exists in federation.proto (grep returned none), so neither side is generated from a shared schema — the fork is unanchored.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption — lift the wire shape into Axon: add a canonical ResolveKey{Request,Response} message to core/proto/axon/v1/federation.proto with ONE encoding (reconcile hex vs base64) and the DEC-EU presented_pubkey field, regenerate SDKs, then make BOTH the CLI daemon wrapper and Axon hub_profile.rs serialize that generated type so the fork collapses to one contract. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo protocol work (new proto message + SDK regen), so it must land under an RFC-003 sec0 spec entry / DEC pinning the resolve_key wire shape and encoding choice before either runtime is repointed; the encoding reconciliation (hex vs base64) is a wire-compat decision that needs CTO/architect ratification, not a silent edit.

### 22. 🔴 federation.subscribe_directory / _v2 (live presence stream)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). Per the boundary criterion a CLI-local copy of an interop wire shape is a protocol fork. Today the SAME ability name `federation.subscribe_directory` carries TWO incompatible serde-tagged enums: Axon directory_events.rs emits {snapshot[DirectoryAgentSummary], agent_advertised, abilities_advertised, agent_revoked, heartbeat}; CLI federation_directory.rs emits {snapshot[DirectoryEntry], upsert, remove, heartbeat}. Note: per F-01 sec7 (CLI runs ahead, Axon absorbs ontology back) the CLI v2 shape is the more correct/richer one (node_id, origin_realm, presence-derived upsert/remove); the fork should be resolved by Axon absorbing the v2 DirectoryEvent vocabulary as the neutral contract, not by reverting CLI.`

CLI should import the DirectoryEvent/DirectoryEntry wire TYPE from Axon rather than declaring its own serde enum; the projection logic and FSM stay CLI. n/a for the wire shape definition itself.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should own the single neutral DirectoryEvent / DirectoryEntry contract (the addressable+governable shape every language decodes against): the variant set (snapshot/upsert/remove/heartbeat), the entry fields (agent_ura, node_id, status, origin_realm, hub_endpoint, last_seen_unix_ms), and the snapshot-first + monotonic-ordering + heartbeat-keepalive rules. It must absorb the CLI v2 vocabulary as canonical and retire the divergent v1 directory_events.rs enum (or version it explicitly), so Rust CLI, Rust peer hub, and Go backend all agree without a per-consumer translator. Axon does NOT run the PresenceRegistry or the FSM — those stay CLI.
- **现状：** forked. CLI v2 shape shipped: EasyNet-Cli/src/services/federation_directory.rs:128-208 (DirectoryEntry, DirectoryEvent upsert/remove, SubscriberFsm), projection at :128/:156, served via EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs:98,141,1177-1187. Axon v1 shape also shipped and incompatible: EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/directory_events.rs:59-162 (agent_advertised/abilities_advertised/agent_revoked) plus pump at :199 and broadcast slot at state/mod.rs:308. EasyNet Go backend carries the dual decoder at backend/internal/events/directory_events.go:42-93.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: lift the DirectoryEvent + DirectoryEntry wire shape into Axon as the canonical neutral contract, adopting the CLI v2 vocabulary (snapshot/upsert/remove/heartbeat with node_id/origin_realm/hub_endpoint), and retire/version the divergent v1 enum in EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/directory_events.rs. Then have CLI federation_directory.rs import that type instead of redeclaring it, and drop the v1 branch of the Go translator. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work touching the federation directory contract — file under the RFC-003 sec0 contract-change log and sequence against any in-flight directory/presence PRs (this is a wire-compatible-migration step, gate behind the existing v1->v2 dual-serve window already noted at federation_wrappers.rs:136-140 so subscribers ramp independently of the absorption).

### 23. 🟡 Hub agent.list / device.* built-in ability cluster

The handler bodies and hub routing themselves are CORRECT where they sit.`
- **当前所在：** easynet
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — device.* abilities live on the owner device (CLI built-ins), are addressed by device URA, returned via advertise/resolve, and the cluster replaced generic shell-out (POST /:id/exec retired) with per-purpose typed schemas; this is exactly the endgame where ability lives on owner device, is URA-addressed, and is governable (each op has its own visibility, e.g. agent.list is SCOPED).
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep all device.* handler bodies as CLI built-in abilities (RUNTIME/EXECUTOR): meta.describe/list_abilities (meta_ability.rs), agent.start/stop/refresh/list (agent_lifecycle_ability.rs, advertise.rs), meta.list_resources (list_resources_ability.rs). CLI continues to advertise them via federation.advertise_abilities and dispatch them. CLI should IMPORT the device.* namespace constants + descriptor metadata-key vocabulary from Axon rather than minting string literals locally.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Pin two contract slices the cluster silently depends on: (1) the reserved built-in device.* namespace (the prefix grammar + which names are kernel built-ins vs agent-owned) and (2) the AbilityDescriptor metadata key vocabulary (host_node_id, tool_name, version, state, install_id) as a typed/enumerated contract in the AbilityDescriptor proto/SDK — not free-form opaque map. Axon does NOT host or run these abilities; it only fixes the shape the Rust publisher and Go reader must agree on. The handler logic and the GET-replacement hub route stay out of Axon (that would be runtime/product over-sink).
- **现状：** shipped (functionally) but the cross-language contract is forked-by-convention. Handlers shipped in CLI: src/runtime/agents/meta_ability.rs:51-52, agent_lifecycle_ability.rs:64, list_resources_ability.rs:53, advertise.rs:785. Hub orchestration shipped: EasyNet/backend/internal/logic/device/listAbilitiesLogic.go (federation.resolve projection), api easynet.api:630-639 (replaced GET /:id/abilities), ability_descriptor_reader.go:138,199 (HostNodeID from metadata). Contract MISSING in Axon: no device.* namespace or descriptor-metadata-key definition in core/proto/axon/v1/*.proto (mission.proto carries only opaque AbilityDescriptor; no device.* reservation).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: lift the device.* reserved-namespace grammar and AbilityDescriptor metadata-key vocabulary (host_node_id/tool_name/version/state/install_id) from the CLI publisher + Go reader back INTO Axon as a typed AbilityDescriptor metadata contract (mirrors F-01 sec7: Cli ran ahead, Axon absorbs the ontology back). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work — open it as an Axon RFC/proto change against core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto (AbilityDescriptor metadata typing) gated through RFC-003 sec0 spec discipline before touching proto; then have CLI ability_descriptor.rs and EasyNet ability_descriptor_reader.go import the shared constants instead of duplicating literals. Leave the handler bodies (CLI) and the listAbilitiesLogic projection (EasyNet) exactly where they are.

### 24. ⬜ Unified discover contract

- **裁决：** `MISSING (and partially FORKED). By the boundary criterion the cross-node discovery shape is CONTRACT: a Rust daemon, the Go hub backend, and a peer hub in another realm must AGREE on the discover request/response (filter axes, the resolved-entry/DirectoryEntry shape, online-derivation) to interoperate — so it belongs in Axon as ONE primitive. Today it is missing as a unified Axon ability and the consolidation logic + verb names live as CLI-local consts (federation_wrappers.rs:84-181) = a protocol fork. NOT a leak in the CLI-local tier: the `<agent>.discover` scope ladder (discover_ability.rs) is correctly CLI runtime — it is how THIS device projects its own registry view; only its cross-node fan-out should bottom out on the one Axon discover shape rather than 7 ad-hoc federation verbs.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES. Discovery is the act of turning a query into an addressable URA before invoke — it is the front half of "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA." Today the same logical question ("what abilities are reachable, and where") is answered by 7+ overlapping verbs (federation.resolve / federation.discover / subscribe_directory(_v2) / proxy_resolve / resolve_key / meta.list_abilities), so a peer in another language or realm cannot agree on one discovery shape. A single neutral discovery primitive is exactly what lets URA addressing be cross-language and governable, so it serves the endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the owner-aware `<agent>.discover` scope=self/device/easynet ladder (src/runtime/agents/discover_ability.rs) as the runtime/front-door projection. For scope=easynet, fan out to the hub by calling the ONE Axon-defined discover ability instead of the current grab-bag (federation.resolve + federation.discover + proxy_resolve + subscribe_directory_v2). Retire meta.list_abilities / easynet.discover compat aliases (discover_ability.rs:33-38) once the Axon shape lands. No new product surface in CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Define ONE neutral discovery ability contract (request {filter: agent_ura | name_prefix | ability | tag | realm-scope; visibility} → response {entries[]: resolved URA + ability summaries + online/origin_realm}) that supersedes the four legacy RPCs already alias-collapsed onto federation.resolve plus federation.discover/list_user_devices, and a stream variant subsuming subscribe_directory_v2's DirectoryEvent frames. Consolidate today's split shapes (federation.proto:287 ListFederatedNodes/FederatedNodeEntry/FederatedAbilitySummary and capability.proto/meta.list_abilities) under one addressable primitive so every SDK generator emits the same discover call.
- **现状：** forked/missing. CLI-local: 7+ discovery verbs as consts in EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs:84-181 (federation.resolve, federation.discover, subscribe_directory, subscribe_directory_v2, proxy_resolve, resolve_key, list_user_devices) plus meta.list_abilities (14 refs) and the correct local ladder in src/runtime/agents/discover_ability.rs. Axon-side: shapes exist but fragmented across EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/federation.proto:287-343 and capability.proto, with RFC-001 restatement-mapping.md:128-130,207,234,236 showing 4 legacy RPCs collapsed onto federation.resolve by alias only — no single Axon-owned `discover` primitive.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: write an AXON RFC (RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo protocol work — land the proto/SDK shape in EasyNet-Axon first, behind the additive-evolution + SDK-regen rule, before CLI cutover) defining one neutral discover ability + stream variant that subsumes federation.resolve/discover/subscribe_directory_v2/proxy_resolve and meta.list_abilities. Then F-05 cleanup in CLI: point `<agent>.discover` scope=easynet fan-out at the single Axon shape and delete the redundant federation.* discovery consts + meta.list_abilities/easynet.discover compat aliases. CLI runs ahead today (the working ladder exists); Axon absorbs the consolidated discovery shape back, mirroring the F-01 sec7 AgentId pattern.

### 25. ✅ <agent>.discover ability (per-agent capability discovery)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). This is the device's front-door discovery verb: how this node exposes its own callable surface to an LLM/agent/MCP client. The candidate envelope `{candidates, scope, query}` is a CLI-local presentation shape consumed on-device, not a cross-language wire contract; the one cross-language agreement point it needs (the federation.resolve receipt → ResolvedAgent) ALREADY lives in Axon and is consumed, not forked. No protocol fork, no over-sink, no product-UI leak.`

The verb returns owner-qualified candidates that map straight to the invoke path, and the federation tiers project hub facts so cross-device/owner discovery works. Directly serves the endgame loop (ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, then invoked + audited).
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this: the per-agent discover verb walking self/device/user/public, BM25-lite local scoring, scope/query/top_k/provider arg parsing, projection of federation.resolve receipts + federated-directory entries into the local Candidate envelope, and the typed-envelope fallbacks (federation_not_joined / federation_unavailable). Evidence: src/runtime/agents/discover_ability.rs:88-211 (register/dispatch), :272-443 (federation projection), :677-715 (federated directory); wired at src/runtime/agents/mod.rs:705.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the per-agent self-surface verb itself — that is device-local front-door, not contract. Axon's only obligation here is the cross-language fact the federation tiers stand on: the federation.resolve RPC + ResolvedAgent/ResolveReceipt shape that two different-language nodes must agree on to share a directory view. That contract already exists (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/control.proto:243 ResolveNode, capability.proto:370 ResolveCapability; consumed by CLI advertise.rs:387 resolve_agents_with_filter). No new Axon surface required for this feature.

Federation contract consumed from Axon control.proto/capability.proto. Test coverage extensive (discover_ability.rs:866-1486).
- **行动：** No layer move. Keep CLI. Correct the accounting/SKILL text to say only `meta.list_abilities` remains as the flat compat surface; `<agent>.discover` is canonical. No Axon-repo work, so no RFC-003 sec0 note required. If a future planner surface (Axon planner-interface-v1.md:13 discover_abilities(filter)) is ever implemented, that is a separate Axon-contract item — out of scope for this device-local verb.

### 29. ✅ PresenceRegistry

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI runtime). The registry's state is `DashMap<String, mpsc::Sender<Result<DispatchFrame, tonic::Status>>>` (presence_registry.rs:124,242) — live Tokio channels and tonic stream handles that are inherently process-local and cannot cross a wire or a language. Two nodes do NOT agree on DispatchSender/PresenceEvent; they agree on the wire liveness shape, which already lives in Axon (`bool online`/`last_seen_unix_ms` in types.proto:703-707, and `DirectoryEvent` Snapshot/Advertised/Revoked/Heartbeat in Axon directory_events.rs). CLI only projects its in-process snapshot into those agreed shapes (federation_wrappers.rs build_subscribe_directory_initial / resolve / list_user_devices). So this is the "how this device makes a URA reachable" RUNTIME role, not a forked protocol; sinking the mpsc/dashmap bookkeeping into Axon would be over-sink that pollutes the neutral library with a device's runtime channels.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — it is the runtime primitive that makes a URA "actually reachable/online." The endgame needs an ability addressed by URA to be invocable only when its owner device is live; PresenceRegistry is exactly the signal that collapses liveness onto the transport (online == `session.open` stream open, presence_registry.rs:50), which the resolve/subscribe_directory front doors and federation forwarding all consume to route a call to a live owner device.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Own the in-process URA->DispatchSender registry, lifecycle (insert/insert_tracked/remove/remove_if_session/force_revoke), the displacement/reconnect race guard via session_id, the bounded-backpressure SendFailed collapse, and the local PresenceEvent broadcast that subscribe_directory pumps and the audit log drain. Then PROJECT this state into the Axon-defined wire shapes at the federation.* front door. This is exactly what is shipped.
- **Axon 预期实现：** The cross-language liveness CONTRACT only: the on-wire/decodable shape of "online/offline" that a different-language subscriber must agree on — already present as NodeDescriptor.online/last_seen_unix_ms (types.proto:703-707) and the DirectoryEvent enum (Axon directory_events.rs). Axon must NOT hold the DispatchSender/mpsc/dashmap — those are device runtime state, not contract.
- **现状：** shipped. CLI: src/services/presence_registry.rs (full registry + tests). Consumed at src/services/invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs (snapshot/subscribe_events projections, e.g. build_subscribe_directory_initial:1159) and referenced by daemon_invocation_service / federation forward paths. Wire contract counterparts shipped in Axon: core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:703-707 and core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/directory_events.rs.
- **行动：** No move; leave PresenceRegistry in CLI as the runtime liveness primitive. Frame F-05 cleanup: audit that the CLI projection layer (federation_wrappers.rs) emits ONLY Axon-defined DirectoryEvent/online shapes and that the legacy PresenceEventDelta + subscribe_directory_v2 dual-serve (federation_wrappers.rs:138-141) does not let a CLI-local presence shape leak onto the wire as a de-facto second contract — if PresenceEventDelta is a wire type that peers decode, its schema must be ratified in Axon (RFC-003 sec0 note: declare the wire delta shape in Axon proto/SDK before retiring v1). The DashMap/mpsc internals stay CLI-private.
