### 30. 🟡 --node remote-pin invoke as full first-class addressed call

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-CLI — the MISSING piece is a runtime/front-door gap, not a protocol gap. The cross-language contract already exists in Axon: invoke.proto:105 InvokeRequest{Envelope(caller/callee), InvocationTarget, function_name} + types.proto:314 AgentIdentity (agent URA /r/<realm>/agent/<owner>.<id>) already let two different-language nodes agree on "address this agent callee, run this function, return a receipt". So this must NOT become a new Axon shape (that would be over-sink). It is purely: how THIS device lets a human/agent address an agent-URA callee and how it renders the result — CLI RUNTIME + FRONT-DOOR. Note: "Surface" is an EasyNet/CLI product concept, ZERO hits in Axon core/proto — do not push a Surface return type into the Axon contract (would be product leaking into protocol).`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this IS the endgame's central act: address any owner's ability by its agent URA from any node, get a Surface back, governed by Axon and auditable by easynet-verify. Without agent-URA-targeted first-class invoke, "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, returns a Surface" is only reachable for device/hub targets behind a feature gate, not for the user-owned hosted agents that are the product's point.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI should (1) accept an agent URA (/r/<realm>/agent/<owner>.<id>) as the --node/--target value, not only device/hub — today federation_invoke.rs:70-101 parse_node_ura rejects agent kind (tests :589-593, :648-653 lock the rejection); (2) drop the axon-pb feature gate so agent-URA realm invoke is first-class in every build (invoke.rs:118-131, federation_invoke.rs:43-47 gate the whole module); (3) thread the result through the Surface render path the CLI/EasyNet front door already owns, not a new protocol field; (4) fix the stale header comment invoke.rs:16-30 which claims --node is unsupported while :105-205 already wires the device/hub path.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — no new contract shape needed. The neutral cross-language surface (Envelope caller/callee + InvocationTarget + AgentIdentity agent URA + InvocationReceipt) already exists in invoke.proto/types.proto. Axon must NOT grow a Surface type or an agent-target-specific RPC; that would over-sink runtime/product concerns into the protocol. The only Axon-adjacent confirmation is that callee may be an agent URA (already true), so CLI rides the existing Invoke RPC.
- **现状：** partial — remote-pin ships ONLY for device/hub targets and ONLY under axon-pb. Evidence: invoke.rs:105-205 wires --node to federation.forward_invoke behind cfg(feature="axon-pb"); federation_invoke.rs:139-336 implements the dial; parse_node_ura (federation_invoke.rs:70-101) accepts Device + Hub kinds and explicitly rejects Agent (tests parse_node_ura_rejects_agent_device_alias_shape :589, parse_node_ura_rejects_real_agent_profile_ura :648). Agent-URA-targeted realm invoke and the no-gate first-class form are missing. Header doc invoke.rs:16-30 is stale (says --node unsupported).
- **行动：** CLI-repo work (frame F-03 surface, secondary F-05 cleanup). Step 1: extend parse_node_ura to accept URAKind::Agent (target the hosted agent callee), routing it through the existing Invoke RPC with callee=agent URA; keep device/hub paths intact. Step 2: ungate the agent-URA path from axon-pb so it is first-class in all builds (leave the legacy device-forward bridge gated if it depends on cross-hub transport). Step 3: render the response via the CLI/EasyNet Surface path; do NOT add a Surface field to Axon. Step 4 (F-05 cleanup): rewrite the stale invoke.rs:16-30 header to match the shipped behavior and the new agent-URA capability; update/replace the two rejection tests. No RFC-003 sec0 note required — this is CLI-repo only; no Axon proto change.

### 31. 🔴 Axon ability decorator/descriptor (Python @ability, Rust AbilityDescriptor)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). Two different structs both named AbilityDescriptor: (a) Axon sdk/rust/src/ability.rs — command_template + to_tool_spec + version/tags, the SKILL.md/MCP export+deploy shape; (b) CLI src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs — owner_agent_ura + visibility + scope_subjects/agents + schema_summary + is_visible_to(), the RFC §1.6 governance shape. The RFC plan itself (§1.6/§A15, P9) says "AbilityDescriptor schema lands in CLI P4", so the governance/contract shape was deliberately homed in CLI. Axon's copy is the over-sunk fork: command_template is a CLI EXECUTOR detail (how a shell ability runs) and the Python @ability's module-global _ABILITY_REGISTRY directly violates INV-3 (ability is a method, NO global registry). The neutral declared SHAPE both languages must agree on is the §1.6 field set, NOT command_template.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — declaring "this is a callable ability with input/output schema + URA + safety hints" is core to the endgame (ability addressed by URA, governed by Axon). But the SHAPE that actually ships here (command_template-centric, to_tool_spec for legacy MCP, Python @ability writing a module-global _ABILITY_REGISTRY) encodes runtime/deploy concerns, not the neutral governance contract the endgame needs.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns the governance AbilityDescriptor (already shipped at src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs: visibility, scope, hints, is_visible_to, canonical_ability_ura) and owns how an ability actually runs — the command_template / exec-binding / SKILL.md-to-runtime mapping belongs in the EXECUTOR layer (core/ability_spec.rs AbilityManifest.exec), not in Axon. CLI is also the right home for the SKILL.md/MCP export rendering (front-door for agents).
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should provide ONE neutral cross-language ability-declaration SHAPE: the §1.6 descriptor field set (name, owner URA, visibility, scope axes, input/output_receipt schema summary, behavioral hints) as the importable contract any-language node agrees on, plus the to_tool_spec/MCP-hint mapping as a pure projection. It must NOT carry command_template (a shell-executor detail) and the Python facade must NOT use a module-global registry — an ability is a bound method on Client.tenant.ability per INV-3, not a decorator-registered global.
- **现状：** forked. Axon shape: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/ability.rs:55-92 (struct) + :36-43 Python @ability with _ABILITY_REGISTRY at sdk/python/easynet_axon/ability.py:33-43. Governance shape shipped separately in CLI: EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs:294-344, consumed at src/runtime/advertise.rs:39. Axon's sibling verbs (invoke_ability, execute_command, list_abilities, forget_all at ability.rs:525-628) are already stubbed "removed by AXON-RFC-001 P1.5; use Invoke", confirming this whole module is a deprecated runtime-flavored surface, not the live contract.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: converge the two descriptors onto the §1.6 field set as the single neutral SHAPE. Concretely (1) treat CLI src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs as the authoritative governance contract and have Axon's neutral library expose that field set (visibility/scope/hints/schema_summary) for cross-language agreement; (2) demote Axon ability.rs's command_template + to_tool_spec + deploy helpers to a CLI EXECUTOR concern (they are already half-dead P1.5 stubs); (3) replace the Python @ability+_ABILITY_REGISTRY decorator with a method-bound declaration per INV-3 (no global registry). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work touching sdk/rust + sdk/python — sec0 forbids modifying axon proto/runtime to satisfy CLI; do this as an Axon-owned RFC change (under AXON-RFC-001 §1.6 lineage), not a CLI-driven patch, and land the absorption back into Axon per F-01 sec7 (Cli runs ahead; Axon absorbs ontology back).

### 32. 🔴 federation.forward_invoke

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The boundary criterion is decisive: two different-language/instance nodes MUST agree on the forward_invoke argument shape to interoperate, so that shape is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon. But the two repos disagree: Axon hub-profile deserializes ForwardInvokeArgs `{target_ura, ability_name, function_name, arguments_b64}` (hub_profile.rs:1311, shard_resolver.rs:50, "RFC-002 §5.2"), while CLI emits/parses `{target_ura, inner_envelope_b64, causal_context_bytes, forward_deadline_ms}` with inner_envelope_b64 wrapping `{ability,args,call_id}` (federation_invoke.rs:190-195, daemon_invocation_service.rs:5166, "DEC-N4 §2.1"). Same ability name, two incompatible wire contracts — a protocol fork. A CLI daemon calling an Axon hub-profile would fail with "ability_name required" / "arguments_b64 decode".`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — cross-hub forward-invoke is the mechanism that makes an owner-device ability addressable by URA from another realm/hub and returns its result; it is the literal wire that "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA" depends on across federation boundaries.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the RUNTIME + FRONT-DOOR role: the `easynet ability invoke --node` verb (parse_node_ura front door), the local-daemon dispatch_federation_forward_invoke that resolves a PresenceRegistry session and pushes the inner envelope down it, and the cross-hub dialer. CLI should CONSTRUCT the Axon-defined ForwardInvokeArgs (import the shape), not mint its own JSON layout. The deadline/causal-context fields, if CLI-only orchestration hints, stay CLI-side or get promoted into the contract by Axon — not invented unilaterally in the daemon.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the SHAPE: one canonical ForwardInvokeArgs / ForwardInvokeReceipt the contract, in any language. Today Axon already has it (ForwardInvokeArgs in shard_resolver.rs, hub_profile.hub_forward_invoke) but only as runtime-rs structs, and it differs from what CLI ships. Axon must publish the single authoritative forward_invoke arg/receipt shape (and decide whether causal_context/deadline are part of the contract) so CLI daemon and SDK bindings derive from one definition.
- **现状：** forked. CLI side shipped: EasyNet-Cli/src/support/federation_invoke.rs:139-300 (invoke_via_federation_forward), daemon parse at src/services/invocation_transport/daemon_invocation_service.rs:5166/5197 (inner_envelope_b64 shape). Axon side shipped with a DIFFERENT shape: EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/shard_resolver.rs:50 (ForwardInvokeArgs target_ura/ability_name/function_name/arguments_b64) and hub_profile.rs:1306-1395 (hub_forward_invoke). Backend now submits complete Invocations and no longer owns a routing classifier.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: converge the forked forward_invoke wire onto ONE Axon-owned contract. Concretely: (1) ratify a single ForwardInvokeArgs/ForwardInvokeReceipt in Axon (reconcile DEC-N4 §2.1 inner_envelope_b64 vs RFC-002 §5.2 ability_name/arguments_b64; decide if causal_context_bytes/forward_deadline_ms are contract or CLI-local), (2) regenerate the SDK shape, (3) make CLI federation_invoke.rs + daemon_invocation_service.rs import/construct that shape instead of hand-rolled JSON. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo contract work — open it under sec0 as a contract-convergence item before touching CLI emitters, since the protocol definition must land first.

### 33. 🔴 Hub ability-catalog + invoke REST surface

- **裁决：** `FORKED — The REST/catalog/prepare-submit ORCHESTRATION correctly lives in EasyNet (product surface + hub routing; consumes CLI runtimes, not Axon directly — right per the thesis). BUT the contract shapes it stands on are forked into the hub: the canonical-invocation-bytes encoder is reimplemented at EasyNet/backend/internal/daemon_grpc/canonical.go ("byte-exact parity with the Rust reference", kept in sync only by conformance vectors) while EasyNet-Axon already ships the Go encoder at sdk/go/easynet/invocation/axiom.go::CanonicalInvocationBytes, and the hub additionally carries a parallel internal/axon/ package (axiom_shape_test.go, admission.go). Two different-language nodes must AGREE on these bytes to interoperate, so by the boundary criterion the encoder/envelope/admission shape is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon; a hub-local copy is a protocol fork. The endpoint shape itself is fine where it is.`
- **当前所在：** easynet
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the product front: a logged-in user sees the federation catalog (abilities addressed by URA, fanned out via each runtime's meta.list_abilities) and drives a signed invoke (prepare → browser Ed25519 sign over canonical bytes → submit → Receipt). That is precisely the endgame surface: owner identity signs, ability lives on the owner device, addressed by URA, returns a result, auditable. The browser holding the per-device key and signing canonical bytes locally (not the server) is the user-owns-identity guarantee made concrete.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI is the runtime the hub fans out into: it must answer meta.list_abilities (it does — src/runtime/ability_dispatch.rs:1418 list_abilities + meta_ability::list_abilities_handler) and execute the signed invoke against the canonical bytes / admission gate. CLI must consume the SAME Axon canonical-bytes + admission contract the hub uses, so a hub-prepared envelope verifies byte-for-byte on the device. CLI provides RUNTIME + EXECUTOR here; it does NOT own the catalog REST shape.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon is the single source of the envelope 7-tuple, CanonicalInvocationBytes, signature verify, and admission gate as a neutral cross-language library (already present: sdk/go/easynet/invocation/axiom.go, sdk/rust reference, sdk/python). The hub and CLI should both IMPORT this one encoder rather than each carrying a parity copy. The catalog/list shape: meta.list_abilities is the federation discovery contract — its descriptor shape (AbilityDescriptor) is Axon-governable; the GET /abilities aggregation/exposure-lane filtering is NOT Axon (product policy, stays in EasyNet).
- **现状：** shipped (product surface) but forked (contract). REST surface shipped: EasyNet/backend/api/easynet.api:677-693 (ListAllAbilities, InvokeAbility/SignedInvokeAbilityReq, PrepareEnvelope); listAllAbilitiesLogic.go:159-306 fans out via NewInvokeAbilityLogic meta.list_abilities; prepareEnvelopeLogic.go + submitSignedInvocationLogic.go implement prepare/HMAC-bind/sign/verify. Fork evidence: EasyNet/backend/internal/daemon_grpc/canonical.go:82 reimplements CanonicalInvocationBytes parallel to EasyNet-Axon/sdk/go/easynet/invocation/axiom.go:245; hub go.mod:94 already has replace easynet.run/axon/sdk/go but also keeps internal/axon/. CLI side shipped: src/runtime/ability_dispatch.rs list_abilities.
- **行动：** Keep the REST/catalog/prepare-submit surface in EasyNet (no move). De-fork the contract: collapse EasyNet/backend/internal/daemon_grpc/canonical.go and the canonical/admission parts of internal/axon/ onto the Axon Go SDK encoder (sdk/go/easynet/invocation/axiom.go) that go.mod already vendors, so there is one canonical-bytes implementation per language. Gate the cutover on the existing conformance vectors (sdk/conformance/cases/axiom-*.json) proving byte parity before deletion. Frame: F-07 ontology-absorption (Axon is the source of the invocation/envelope contract; the hub absorbs back to it rather than maintaining a parity fork). RFC-003 sec0: the Axon-repo work is consume-side only — no new contract is being authored, the SDK encoder already exists — so this is a fork-removal/import change, not an RFC-003 transport-plane spec change; no sec0 protocol amendment required.

### 35. ✅ AXIOM subject threading on invoke (--subject from envelope only)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — split is clean, not a fork. The subject SHAPE (SubjectIdentity, its presence in the signed 7-tuple, the wire field) is the cross-language contract two different-language nodes must agree on, and it lives in Axon (types.proto:440 `SubjectIdentity subject = 22`; axiom.rs subject in canonical bytes + `tamper_subject_breaks_signature`/`subject_is_part_of_signed_bytes`). The CLI side is purely how this device runs/exposes it: a `--subject` front-door flag, threading onto the Axon Invoke wire, and the INV-SUBJECT-ENVELOPE handler guard. No protocol copy is forked into CLI (the canonicalisation/signing stays in Axon); no runtime/UI is over-sunk into Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — subject is the AXIOM Q5 "acted-on resource" slot. Binding the resource a URA acts on into the signed envelope (not args) is exactly what makes an invocation auditable by easynet-verify (the receipt's subject_binding is covered by the signature) and governable (admission/permission can scope on subject). It directly serves owner-state-on-device + audit endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Front door: `--subject <URI>` flag on `ability invoke` (and reject empty). Runtime: thread the trimmed subject onto the local-daemon Axon Invoke wire (invoke_local_ability_with_subject → invoke_with_subject → proto subject field). Executor: deliver subject to handlers ONLY via EnvelopeContext.subject and enforce INV-SUBJECT-ENVELOPE by rejecting any `subject` key smuggled through --args. CLI must NOT re-implement subject canonicalisation/signing — it imports Axon for that.
- **Axon 预期实现：** The neutral contract: SubjectIdentity as a distinct type from AgentIdentity (INV-7), subject as a member of the AXIOM 7-tuple, subject included in the canonical signed bytes (so tampering breaks the signature), §5.1 subject=callee defaulting at the SDK boundary so the wire field is always populated (INV-1), and the `subject = 22` proto wire field. This is the byte-identical, cross-language shape every node honours.
- **现状：** shipped on both sides. CLI: src/cli/invoke.rs:91-102 (flag + INV-SUBJECT-ENVELOPE doc), 195-202 (trim/thread to invoke_local_ability_with_subject); src/support/local_invoke.rs:74 and src/support/local_daemon_grpc.rs:346-351 (wire); src/runtime/ability_dispatch.rs:70 EnvelopeContext; src/runtime/agents/media_abilities.rs:319 reject_subject_in_args + tests at 560. Axon: sdk/rust/src/invocation/axiom.rs:69-95,160,289,599,727; core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:440.
- **行动：** No code change required — feature is correctly placed and shipped on both sides. Maintenance only (F-05 cleanup): keep the CLI flag doc and INV-SUBJECT-ENVELOPE reason strings in sync with Axon RFC-001 §5.1 wording as the axiom doc evolves; ensure any new envelope-aware ability uses register_*_with_envelope rather than reading subject from args. No RFC-003 sec0 Axon-repo work needed (Axon contract already landed).

Next step (F-07 ontology-absorption): keep Axon as the single source for invoke->receipt and continue folding any CLI-side receipt/envelope helpers back into Axon's contract rather than letting CLI grow parallel structs; verify the Rust LocalRuntime stays behaviour-pinned to the Python reference as RFC-001 evolves (RFC-003 sec0: any change here is an Axon-repo edit and must NOT be made as a CLI-local protocol patch).

### 39. ✅ core ability invoke verb (local Axon dispatch)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI front-door + runtime). The boundary criterion: the thing two different-language nodes must AGREE on is the InvokeRequest/Envelope/InvocationReceipt wire shape — and that is in Axon (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto: service Invocation rpc Invoke, message InvokeRequest, InvocationReceipt). The CLI verb does NOT redefine that shape; it parses operator flags, builds the Axon InvokeRequest, ships it over daemon.sock, and unwraps the result for a human. That is "how this device exposes invoke to a human", which is CLI FRONT-DOOR. No protocol fork (CLI imports the generated axon.v1 client from easynet_axon::pb::axon::v1::invocation_client), no product UI leak, no over-sink. LocalRuntime/admission/dispatch/receipt/ledger correctly sits in the CLI daemon (src/runtime/), as "how this device runs an ability," not in the neutral protocol.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the canonical front-door that turns "every action is an ability invocation" (AXON-RFC-001) into a runnable human verb. It dispatches through the local daemon's Axon Invocation surface into admission/dispatch/receipt/ledger, i.e. the owner device runs the ability, addressed by ability name (URA-shaped), governed and auditable. Directly serves the endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Exactly what it does: a human-facing verb that maps CLI args to a JSON args object + envelope subject, builds the Axon InvokeRequest, dispatches over daemon.sock to the daemon-hosted Invocation.Invoke, threads timeout via timeouts::effective_ms, and renders result (default envelope-unwrap to .result, --raw passthrough). Single dispatch chokepoint via invoke_local_ability_with_subject so every CLI surface shares one transport. Also the agent-facing MCP mirror of this verb belongs to CLI's dual front door.
- **Axon 预期实现：** The neutral wire contract only: Invocation service (Invoke/InvokeStream/InvokeBidi two-plus-realtime surface), InvokeRequest/InvokeResponse, Envelope (AXIOM seven-tuple), InvocationReceipt audit chain, InvocationState terminal states — all already in invoke.proto. Axon should NOT carry the `--args/--timeout/--subject/--raw` ergonomics or the envelope-unwrap-to-.result print convenience; those are device-front-door concerns and would pollute the contract.
- **现状：** shipped. CLI verb: EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/invoke.rs:105-223 (local arm at 188-204). Dispatch chokepoint: EasyNet-Cli/src/support/local_invoke.rs:74-82 (invoke_local_ability_with_subject) -> support/local_daemon_grpc.rs:346-416 (builds axon.v1 InvokeRequest, calls invocation_client::InvocationClient). Contract: EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:88-147. Runtime: EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/ (LocalRuntime, ability_dispatch.rs, local_runtime_invoker.rs).
- **行动：** No layer move needed; feature is correctly placed. F-05 cleanup: the local_invoke.rs header still documents the pre-P1.5 "control socket" lineage and a now-removed --node prohibition ("never grows a --node knob"), which contradicts the shipped --node federation arm in invoke.rs:111-183 — reconcile the doc comment to the current daemon.sock + federation-forward reality so a reader does not treat the comment as the boundary rule. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 does not apply.

## 回执与审计 / Receipt-Audit（21）

### 40. ◻️ Dispatch-side receipt header builder (ability_name -> header)

- **裁决：** `SPLIT: the BUILDER is CORRECT-in-CLI, but its OUTPUT TYPE is FORKED-should-be-Axon. The mapping logic (ability namespace prefix + local-agents.json + host URA -> which Agent owns this, self vs hosted) is how THIS device attributes its own dispatches at runtime — pure RUNTIME, belongs in CLI; pushing it into Axon would be over-sink (Axon does not read local-agents.json or run on a device). BUT the SHAPE it emits — HostedAgentReceiptHeader {callee_agent_ura, signer_agent_ura, Selfsigned|HostedBy{host_ura, host_attestation}} (src/runtime/hosted_receipt.rs:50-86) — is the §A12 hosted-attestation contract that two different-language nodes MUST agree on to verify, and Axon already defines it canonically as InvocationReceipt.callee_binding (21) / signer_binding (26) / host_attestation (27) with the identical Model-A/B self-vs-hosted semantics (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:692-725). The CLI re-states that contract as a hand-rolled serde struct instead of importing the Axon SDK type — a protocol fork of the receipt-attestation shape.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the seam that makes a locally-dispatched ability's result auditable: it attributes each successful local dispatch to the owning Agent URA (self-signed device-profile vs hosted consent/policy/mcp/llm) so easynet-verify can later pick the right pubkey. Directly serves "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, governed+auditable." Returning None pre-join is correct (no realm identity = nothing verifiable yet).
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the builder: the namespace->owner runtime mapping (device::owns / consent/policy/mcp/llm/<agent>.chat -> URA via local-agents.json, None pre-join) is legitimate CLI RUNTIME and stays. CLI SHOULD construct the header by populating the Axon SDK receipt-attestation type (callee_binding / signer_binding / host_attestation) rather than a CLI-private struct, and CLI owns the actual ed25519 signing (device-profile key in the daemon) when P5 lands — signing/identity-key custody is the CLI runtime's job.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already provides the contract: InvocationReceipt.{callee_binding, signer_binding, host_attestation} + the canonical_host_attestation_bytes(callee_ura, signer_ura) rule and the Model-A (selfsigned: signer absent/==callee, attestation empty) vs Model-B (hosted: signer != callee, attestation verifies) semantics, RFC-001 §A12, invoke.proto:702-725. Axon SHOULD additionally expose a small typed builder/constructor in its Rust SDK (sdk/rust) that enforces those invariants (the EmptyCallee/SignerNotHost/SignerNotCallee checks the CLI hand-rolled in hosted_receipt.rs) so CLI consumes one neutral builder instead of forking it.
- **现状：** partial+forked. Builder shipped and well-tested: src/runtime/dispatch_receipt.rs:68-123 (+ tests 125-288), wired into the dispatch path at src/services/control/ability_proxy.rs:793-802 (attached to OutgoingFrame::Result.receipt_header, a CLI IPC frame). FORKED type: src/runtime/hosted_receipt.rs:50-206 hand-rolls the §A12 shape + invariants that Axon already owns in invoke.proto:692-725; CLI does not import the Axon receipt type for this. PARTIAL signing: host_attestation is a b"P5-pending" sentinel (dispatch_receipt.rs:117-122), real ed25519 signing deferred to P5; header rides the CLI control-channel JSON frame, not yet mapped onto the Axon proto InvocationReceipt on the wire.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: keep the namespace->owner builder in CLI (it is runtime, do not move it), but retire the hand-rolled HostedAgentReceiptHeader/SigningModel struct in favor of the Axon SDK receipt-attestation type (callee_binding/signer_binding/host_attestation) so the §A12 shape stops being forked into CLI; at P5, have the builder emit into the Axon InvocationReceipt and add device-profile ed25519 signing to replace the P5-pending sentinel. RFC-003 sec0: the Axon-side work is additive only (expose a typed §A12 attestation builder in EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust around the EXISTING invoke.proto fields 26/27) — no proto field changes, so it does not modify the Axon protocol contract; if even the SDK builder is out of scope, CLI must at minimum import the existing Axon proto types rather than its parallel struct. Verify with cli + axon test suites green after the type swap.

### 41. 🔴 host_attestation signing (P5 ed25519 pass)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — The canonical byte layout being signed (canonical_host_attestation_bytes, the HostedAttestation struct, the §A12 0x00/0x01 discriminator tail) is a CONTRACT: the CLI signer and the easynet-verify verifier are two separate binaries that MUST agree on it byte-for-byte to interoperate. It is defined ONLY in EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:304 (consumed by easynet-verify) and is ABSENT from the Axon SDK the CLI actually links (sdk/rust, easynet_axon::invocation::axiom — has sign_receipt but no host_attestation surface). So the contract is forked across two Axon trees and the CLI cannot reach the canonical bytes; that is why it emits b"P5-pending". The signing pass itself (load device key, call sign) is correctly CLI runtime; the missing piece is the contract export.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — host_attestation is what makes a hosted-Agent receipt auditable by easynet-verify: it proves the device that signed on behalf of a hosted profile (consent/policy/mcp/llm) actually claims to host that callee URA. Without it the "governed by Axon, auditable by easynet-verify" endgame is unenforceable for any non-self-signed (Model B) ability, which is the common case once a device hosts sub-agents.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI RUNTIME should perform the actual signing pass: in dispatch_receipt.rs replace b"P5-pending" with ed25519_sign(device_signing_key, canonical_host_attestation_bytes(callee_ura, host_ura)) using the keyring it already holds (src/runtime/keyring/*), and pass the resulting bytes into HostedAgentReceiptHeader::new_hosted. CLI must NOT define the canonical byte layout locally — doing so would deepen the fork.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should export ONE canonical host-attestation contract from the SDK the CLI imports (sdk/rust / easynet_axon::invocation::axiom or ::admission): canonical_host_attestation_bytes(callee_ura, signer_ura), the HostedAttestation type, and the §A12 hosted tail discriminator in canonical_receipt_bytes — identical to what easynet-verify checks. The two Axon trees (core/runtime-rs/client-sdk and sdk/rust) must converge on a single definition so signer and verifier cannot drift.
- **现状：** partial/forked. CLI: EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/dispatch_receipt.rs:90-122 emits b"P5-pending" placeholder (doc at :38-41 "P5 backend SDK rewrite ships the actual ed25519 signing pass"). Contract present in EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:304 (canonical_host_attestation_bytes) and used by EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify/src/main.rs:212. Contract ABSENT from the CLI-linked SDK: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/invocation/axiom.rs has sign_receipt (:467) but no host_attestation surface (grep for canonical_host_attestation_bytes in sdk/rust returns nothing).
- **行动：** Step 1 (Axon, F-07 ontology-absorption): converge the host-attestation contract into the SDK the CLI links — re-export canonical_host_attestation_bytes + HostedAttestation + the §A12 tail from easynet_axon (sdk/rust), making it byte-identical to core/runtime-rs/client-sdk so signer and easynet-verify share one definition; resolve the two-tree fork. This is Axon-repo work — per RFC-003 sec0 it must land as a contract change (proto/SDK) reviewed before the CLI consumes it, not as a CLI-local copy. Step 2 (CLI, F-05 cleanup): in dispatch_receipt.rs:90-122 swap b"P5-pending" for a real ed25519 signature over the now-exported canonical_host_attestation_bytes(callee_ura, host_ura), signed with the device keyring key; add a test that easynet-verify accepts the produced hosted receipt. Do NOT define the canonical bytes CLI-side.

### 42. 🔴 Hosted-agent receipt header (Model A self-signed / Model B hosted)

- **裁决：** `FORKED — the receipt SHAPE two different-language nodes must AGREE on already lives in Axon (invoke.proto InvocationReceipt fields 26/27 signer_binding + host_attestation, with §A12 canonical-bytes/verify rule and RFC-001 §1.3 Model A/B). CLI's HostedAgentReceiptHeader/SigningModel is a second, hand-written copy of that contract (Vec<u8> + String URAs vs proto AgentIdentity/bytes) with no codegen binding the two, so it can drift. The CONSTRUCTION/staging of these bytes on this device is legitimately CLI (runtime/executor), but the field set is contract and must derive from Axon, not be redeclared.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — it is the mechanism that makes a daemon-spawned keyless agent's receipt reconstructable offline by easynet-verify (callee/signer split + host_attestation lets a verifier pick the right pubkey and confirm the host legitimately hosts the callee). Directly serves "owner identity + auditable, governed by Axon" endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI SHOULD keep the runtime concern: decide per-ability whether dispatch is Model A vs B (device::owns vs hosted profile lookup in dispatch_receipt.rs), obtain the real host_attestation, and stage the values to hand the dispatcher — but it should populate the Axon-generated InvocationReceipt type (signer_binding/host_attestation) rather than carry an independently-defined HostedAgentReceiptHeader. The validate() invariants (signer==callee for A, signer==host for B) are fine as a CLI-side trust-boundary guard layered on the Axon type.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD remain the single definition of the on-wire shape (already does: invoke.proto fields 26/27 + canonical_host_attestation_bytes + the Model A/B verification rule), and SHOULD expose a small SDK builder/helper for self-signed vs hosted so CLI imports the contract instead of re-typing it. Verification (signer pubkey + attestation check against DirectoryEntry) stays in axon-runtime admission gate per the module's own "NOT a verifier" note.
- **现状：** partial+forked. Axon: contract shipped at EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:702-725 (signer_binding=26, host_attestation=27) + §A12 in docs/rfc/AXON-RFC-001-plan-v4.1.1.md:188-215. CLI: parallel staging shape shipped+wired at EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/hosted_receipt.rs:50-206, consumed by src/runtime/dispatch_receipt.rs:68-123 and src/services/control/ability_proxy.rs:1396-1460, but signing deferred — host_attestation is the b"P5-pending" placeholder (dispatch_receipt.rs:90,121), so no real attestation is produced yet.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: collapse the fork — make CLI's hosted-receipt staging derive from the Axon InvocationReceipt fields (signer_binding/host_attestation) via the generated proto type / a thin Axon SDK builder, demoting hosted_receipt.rs to a CLI-side adapter+validator over the Axon type rather than a second contract definition. Keep dispatch_receipt.rs's per-ability Model A/B decision in CLI (runtime). RFC-003 sec0 note: the Axon-repo work (adding/confirming the self-signed vs hosted SDK builder so CLI imports rather than re-types the contract) must be filed under RFC-003 §0 classification as a contract/SDK change, not a transport change. Until P5 signing lands, the b"P5-pending" placeholder stays a tracked partial, not shippable as a real attestation.

### 43. 🔴 Receipt JSON on-disk contract (ReceiptJson schema)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos/places incompatibly) — belongs in Axon, but the shape is forked even WITHIN Axon. By the boundary criterion this is pure cross-language CONTRACT: two different-language verifiers must AGREE byte-for-byte on field names to interoperate, so it correctly lives in Axon, NOT CLI. But there is no single canonical schema. The verifier `easynet-verify/src/main.rs:662-727` (ReceiptJson) expects `payload_digest_hex` / `invocation_nonce_base64` / nested `callee_signature.signature_base64` / `signer_binding` / `host_attestation_base64`, and the ONLY producer of that shape is its own sibling e2e test. The actual cross-language SDK writers use a DIFFERENT vocabulary: Python `sdk/python/.../invocation/bundle.py:170-204` emits `payload_sha256_hex` (not payload_digest_hex), `invocation_nonce_hex` (not _base64), flat `callee_signature_hex` (not nested base64), and has NO signer_binding/host_attestation fields; the Python cross-lang test reads `callee_signature_hex`. So a Python-written receipt cannot be verified by the Rust `easynet-verify` binary that is meant to be the universal verifier — the "neutral contract" is two incompatible dialects. CLI is correctly NOT a fork (its serde structs in src/runtime/invocation.rs are the runtime's internal in-memory model, not this on-disk audit shape).`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame requires "auditable by easynet-verify": a user owns their audit trail and any third party in any language must be able to re-verify a receipt chain offline from a stable on-disk shape. A language-neutral field-name JSON for receipts (hex/base64 digests, signature, signer_binding, host_attestation) is exactly the artifact that makes the audit dimension portable and trust-minimized.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a for defining the shape — CLI must NOT define or fork the receipt JSON field-names (that would be a protocol fork). CLI's only role here (EXECUTOR/RUNTIME) is to serialize its receipts THROUGH the Axon-defined schema when it persists/exports the audit chain, and to shell out to / embed the Axon `easynet-verify` for `easynet-verify` CLI verb. CLI should depend on the one canonical Axon serializer, never hand-roll the keys.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns this and must ship ONE canonical ReceiptJson schema (field-names + hex-vs-base64 encoding decision per field) plus a serialize+deserialize pair in every SDK (rust/python/node/go) that round-trips against the easynet-verify reader. Reconcile the two dialects: pick payload_digest_hex vs payload_sha256_hex, invocation_nonce_hex vs _base64, nested callee_signature{} vs flat callee_signature_hex, and make signer_binding/host_attestation_base64 (the §A12 hosted-Agent fields) present in all writers. Pin it in the RFC-001 receipt section as the on-disk audit contract distinct from the pb prost type. A cross-language conformance test (Python writes → Rust easynet-verify accepts, and vice versa) must guard it.
- **现状：** forked — defined only as the reader in EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify/src/main.rs:662-727 and produced only by its sibling test easynet-verify/tests/e2e_receipt_verify.rs:102-109. The cross-language SDK writers use an incompatible field vocabulary: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/python/easynet_axon/invocation/bundle.py:170-204 (payload_sha256_hex / invocation_nonce_hex / callee_signature_hex, no signer_binding/host_attestation). No conformance test ties SDK writers to the verifier reader.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption (Axon-repo work, RFC-003 sec0 note: this is contract-shape work that must land in Axon under the F-01 sec7 "Cli runs ahead; Axon absorbs the ontology back" regime, with the canonical schema ratified in RFC-001 before any writer is touched). Next step: (1) in Axon, ratify ONE ReceiptJson field-name+encoding schema in RFC-001's receipt section, choosing between the verifier dialect (payload_digest_hex/_base64/nested+attestation) and the SDK dialect (payload_sha256_hex/_hex/flat); (2) make all SDK writers (python bundle.py, rust-sdk, node, go) emit that exact shape including signer_binding/host_attestation_base64; (3) add a cross-language conformance test (Python/Node-written receipt verified by the Rust easynet-verify binary) so the fork cannot reopen. Do NOT add any receipt-JSON schema definition into CLI — CLI consumes the Axon serializer only.

### 44. ✅ Admission-emits-receipt at gate (RFC-001 §5.3)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (split is right; the gap is CLI wiring, not abstraction). The receipt SHAPE is already in Axon and must be: InvocationReceipt struct (sdk/rust/src/invocation/audit.rs:87), constructor with prev_receipt_hash/self_hash chaining (audit.rs:161-178), sign_receipt (axiom.rs:467), verify_receipt_signature consumed by easynet-verify (core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify/src/main.rs:48). Cross-language nodes must AGREE on this to interoperate, so it is contract = Axon, and it already lives there. Axon's run_admission deliberately returns Result<(),AxonError> (admission.rs:626) and the doc-contract (admission.rs:614-616) explicitly assigns the mint-and-emit to the runtime: "MUST NOT emit an admitted receipt" on reject implies the runtime MUST emit it on accept. So minting/emission at the gate is RUNTIME = CLI. No fork, no over-sink: this is a genuine CLI-side MISSING-WIRING against a contract that exists.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the admitted-receipt is exactly the first link of the audit chain the endgame promises (owner+easynet-verify can prove "this call was admitted at the gate, by this caller, for this ability, at this time"). Today the chain only begins at terminal time via LedgerSink, so an accepted-then-aborted invocation leaves no signed gate evidence. Verifiable audit is served only halfway.
- **CLI 预期实现：** On admission accept (run_admission Ok, and the URI-only Device arm), the runtime daemon should mint an Admitted InvocationReceipt using the Axon constructor, sign it with the daemon's identity key via Axon sign_receipt, chain it (prev_receipt_hash) ahead of the terminal receipt, hand it to the same persistence the LedgerSink/InvocationLedger uses, and surface it: set it on unary InvokeResponse.receipt (currently unset at daemon_invocation_service.rs:1447-1456,1482-1536) and make the bidi/session frame-0 a real minted+signed receipt rather than the bare state=Admitted liveness stub (build_bidi_admission_receipt daemon_invocation_service.rs:3980-3989). This is RUNTIME+EXECUTOR work, CLI-local.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — Axon already provides everything needed (InvocationReceipt + chaining constructor + sign_receipt + verify_receipt_signature) and correctly keeps run_admission a pass/fail decision. No Axon change required; do NOT push mint/emit into Axon (would over-sink runtime concerns into the neutral contract).
- **现状：** missing (CLI runtime). SharedReceiptStore removed in Phase 5a (admission_facade.rs:466-472 Device arm, 575-586 strict arm both return Ok(()) with no mint; tombstone at daemon_invocation_service.rs:5506). Unary InvokeResponse.receipt never set (build sites 1447-1456,1482-1536). Only a non-load-bearing transport-liveness frame-0 with bare state=Admitted and all other fields default exists for bidi/session (build_bidi_admission_receipt 3980-3989) — not a signed, caller/ability/nonce-bound, chained admitted-receipt. DEC-012 deferred mint to PR-10 then Phase 5a deleted the store; the §5.3 obligation is currently unmet.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup (wire, do not invent): in EasyNet-Cli, re-introduce admission-time minting at the run_admission Ok arm (admission_facade.rs:575-586) and the Device URI-only Ok arm (466-472) — call Axon's InvocationReceipt constructor + sign_receipt with the daemon key, chain prev_receipt_hash before the terminal receipt, persist through the existing InvocationLedger path, and populate unary InvokeResponse.receipt (1447-1456,1482-1536) plus upgrade build_bidi_admission_receipt (3980-3989) to emit the real signed receipt instead of the liveness stub. Verify end-to-end with easynet-verify accepting the admitted receipt's callee_signature. No RFC-003 sec0 note needed: this is CLI-repo runtime wiring against an already-ratified Axon contract; no Axon-repo change.

### 45. ✅ AxiomBinding 7-tuple on every receipt (RFC-001 §3.6/§4.3)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (Axon). By the boundary criterion this is pure CONTRACT: two different-language nodes MUST agree on the exact canonical byte layout or cross-SDK verification breaks. Verified the agreement is real — canonical_receipt_bytes/AxiomBinding exist in Axon's Rust (sdk/rust/src/invocation/axiom.rs:300-321, audit.rs:62-130), Go (sdk/go/easynet/invocation/audit.go), and Python (sdk/python/easynet_axon/invocation/axiom.py) SDKs, with sdk/python/tests/test_cross_language_verify.py asserting Python-producer→Rust-verifier parity. Excluding callee_signature from self_hash lets hash-integrity and signature-integrity verify independently, which is what a neutral auditor needs.

- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the binding struct, the canonical_receipt_bytes layout (length-prefixed blocks, callee_signature excluded from self_hash, absent-signer marker), and the cross-language conformance test. Verified shipped at EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/invocation/audit.rs:62-130 (AxiomBinding, canonical_serialise, compute_receipt_hash, new_receipt) and axiom.rs:300-321 (ReceiptBody, canonical_receipt_bytes). This is the neutral cross-language contract — exactly where it belongs.
- **现状：** shipped — Axon Rust SDK (audit.rs:62-130, axiom.rs:300-508), Go SDK (sdk/go/easynet/invocation/audit.go), Python SDK (sdk/python/easynet_axon/invocation/axiom.py), cross-language parity test (sdk/python/tests/test_cross_language_verify.py:203-217). CLI consumption shipped at runtime_factory.rs:18,67-160. RFC anchors present: 001-envelope-axiom-alignment.md R1/R2, 001-a16-bidi-frame-chain.md:274-276 (both receipts carry 7-tuple per §3.6).
- **行动：** No layer move needed — feature is correctly placed and shipped in Axon with verified tri-language parity. Frame tag F-07 ontology-absorption (settled): the receipt/binding ontology already lives in Axon as the neutral contract; CLI imports rather than forks. Optional follow-up under F-05 cleanup: confirm node/java/swift SDKs (present as dirs under sdk/) carry the same canonical_receipt_bytes layout and are covered by the cross-language verify matrix — if any lack it, that is a PARITY gap, not a placement error. RFC-003 sec0: not a transport-plane change; no sec0 note required.

### 46. ✅ Backend durable receipt store (Postgres/ent Receipt entity)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (EasyNet backend). ### 47. ✅ canonical_host_attestation_bytes + HostedAttestation verify contract

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (belongs in Axon). This is pure contract SHAPE: two different-language nodes must AGREE on the exact canonical byte layout (`"host-attest:v1\n"` + length-prefixed callee_ura + signer_ura) and the proto fields signer_binding=26 / host_attestation=27, or a signer and an offline verifier cannot interoperate. It is decidable/governable per the neutral contract, not how one device runs. It is not runtime, not product UI. A CLI-local copy of these bytes would be a protocol fork; none exists (verified — CLI has no reimplementation). Caveat: it is currently a Rust-only realization of a cross-language contract, so it is CORRECT-layer but not yet fully cross-language landed.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — it directly defends the audit endgame ("auditable by easynet-verify"). Without host-attestation a hosting key could forge receipts under arbitrary callee URAs, breaking the "user owns identity+state+audit" guarantee for hosted (Model B) agents that have no key of their own. It is the cryptographic binding that lets an offline verifier trust a host-signed receipt attributed to a hosted callee.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a for the contract itself — CLI must NOT define or fork the canonical bytes. CLI's only legitimate role here is RUNTIME: when CLI signs receipts on behalf of a hosted agent (Model B), it must populate signer_binding + host_attestation by CALLING the Axon primitive (canonical_host_attestation_bytes / set the proto fields), never by hand-rolling the layout. Today CLI does not exercise hosted signing (admission_gate emits Model A self-signed only), so no CLI work is required yet.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns it and already provides: (1) versioned canonical_host_attestation_bytes(callee_ura, signer_ura) in client-sdk admission.rs:304-310; (2) HostedAttestation struct + its inclusion in canonical_receipt_bytes tail (0x00/0x01 discriminator, admission.rs:343-352); (3) proto fields invoke.proto:710-725; (4) offline enforcement in easynet-verify main.rs:97-175 (classify self-signed vs hosted, require/forbid attestation, re-derive bytes, check signature). What Axon still OWES: cross-language parity — Python/Go/Node SDKs do not yet implement host-attestation, so the contract is only honored by the Rust SDK. A non-Rust signer/verifier cannot round-trip a hosted receipt today.
- **现状：** partial. Shipped in Rust: EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:288-310 (struct + canonical bytes) and :343-352 (receipt tail); proto fields in core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:724-725 (+ vendored client-sdk/proto copy); offline verification in core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify/src/main.rs:97-175. Runtime only emits self-signed (Model A): admission_gate.rs:436-437,563-564 set signer_binding=None, host_attestation=empty. Cross-language SDKs (sdk/python, sdk/go, sdk/node) have NO host-attestation implementation (grep returned nothing). No fork in CLI.
- **行动：** Frame F-07 (ontology-absorption): the host-attestation shape is correctly born in Axon Rust; the next step is to ABSORB it into the neutral cross-language contract so Python/Go/Node SDKs emit and verify the same canonical_host_attestation_bytes(callee_ura, signer_ura) and the same self-signed/hosted discriminator. Concretely: (1) add canonical_host_attestation_bytes + receipt-tail discriminator to sdk/python, sdk/go, sdk/node with a conformance case under sdk/conformance/cases (mirror the existing cross-language-verify-parity fixture) pinning byte-identical output; (2) only after that, wire CLI runtime Model-B hosted signing to call the Axon primitive (no CLI-local bytes). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work — record it as a contract-parity item against RFC-001 §A12 in the Axon RFC sec0 changelog (versioned tag host-attest:v1 must remain stable; any layout change requires a v2 tag, never an in-place edit).

### 49. ✅ Causal-context audit closure (Scalar/List/Merkle predecessor resolution)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (Axon). By the boundary criterion this is contract-half work that belongs with the contract: the CausalContext shape (None/Scalar/List/Merkle, ReceiptRef/ReceiptList/MerkleRoot, canonical-byte layout) is the cross-language thing two nodes MUST agree on — it is defined in EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:343 (RFC 001 §3.3 / AXIOM §2.2). The walk is the auditor that closes invariants iii+iv over that contract, and it is deliberately runtime-free (Cargo.toml forbids tonic/gRPC/runtime deps), so it is not device-runtime. It must produce the same verdict regardless of which language emitted the receipts, so it is contract, not CLI orchestration. Not a fork: CLI source references causal_binding/CausalContext only as a consumer through easynet_axon::invocation::wire helpers and dispatch_shim wiring; it carries no walk_from/verify_chain copy.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — directly realizes the "auditable by easynet-verify" leg of the endgame. The whole point is that the user owns an audit trail that any third party can independently re-verify: every receipt's signed predecessor reference is resolved and its canonical SHA matched against the actual prior receipt, so the causal graph cannot be forged or re-ordered after the fact. This is the governability/decidability guarantee made checkable.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a for the audit-closure walk itself — verification of an offline receipt bundle is not how this device runs/exposes; it is a neutral checker. CLI's only legitimate role here is upstream and as a consumer: as EXECUTOR it must emit receipts that carry a correct, signed causal_binding (Scalar on linear successor, List on fan-in join without reordering) so that the Axon walker can later close the chain, and as FRONT-DOOR it may shell out to the easynet-verify binary on a user's bundle. It must not host its own chain-walk implementation (that would be a protocol fork).
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns both halves and already does: (1) the CausalContext contract in proto/types.proto including canonical byte layout (RFC 001 §4.2) and "unknown form ⇒ admission reject"; (2) the independent auditor easynet-verify that resolves each predecessor by canonical SHA, matches the referenced receipt_hash to a bundle entry, recurses on Scalar/List, detects cycles, bounds depth, and terminates Merkle with a typed warning. Remaining contract-level gap Axon should own: define the proof_ura deref / Merkle proof-artefact verification so large-fan-in audit is closable rather than deferred.
- **现状：** shipped (partial on Merkle). Walk is implemented and live: EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify/src/main.rs:298-358 (verify_chain) and 360-468 (walk_from); contract at core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:343-370. Partial: Merkle arm (main.rs:449-465) terminates with a TerminatedAtMerkle-style typed warning — proof_ura is not dereferenced, so unbounded-fan-in chains are signature-checked but not causally closed (proto comment at types.proto:348 marks merkle "auditor deferred").
- **行动：** Keep in Axon; no move. Frame F-07 ontology-absorption — the audit closure is the verifier consuming the ratified CausalContext ontology, exactly where it belongs. Next step (Axon-repo, so RFC-003 sec0 note applies: land the spec delta before code): file the Merkle proof_ura deref as a scoped follow-up — specify in RFC 001 §3.3 the proof-artefact receipt shape and resolution rule, then extend walk_from's Merkle arm (main.rs:449-465) to fetch and verify the proof artefact, replacing the typed-warning termination. Until then leave v1 behavior as-is (honest, non-contradicting termination).

It is not runtime/transport (Cargo.toml hard-bans tonic/gRPC/async/bridge; only dep is admission), so it is not product/runtime over-sunk. It has no product UI (single-line stdout, exit code). It is the neutral, importable, decidable+governable contract landed as a standalone reference checker — exactly Axon's role. Note: it is a binary in the Axon repo rather than a pure library symbol, but that is the legitimate "reference verifier" form of a contract; a Python/Go node would reimplement the same canonical bytes and MUST match this binary's verdict, which is precisely why the bytes must be defined once in Axon.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame test literally names it: "auditable by easynet-verify." It is the artifact that makes the user-owned identity+state+audit promise checkable. A third party on a fresh machine, holding only the binary + a receipt JSON + the callee pubkey, can prove an invocation was admitted by a specific callee with zero trust in the runtime/transport that produced it. Chain mode extends this to causal provenance audit across a bundle. This is the trust capstone of the whole model.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a for the verification primitive itself — CLI must NOT grow its own receipt-verify logic (that would fork the canonical encoder, WRONG). CLI's only legitimate adjacent role is FRONT-DOOR convenience: a daemon/CLI verb (e.g. `easynet receipt verify`/`receipt audit`) that shells out to or invokes the Axon verifier over a local receipt store and renders the result to a human or MCP agent. The decision logic stays in Axon; CLI only locates the receipt bundle and presents the verdict. Confirmed today CLI has no verify verb and no canonical_receipt_bytes copy (grep of EasyNet-Cli/src found none), so there is no fork to unwind.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Exactly what exists: own the single canonical signing-byte definition + signature/chain verification semantics, and ship the dependency-disciplined standalone reference verifier (single + chain, cycle detection, depth cap, Merkle-terminate-and-defer) so any language binding can be conformance-checked against it. Axon should additionally publish the canonical-bytes spec + a conformance vector set so cross-language nodes (Python/Go SDK) provably agree with this binary. The current Merkle v1 "terminate without resolving proof_ura" is the one open contract gap to close in-spec, not in CLI.
- **现状：** shipped, in Axon. Binary at EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify/src/main.rs (single mode verify_single_receipt; chain mode verify_chain at :298 with walk_from depth/cycle guards :360-468; canonical SHA via compute_canonical_sha :475-523). Canonical primitives it imports live at client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs (canonical_receipt_bytes :326, verify_receipt_signature :362, sha256 :223, Scalar/List/Merkle forms :172-185). Cargo.toml enforces the zero-runtime/zero-transport dependency ban list. No CLI fork, no CLI verb (EasyNet-Cli grep clean). EasyNet repo references it only in RFC docs (AXON-RFC-001-P5-rewrite-receipt-audit.md etc.), not in product code.
- **行动：** No relayering required — feature is in the correct layer. Next steps, all F-07 ontology-absorption (consolidating contract truth in Axon): (1) close the Merkle v1 gap — define in-spec how proof_ura resolution works for unbounded fan-in audit so the verifier stops "terminating without contradiction"; (2) publish cross-language conformance vectors so a future Python/Go verifier provably matches this binary (prevents a future fork). Both are Axon-repo work — RFC-003 sec0 note: this touches the receipt/canonical-bytes contract surface, so any change must land as an RFC-003 sec0 protocol amendment (spec + DEC) before code, not as an ad-hoc verifier tweak. Optional F-03 surface follow-on (separate, CLI-owned, do NOT bundle): add a thin `easynet receipt verify` front-door verb in CLI that invokes this Axon binary over the local receipt store — front-door only, zero verification logic copied.

### 55. ✅ Hash-chained receipt + chain verification (verify_receipt_chain)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (Axon). Two different-language nodes MUST agree byte-for-byte on canonical receipt bytes and the chain-walk rules to interoperate as auditor/auditee, so the shape is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon. Verified it is NOT forked into CLI: CLI imports the proto-generated InvocationReceipt (daemon_invocation_service.rs:137) and has no verify_receipt_chain / hash-recompute copy. Not over-sunk product/runtime either — it is pure data shape + a verifier, no device orchestration or UI.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame requires "auditable by easynet-verify": a third party recomputes each receipt hash from canonical bytes and detects tampering/non-monotonic index without trusting the owner device. The hash chain + verify walk is the literal mechanism that makes owner-held state independently auditable.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI emits receipts using the Axon wire type as it runs an invocation (RUNTIME/EXECUTOR), and SHOULD expose an `easynet verify` front-door verb that loads a receipt log and calls the Axon-defined verifier — but CLI must NOT reimplement the hash recompute or chain rules. Today CLI correctly only emits (daemon_invocation_service.rs) and does not fork the walk.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD own (and does) the canonical-byte layout (RFC 001 §4.3 canonical_receipt_bytes), the proto fields prev_receipt_hash/self_hash (invoke.proto:660-674), and the reference verify_receipt_chain. What it still OWES: language-neutral golden conformance vectors so non-Rust verifiers (Go/TS/Python) provably reproduce self_hash and the same broken_index — the proto comment "Clients recompute to verify chain" is currently a promise with no cross-language fixture pinning it.
- **现状：** shipped in Axon: reference verifier EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/invocation/audit.rs:80-250 (InvocationReceipt, compute_receipt_hash, verify_receipt_chain, ChainCheckResult); wire contract EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:660-674. Consumed in CLI EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/daemon_invocation_service.rs:137 (proto type only, no fork). No EasyNet-hub reimplementation found. Conformance vectors: missing.
- **行动：** No relayering needed — placement is correct, do not touch. Next step (F-07 ontology-absorption, Axon-repo): add cross-language golden conformance vectors for verify_receipt_chain (canonical-bytes -> self_hash, plus tamper/non-monotonic broken_index cases) under Axon so Go/TS/Python verifiers are pinned to the Rust reference. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo audit-contract work; register it against the receipt-audit conformance line in RFC-003 sec0 before implementing, since it hardens the cross-language agreement surface rather than adding new fields.

### 56. ✅ Heartbeat sidecar terminal-receipt push to backend

- **裁决：** `CORRECT split; the missing piece belongs in CLI. The receipt SHAPE — InvocationReceipt with hash-chained prev_receipt_hash/self_hash + axiom 7-tuple binding + callee_signature (EasyNet-Axon invoke.proto:659, the cross-language contract two nodes must agree on) — is already in Axon, NOT forked into CLI: correct. The PUSH/MIRROR mechanism (daemon async-POST with bounded retry, deciding when/how this device ships its terminal receipts to the hub) is how-this-device-exposes-its-audit-trail = RUNTIME, belongs in CLI. It is not a protocol fork (carries the Axon receipt verbatim) and must not sink into Axon (Axon doesn't run on a device or hold the user key). Backend Append is pure Go persistence. Only defect: the CLI-side push module is essentially absent, not mislayered.`

The transport that lands a device-local signed receipt into Postgres so chain-walking and resource-history queries work is load-bearing for the audit value.
- **CLI 预期实现：** A single owned daemon-sidecar module that, on terminal-phase receipt emission, serializes the canonical Axon InvocationReceipt (not a re-derived CLI copy of the schema) and async-POSTs it to the hub's receipt-append endpoint with bounded retry + backoff, dedup by receipt_id/self_hash, and a bounded outbound queue that degrades to the existing in-memory ring (receipt_store.rs) on backend unavailability. Hosted by the heartbeat/daemon sidecar layer (easynet-daemon.rs:271-291, which today only sends federation.heartbeat). Failures logged, must not tear down IPC (existing heartbeat policy). RUNTIME plumbing only, no schema authorship.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Only the receipt contract, which Axon already provides: InvocationReceipt with prev_receipt_hash/self_hash hash-chain + axiom-7-tuple binding + callee_signature (invoke.proto:659). Axon must NOT gain any push/retry/transport-to-backend logic — that is device runtime, not the neutral cross-language contract. Axon's only standing obligation is to keep the canonical serialization (RFC-001 §4.3) stable so CLI push and Go backend Append agree byte-for-byte on self_hash; already specified.
- **现状：** partial-toward-missing. Receipt schema SHIPPED in Axon (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:659; ReceiptRef types.proto:354). Backend persistence SHIPPED as a Go store method only — receipt/store.go:46 AppendArgs, :79 func Append — whose own header (store.go:18-20) states "Daemon receipts mirror here via async POST from the heartbeat sidecar (Phase 9 wire). This package only owns the persistence surface; mirror plumbing is separate." No HTTP route /internal/receipt/append is mounted (grep across backend found none). CLI wire path MISSING — grep for "internal/receipt" across the entire EasyNet-Cli repo returns zero hits; no push_receipt/receipt-mirror module; the daemon sidecar (easynet-daemon.rs:271-291) only pushes federation.heartbeat. hosted_receipt.rs is a staging shape/builder, explicitly "Not a verifier… carries the bytes around." Feature is described in backend comments but the dedicated CLI push module does not exist.
- **行动：** new — Author the CLI-side receipt-mirror module under the daemon sidecar: on terminal receipt, async-POST the canonical Axon InvocationReceipt to a hub receipt-append endpoint with bounded retry/backoff + dedup, degrading to receipt_store.rs ring on failure (verify: kill backend mid-run, confirm daemon stays up and queue drains on reconnect; verify self_hash round-trips byte-identical to Go Append). Backend side (separate, EasyNet repo): mount the /internal/receipt/append HTTP handler over the existing store.Append so the "Phase 9 wire" the comments assume actually has a landing route. Frame F-03 (surface the gap: a described-but-unimplemented wire path the backend already assumes exists). No RFC-003 sec0 note required — CLI runtime + EasyNet backend plumbing, NOT Axon-repo work; the receipt contract in Axon is already correct and must stay untouched.

Axon should keep this strictly a read-only projection of payload kind (no decryption, no key material) so it remains a neutral, safely-shareable audit contract.

EasyNet: Go pb types.pb.go:3170 and TS EasyNet/Frontend/src/lib/api/easynet-abilities.ts:320-332,397, both from the same proto. CLI: consumes Axon types, no fork found.

RFC-003 sec0 note: any such Axon-repo edit is a protocol-contract change and must go through the Axon RFC sec0 contract-change gate before landing, with Go/TS regenerated in lockstep.

### 60. ✅ Sealed / MultiSealed ledger payloads (per-recipient encryption)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (Axon). By the boundary criterion: two different-language nodes must AGREE on the seal/open shape and algorithm to interoperate — caller's device seals, callee's device opens. The algorithm string "x25519-chacha20poly1305-sha256-aad-v1", the SealedLedgerEventPayload field layout, and the MultiSealed recipient set are a cross-language CONTRACT, not device runtime. It is landed identically in the canonical proto (core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:473-502) and the Rust SDK (sdk/rust/src/invocation/ledger.rs:136-326). No CLI fork exists — verified CLI only calls LedgerEventPayload::digest(...) and never reimplements seal/open. - **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — directly serves the endgame "auditable by easynet-verify" + "user owns audit": the ledger event payload is sealed so only the caller/callee keypairs (MultiSealed default for private invocation) can open it, while non-participants still see a verifiable digest. This is exactly "user owns identity+state+audit" — confidentiality of the audit trail without sacrificing verifiability.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI imports the contract and is the EXECUTOR/RUNTIME that supplies plaintext + AAD + recipient pubkeys: the daemon holding the user identity key calls Axon's sealed_for_recipients(...) when recording a private invocation, and calls open_for_recipient(...) with the local payload keypair to surface decrypted args/result to a human or agent. CLI is also where the recipient-pubkey lookup (caller+callee URA -> encryption pubkey) is resolved. Today CLI only emits Digest — see daemon_invocation_service.rs:5748 and invocation_history_ability.rs:772/776/805 — so the sealing path is unwired on the runtime side.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD provide (and does) the neutral confidentiality contract: LedgerEventPayloadKind {Digest, Sealed, MultiSealed}, SealedLedgerEventPayload shape, the algorithm string, and seal/open_for_recipient/sealed_for_recipients/visibility_summary functions, kept in lockstep across proto and every language SDK. Gap: the Python SDK has NO sealed-ledger surface (grep found zero hits), so the cross-language contract is currently Rust+proto only — incomplete as a "library you import in any language".
- **现状：** partial. Contract shipped in Axon proto (core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:473-502; mirrored core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:473-501) and Rust SDK (EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/invocation/ledger.rs:136-326). NOT yet landed in Axon Python SDK (no seal/open in *.py). NOT yet exercised by CLI runtime — CLI emits only Digest (daemon_invocation_service.rs:5748, invocation_history_ability.rs:772). No fork in CLI.
- **行动：** Two-track, both downstream of the existing-correct Axon contract. (1) F-07 ontology-absorption / RFC-003 sec0 (Axon-repo work): port SealedLedgerEventPayload + seal/open + MultiSealed to the Axon Python SDK so the confidentiality contract is genuinely cross-language, matching the Rust algorithm string byte-for-byte (add round-trip cross-SDK test). (2) F-05 cleanup (CLI runtime): wire daemon_invocation_service.rs to call sealed_for_recipients for private invocations (resolve caller+callee encryption pubkeys, MultiSealed default) and open_for_recipient on history surfacing, replacing the Digest-only path. Do NOT add any sealing crypto to CLI — it must import Axon. No re-litigation needed; this is execution of an already-ratified Axon contract.
