## 权限与治理 / Permission-Governance（16）

### 61. 🔴 identity.register_pubkey pairing trust-set write

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — the MECHANISM (atomic write to ~/.easynet/realm-trust.toml, realm-roaming policy, SharedTrustAnchor cell republish) is correctly CLI-local runtime/keyring and must NOT sink to Axon. But the WIRE SHAPE — ability-name literal "identity.register_pubkey" plus JSON arg tuple {agent_ura, public_key_b64, role} plus the {ok:true} receipt — is a cross-language contract: the Go backend caller and the Rust daemon must AGREE on it to interoperate, yet today they agree only via two hand-copied string constants (EasyNet/backend register_user_pubkey.go:57 abilityRegisterUserPubkey verbatim-tracks the CLI const, comment at register_device_pubkey.rs:73-85 admits the manual pin). No proto/message for this exists in EasyNet-Axon (only a CLI integration test references the name); contrast federation.resolve_key, the cross-realm READ sibling, which DOES have core/proto/axon/v1/federation.proto. So the arg/name contract is forked into CLI+backend with no neutral Axon owner.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — seeding the daemon's realm trust anchor with a device's signing key is the foundation of "user owns identity"; without a trust-set write the URA admission gate (§5.2) cannot admit signed envelopes, so addressable+governable+auditable invocation never starts. This is the pairing-time bootstrap of the owner-device identity plane.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the handler, the trust-write mechanism, the realm-roaming policy (device may register out-of-realm URI; backend/hub daemon-local), the atomic save + cell republish, and the gRPC dispatch wiring. This is RUNTIME/EXECUTOR — exactly how this device lands and orchestrates its own trust-set. CLI should consume the Axon-defined arg/name shape rather than own the literal.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Own neutral admission/signature contracts only. The trust-write mechanism and identity.register_pubkey handler stay in CLI; Axon must NOT contain any trust-anchor storage, file path, realm-roaming policy, or daemon admission policy.
- **现状：** partial/forked — shipped and working as a CLI ability (EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/register_device_pubkey.rs handler + daemon_invocation_service.rs:1011 dispatch) with the backend best-effort caller shipped (EasyNet/backend/internal/handler/device/verifyCredentialHandler.go:129 RegisterDevicePubkeyBestEffort; logic/auth/register_user_pubkey.go:57). The name+arg contract is forked: duplicated as hardcoded constants in CLI and Go with no Axon proto owner (confirmed: zero register/trust-write message in EasyNet-Axon core/proto).
- **行动：** Keep identity.register_pubkey as the canonical daemon ability and keep its mechanism in CLI. Any future typed message extraction must be contract-shape-only and must not move storage, policy, or admission runtime into Axon.

### 62. 🟡 Ability visibility tiers (Selfish/Device/Public) + discovery scope match

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (partial) — The Selfish and Device tiers plus allow/deny caller lists plus the live-AgentRegistry re-check are correctly CLI: they are how THIS device gates same-box dispatch, no cross-language agreement needed. BUT the Public tier carries cross-node semantics: two different-language nodes must AGREE on whether an ability is federation-reachable to interoperate. Axon already owns that axis as the URA visibility realm segment (pub|org|prv, semantic.rs:522) and consent governance (GrantConsent). The CLI Visibility::Public is a CLI-local stand-in ("treated like device until federation ships", ability_spec.rs:313-317) that does not bind to Axon's pub|org|prv realm — that is the seam where a fork can form. The enum itself is CLI-correct; only the Public→federation contract binding belongs in Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — directly serves "ability lives on owner device, governed, auditable": it decides who may see/invoke an ability on the owner device, which is exactly the device-local half of "governable". Selfish/Device are pure device-trust-boundary gates; the Public tier is the on-ramp to URA-addressed federation reach.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the device-local access engine: Selfish/Device tiers, AccessPolicy allow_callers/deny_callers, monotonic caller_scope tier check (allows_caller / allows_caller_name), and the live-AgentRegistry re-check on discover + a2a-bridge dispatch. This is RUNTIME/EXECUTOR gating and must stay CLI. CLI's Public tier should map to / derive from Axon's URA pub|org|prv realm rather than redefining federation reachability locally.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Own the cross-node reachability contract that the Public tier rides on: the URA visibility realm (pub|org|prv, already in client-sdk semantic.rs) and consent governance (GrantConsent/RevokeConsent in capability.proto). Axon should NOT grow a Selfish/Device discovery-policy concept — same-device peer gating is device-local and not a cross-language contract; sinking it would over-pollute the neutral protocol. Axon should expose enough that CLI's Public maps onto pub|org|prv instead of forking a parallel tier.
- **现状：** partial/forked-risk — CLI shipped: Visibility enum + tier()/as_wire_str at ability_spec.rs:303-416; AccessPolicy allow/deny + allows_caller at :320-391; discover scope match at discover_ability.rs:545-591; a2a-bridge live-registry re-check at a2a_bridge_ability.rs:118-158. Axon shipped a DIFFERENT axis: URA pub|org|prv realm (client-sdk semantic.rs:522) + GrantConsent/RevokeConsent (capability.proto:389-426). CapabilityDescriptor (types.proto:746-759) carries NO visibility/access field, so the two layers are not yet reconciled — CLI Public is a local placeholder.
- **行动：** Keep Selfish/Device/caller-list/live-registry logic in CLI as-is (F-05 cleanup: it is correct device-runtime gating). Open an F-07 ontology-absorption item to bind CLI Visibility::Public to Axon's URA pub|org|prv realm + consent model so the federation tier is not forked when the federation layer lands — do NOT push Selfish/Device into Axon. RFC-003 sec0 note: the Axon-side work is contract reconciliation (map CLI Public onto existing pub|org|prv realm / consent), not a new protocol surface; pin it to the existing URA visibility + GrantConsent definitions rather than adding a visibility field to CapabilityDescriptor.

### 63. 🔴 consent.* profile + abilities (request/subscribe/decide/grant/revoke/list)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The feature is two distinct shapes wrongly merged under one CLI namespace. (1) subscribe/decide/list_pending = how THIS device surfaces its pending-approval queue to a human/UI tail -> correctly CLI (RUNTIME/FRONT-DOOR), no cross-language agreement needed. (2) request (admission sub-invocation) + grant/revoke/list_grants (long-lived consent) = a contract two different-language nodes MUST agree on for a remote caller's admission gate to honor a grant. Axon already owns exactly this: capability.proto GrantConsentRequest/RevokeConsentRequest/ConsentView/ListConsentsRequest (lines 388-440) + rpc_consent.rs tenant-scoped handlers + admission_gate.rs. The CLI profile re-declaring device.consent.grant/revoke/list_grants/request as its own strings (consent.rs:11-16) is a protocol fork — a CLI-local copy of an Axon contract. CLI grep for GrantConsent/RevokeConsent = empty, so the two halves do not connect.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL. The human-in-the-loop loop (subscribe/decide/list_pending) directly serves the endgame: the owner approves what runs on their device, no side-channel (RFC A6), and decisions are auditable. But "user owns governance" only fully lands if the long-lived grant decision is recorded in the SAME governable contract a remote caller's admission gate checks. Today the durable grant/revoke/list_grants half is declared in CLI but not wired and not bound to Axon's grant store, so the governance is not yet end-to-end.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep and finish the RUNTIME/FRONT-DOOR half only: consent.subscribe (InvokeStream UI tail), consent.decide (deliver decision), consent.list_pending (snapshot) — these are the device-local approval-broker surface and belong here. For the durable half, CLI should NOT own a grant namespace; it should EXECUTE against Axon: on a decide-that-grants, call Axon GrantConsent; list_grants should proxy Axon ListConsents; revoke should call Axon RevokeConsent. device.consent.request stays as the kernel admission sub-invocation that consults the Axon grant store, not a CLI-private grant table.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already provides the consent contract shape and should remain its sole owner: GrantConsentRequest/Response, RevokeConsentRequest/Response, ConsentView, ListConsents{Request,Response} in capability.proto, the rpc_consent.rs lifecycle handlers (tenant-scoped, bounded TTL, never-permanent expiry), and the admission_gate.rs consult point. No new contract needed — Axon just needs CLI to bind to it instead of forking device.consent.grant/* strings. The neutral cross-language guarantee (a Python node and the Rust daemon agree on what a consent grant is) lives here.
- **现状：** forked/partial. SHIPPED in CLI: subscribe/decide/list_pending real handlers at EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/agents/permission_ability.rs:40-160, registered+described in agents/mod.rs:1176-1178,1335-1337. DECLARED-ONLY in CLI (no handler, no Axon call): device.consent.request/grant/revoke/list_grants — named in profiles/consent.rs:11-16 and owns()/tests, but grep for register/handler of those four = empty and grep for GrantConsent/RevokeConsent in CLI = empty. SHIPPED in Axon (the real contract): capability.proto:388-440 messages + rpc_consent.rs grant/revoke/list handlers + admission_gate.rs. The two are not connected = fork.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup + RFC-003 sec0 note (touches Axon contract usage). Step 1: in CLI, drop device.consent.grant/revoke/list_grants/request from the owned-namespace declaration in profiles/consent.rs (they are an Axon contract, not a CLI namespace) — keep only subscribe/decide/list_pending as CLI-owned, and rename the legacy permission_ability.rs to consent_ability.rs per the file's own P2.2 TODO. Step 2: wire decide/list_grants/revoke to invoke Axon's existing GrantConsent/ListConsents/RevokeConsent RPCs (executor binding, not a new namespace), and make device.consent.request consult the Axon grant store via admission_gate rather than a CLI-private table. RFC-003 sec0: no proto change required — Axon's consent contract is already shipped; this is CLI binding to it, so log it as "CLI consumes existing capability.proto consent RPCs; no Axon contract delta." Do NOT add grant/revoke shapes to Axon (already there) and do NOT keep them as CLI strings (fork).

### 64. 🟡 Device-path receipt-on-admit annotation (unsigned_caller_ura_admitted)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (as a contract value) but currently DEAD in CLI. The shape "a receipt carries a human-readable admit/failure reason on the admitted lifecycle state" is exactly what two different-language nodes must agree on to audit interoperably — and it already exists in Axon: invoke.proto InvocationReceipt has `string reason = 11` (line 682) plus lifecycle states "admitted/running/terminal" (line 667). The literal token `unsigned_caller_ura_admitted` is a CLI-local reason VALUE; an enumerated, cross-language-agreed admit-reason vocabulary belongs in Axon, while emitting it for this device runtime is correctly CLI. As written today it is neither: it is a stale comment describing a value that was never persisted after SharedReceiptStore removal (Phase 5a). So functionally: dead code masquerading as a CLI feature; the contract surface it should use is already in Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the underlying intent (audit sees every admit, including the unsigned transitional Device path) directly serves the auditable-by-easynet-verify endgame. But this *specific* annotation serves nothing today: the Device arm returns bare Ok(()) (admission_facade.rs:471) and writes no receipt, so the audit pipeline does NOT see the unsigned admit. The value lives entirely in the future PR-8 strict flip, at which point the annotation is explicitly stated to die. Net: an audit-value idea whose current incarnation is dead weight.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI (EXECUTOR/RUNTIME) is the right place to DECIDE the Device arm admitted via URI-only and to STAMP that reason onto the receipt it emits at terminal time via the LedgerSink/InvocationLedger path (admission_facade.rs:466-470 already names this as the replacement for SharedReceiptStore). What CLI should NOT do is keep an orphan comment-only reason token that is never written. Until PR-8 flips Device to strict, CLI should either (a) actually emit a receipt with reason set on the Device URI-only admit so audit is honest, or (b) delete the dead annotation comment block (lines 456-461) since it documents behavior that no longer exists.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already provides the contract: InvocationReceipt.reason (invoke.proto:682) and the admitted lifecycle state (invoke.proto:667). The only thing Axon SHOULD additionally own, if cross-realm auditors must agree on what "unsigned URI admit" means, is a stable enumerated reason vocabulary (or documented well-known reason strings) so a peer hub's easynet-verify reads the same meaning the CLI wrote. Axon must NOT absorb the Device-vs-strict admission policy itself — that is CLI runtime per DEC-013/INV-3.
- **现状：** missing/dead — cited at EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/admission_facade.rs:456-472: the `reason` annotation `"unsigned_caller_ura_admitted"` exists ONLY in the comment (the sole repo hit, confirmed by grep across CLI+Axon); the Device arm body returns Ok(()) and writes no receipt because SharedReceiptStore was removed in Phase 5a (admission_facade.rs:466-470, tombstone at daemon_invocation_service.rs:5506). Axon side: contract shape present (invoke.proto:659-682) but no admit-reason enum.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup: delete the stale annotation comment block at admission_facade.rs:456-461 (it documents a SharedReceiptStore-era behavior that no longer exists) and keep the Phase-5a comment at 466-470 that states the real replacement. Do NOT add a CLI-local reason token. When PR-8 flips Device to strict (admission_facade.rs:397, the single-source-line flip), this arm collapses into run_strict_admission and the question is moot. Separately, if pre-PR-8 audit honesty for unsigned Device admits is actually required, raise it as F-07 ontology-absorption against Axon: define a well-known/enumerated admit-reason value on InvocationReceipt.reason (invoke.proto:682) so CLI's LedgerSink can stamp it and cross-realm easynet-verify reads it identically — RFC-003 sec0 note: this is an Axon-repo proto/contract change, route through the proto-contract review gate, not a CLI-local patch.

### 65. 🔴 FederatedKeyResolver (cross-realm pubkey via federation.resolve_key)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — the RESOLVER mechanism (local-first, TTL cache, async-bridge dial, operator federated_peers gate, fail-closed) is correctly CLI RUNTIME. But the cross-node CONTRACT it speaks, the federation.resolve_key request/response wire shape, is forked: CLI's peer-side handler federation_wrappers::ResolveKeyResponse and FederatedKeyResolver's parser both use public_key_b64 (admission_facade dial parses parsed["public_key_b64"]), while Axon's canonical federation.resolve_key handler hub_profile::hub_resolve_key AND the CLI's own RFC-002 client federation_client::ResolveKeyReceipt both return public_key_hex. Two nodes that must agree to interoperate cross-realm disagree on field name + encoding, so a CLI daemon dialing a real Axon hub fails to parse the key. The shape that must be agreed cross-language is a contract and belongs in Axon; the CLI copy has drifted.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — cross-realm signed admission is exactly what lets a URA owned on one owner-device be verified/governed when it lands at a foreign hub; without resolving a peer realm's pubkey, federated invocations cannot be admitted, audited, or returned to the caller. It directly serves "ability addressed by URA, governed by Axon, auditable" across realm boundaries.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the FederatedKeyResolver itself in CLI — it is RUNTIME executor behavior: local-first short-circuit, the SharedFederatedKeyCache TTL, the block_in_place sync→async dial bridge, operator-explicit [daemon.federated_peers] gating, and INV-4 fail-closed mapping to unknown_agent_ura. CLI should import the canonical resolve_key request/response type from the Axon SDK and decode against it (consume the contract), rather than defining its own ResolveKeyResponse/parsing.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already owns the right half: the KeyResolver trait and AdmissionMode::Federated (client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:84,91) and the federation.resolve_key ability (hub_profile hub_resolve_key returning public_key_hex). Axon SHOULD additionally publish the resolve_key request/response shape as a SDK-exported neutral type (one encoding, one field name) so every language node decodes identically. It should NOT absorb the TTL cache, the federated_peers operator map, or the dial-bridge — those are device runtime, not contract.
- **现状：** forked — resolver shipped in CLI at src/services/invocation_transport/federated_key_resolver.rs (full impl + tests) and wired in src/services/invocation_transport/admission_facade.rs:310-332 (with_federation), 504-563 (run_strict_admission builds it), 596-622 (is_federated_caller). Wire-shape fork: CLI parses public_key_b64 (federated_key_resolver.rs:394-397; federation_wrappers.rs:684-690) vs Axon hub returns public_key_hex (hub_profile.rs:476 + body) and CLI's own client expects public_key_hex (federation_client.rs:80-90).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: reconcile the federation.resolve_key wire contract in Axon (pick one encoding — Axon ships public_key_hex today, so either Axon adds public_key_b64 or CLI switches its handler+resolver+wrappers to hex), export the resolve_key request/response as an Axon SDK type, then have CLI import and decode against it; delete the divergent CLI-local copy. RFC-003 sec0 note: Axon-repo change touches the federation contract surface — record it in RFC-003 sec0 as a contract reconciliation (resolve_key shape), not a transport change; do not alter the Receipt/two-RPC surface (DEC-F01-Q9). Leave the resolver mechanism, cache, and federated_peers gate in CLI.

### 66. 🟡 Loopback self-trust bypass (&lt;self&gt;.* admission)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (the DECISION RULE), with a CLI runtime residue. Apply the criterion: the rule "an envelope whose caller.ura equals the node's own URI is admitted without crypto/replay" is an admission-pipeline semantic. Axon already owns the admission contract (run_admission with exactly two ratified modes: LocalFast structure-only and Federated full-verify, EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:625, AdmissionMode at :76-83). Self-trust is a THIRD admission outcome that lives only in the CLI (admission_facade.rs:406-409) and is invisible to any other-language node and to easynet-verify. Two nodes that must agree on whether a self-addressed envelope is trusted (e.g. a Go hub forwarding, a Python SDK node receiving a self-claimed URI) have no shared definition — that is a protocol fork of the admission state machine into CLI. Whether to ACT on the rule for a given inbound socket (the transport-origin proof) is correctly CLI-runtime; the rule's SHAPE and its receipt consequence are contract and belong in Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — Self-origin abilities (the owner device invoking its own &lt;self&gt;.* and admin RPCs) must be cheap and not require the daemon to sign requests to itself; that "the ability lives on the owner device" endgame genuinely needs a local-origin fast path. But the value is served only insofar as "self" is actually proven to be self. As shipped the bypass proves nothing (string match on a claimed caller.ura), which undercuts the "auditable by easynet-verify / governed" half of the endgame: a self-originated admin call leaves no signed receipt and no crypto evidence it was really the owner.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the RUNTIME half: prove self-origin from the TRANSPORT, not from a claimed string. The UDS listener (boot.rs:793, mode 0600) is the real self/local channel; a self-trust admit should be conditioned on "arrived on the owner-only local socket" (or a per-boot process credential), then mapped onto Axon's self-origin mode. CLI must NOT honor caller.ura==daemon_uri for requests arriving on the TCP-TLS listener (boot.rs:606-607) — today is_loopback (admission_facade.rs:589-594) is transport-blind, so a TCP caller forging the daemon's own URI takes the Ok(()) bypass. CLI also owns emitting the receipt for self-originated calls so the audit pipeline (LedgerSink) records admin RPCs.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should name self-origin admission as a first-class AdmissionMode alongside LocalFast/Federated (e.g. AdmissionMode::SelfOrigin), define what evidence makes "self" valid (transport-attested local origin, not a string equality the caller controls), and define the receipt/reason annotation it produces so easynet-verify can audit a self-admitted admin call. This is the neutral cross-language statement of "a node may trust itself, and here is what that means on the wire and in the ledger" — without it every language binding reinvents (or forks) the rule. It must stay a decision-rule contract, not runtime: Axon defines the shape, the CLI supplies the transport proof.
- **现状：** forked. The self-trust rule exists only in CLI as Invariant 2 (admission_facade.rs:81-85, 406-409, 589-594), with no counterpart in Axon's admission contract (EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs has only LocalFast + Federated; grep for loopback/self_uri/self-trust in Axon proto + admission.rs returns nothing). The check is URI-string-bound, not transport-bound, and the daemon serves both UDS and optional TCP-TLS — so the bypass is reachable off-box if the TCP listener is enabled and the caller claims the daemon's URI.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: lift the self-trust admission outcome into Axon as a named AdmissionMode::SelfOrigin (mode + reason string + receipt consequence), mirroring F-01 sec7's pattern of Cli-runs-ahead / Axon-absorbs-the-rule-back. Step 1 (Axon repo, RFC-003 sec0: this touches the ratified two-mode admission surface from DEC-F01-Q9 — open a sec0 note before editing admission.rs, do not silently add a third mode): add SelfOrigin to AdmissionMode with its evidence contract and audit annotation. Step 2 (CLI, F-05 cleanup): rebind is_loopback to transport-origin (admit only on the UDS path, reject self-URI claims arriving over listen_tcp) and route the admit through the new Axon mode so a receipt is emitted for self-originated admin RPCs. Net: CLI keeps the runtime origin-proof; Axon owns the rule's shape and its auditability.

### 67. 🟡 FederatedKeyResolver presented-pubkey pin (DEC-EU multi-device user admission)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (partially) — the runtime MECHANISM (which device's resolver answers, reading the hint, the local realm-trust.toml lookup) is correctly CLI, but the SEMANTIC RULE "a User URA is 1:N pubkeys and verify MUST pin to the envelope-presented key" is a cross-language contract: a Python/Go node admitting a user envelope must agree on the same rule or it forks admission. Today Axon's KeyResolver trait (sdk/rust/src/invocation/axiom.rs:357-360) is 1:1 (fn resolve(agent_ura)->one key) and the 1:N user model exists nowhere in Axon. The CLI is NOT itself a wire fork (it implements the SDK trait and reuses the existing proto key_id_hint field per its documented "verifiers MUST resolve independently" semantics), but the contract shape it depends on is MISSING from Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — multi-device user admission is exactly the endgame's "user owns identity": a person signs from any of N devices under one User URA and the owner-device admission gate must verify against the exact key that signed, not a lex-smallest guess. Without the pin, every non-lex-smallest device silently fails verify, breaking governable/auditable invocation for real users.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the presented-pubkey pin as the CLI-local IMPLEMENTATION of the Axon resolver contract: read presented pubkey from caller_signature.key_id_hint, confirm it is registered under the caller URI in realm-trust.toml, return that exact VerifyingKey. This is admission RUNTIME on the owner device and belongs in CLI. It should implement whatever multi-key resolver contract Axon defines (below), rather than encoding the 1:N rule as a CLI private invention.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should own the 1:N user-admission CONTRACT so all language nodes agree: (a) document in the SDK/RFC that a User-kind URA may carry N verifying keys and that admission MUST pin verify to the envelope-presented key, never lex-guess; (b) extend the KeyResolver contract to a key-disambiguating shape (e.g. resolve(agent_ura, presented_key_hint) or a TryResolveExact) so the 1:N rule is expressible cross-language, not re-derived per implementation; (c) restate key_id_hint's intended use for user multi-device pinning in types.proto (line 384) / RFC-001 so easynet-verify (which today is 1:1) applies the same pin offline.
- **现状：** partial — CLI side SHIPPED and deforked: the same-realm presented-pubkey pin now lives inside FederatedKeyResolver's local branch, with lookup_user_by_pubkey owned by realm_trust_anchor.rs; the deleted pinned_user_key_resolver.rs no longer carries a second resolver stack. Axon side MISSING: SDK KeyResolver trait is 1:1 (axiom.rs:357-360), no 1:N user model in proto/SDK/easynet-verify; key_id_hint documented only as generic non-trustworthy hint (types.proto:384-385), not as the user multi-device pin.
- **行动：** Open an Axon RFC-003 sec0 item (Axon-repo work) to ratify the 1:N User-URA admission contract: (1) add the multi-key/exact-key resolver shape to the SDK KeyResolver trait and restate key_id_hint semantics in types.proto + RFC-001; (2) teach easynet-verify the same pin so offline audit matches daemon admission. Then F-07 ontology-absorption: once Axon lands the contract, refactor the FederatedKeyResolver presented-pubkey branch to implement the new Axon resolver shape instead of carrying the 1:N rule as CLI-private logic. Keep the resolver itself in CLI (runtime/admission). Note F-01 sec7 precedent: CLI ran ahead correctly here; Axon now absorbs the rule back as neutral contract.

### 68. 🔴 policy.* profile + abilities (evaluate/simulate/publish/override/get_decision)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (contract+runtime split across two repos, partially inconsistent). The SHAPE is correctly contract: Axon owns it at core/proto/axon/v1/policy.proto (PolicyDecision, Evaluate/Simulate/CRUD/Override/GetDecision) and RFC-001 §2 collapses all 9 RPCs to policy.* abilities — two different-language nodes must AGREE on decision/audit shape to interoperate, so the shape belongs in Axon (correct). The CLI policy_ability.rs is the RUNTIME executor of that contract, which is the right CLI role. The fork is twofold: (a) Axon's policy.proto PolicyDecision = {allowed, decision_id, deny_reasons[], constraints{}} but RFC-001 §2 mapping says policy.evaluate receipt body = {decision, reason, expires_at} and CLI returns exactly the RFC shape — proto and RFC restatement disagree on the receipt body, an Axon-internal inconsistency the CLI inherits; (b) CLI does not import the Axon policy types — the wire shape is hand-rolled JSON in the executor, not bound to a generated Axon contract type, so drift is unchecked.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — governance is one of Axon's three pillars (addressable/decidable/governable). policy.evaluate is the admission gate that decides whether a URA invocation is admitted, and policy.get_decision makes every decision auditable by easynet-verify. Directly serves the endgame where ability invocations are governed and auditable.
- **CLI 预期实现：** RUNTIME/EXECUTOR role: register policy.* as device-owned ability handlers and actually run the evaluator (config-driven rule engine + persisted policy store + override store + decision-record store for get_decision), and wire policy.evaluate into the kernel admission gate hot path with the kernel-local admission_internal exemption. CLI produces decision receipts whose shape conforms to the Axon contract; it must not define the decision shape itself.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Owns the SHAPE: PolicyDecision message + the policy.* ability signatures (evaluate/simulate/publish/list/get/create_override/revoke_override/get_decision) as the neutral cross-language contract, so a Go hub or Python auditor reads the same decision/audit shape a Rust CLI emits. Must resolve the proto-vs-RFC receipt-body inconsistency (PolicyDecision {allowed,decision_id,deny_reasons,constraints} vs RFC §2 {decision,reason,expires_at}) into one ratified shape, and surface a generated type the CLI binds to.
- **现状：** partial + forked. Axon contract shipped: EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/policy.proto:47-209 (full 9-ability surface), RFC-001 restatement-mapping §2 (policy.proto 9/9 mapped). CLI partial: EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/agents/policy_ability.rs only registers evaluate+simulate as v1 allow-all stubs (lines 65-110); profiles/policy.rs:10-15 declares publish/list/get/create_override/revoke_override/get_decision as owned but they are NOT registered (mod.rs:507 registers only the two; descriptions/schemas at mod.rs:1172-1173,1331-1332 cover only the two). admission_internal is comment-only (policy_ability.rs:8,38; profiles/policy.rs:5) — the gate still calls PermissionService directly (file note lines 9-15), so even evaluate is not on the hot path. CLI hand-rolls the JSON shape; no import of Axon policy pb types (only src/eal/error.rs:277 uses Axon::PolicyDenied).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: do NOT re-implement the contract in CLI. (1) RFC-003 sec0 note — this is Axon-repo work: ratify ONE policy decision/receipt shape in EasyNet-Axon (reconcile policy.proto PolicyDecision against RFC-001 §2 receipt-body mapping; the proto and the restatement currently disagree) and expose it as a generated type. (2) In CLI, bind policy_ability.rs handlers to that absorbed Axon type instead of ad-hoc JSON so the wire shape can't drift, then land the four MISSING admin/auditor abilities (publish/list/get + create_override/revoke_override + get_decision) over a persisted policy/override/decision store. (3) Separately (own milestone per the file note) wire policy.evaluate into the kernel admission gate with the kernel-local admission_internal exemption. Sequence Axon-shape-ratification BEFORE CLI binding to avoid landing a second fork.

Be the single source of truth; any new admission reason is added here first. The "MUST match byte-for-byte" invariant comment is the contract guarantee.

### 70. ✅ Cross-realm user roaming key resolution (multi-realm resolve)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (belongs in CLI; genuine MISSING piece within CLI). Boundary test: the cross-language wire CONTRACT already exists and agrees — ResolveKeyRequest carries presented_pubkey_b64 (federation_wrappers.rs:661-677) and the peer handler honors it (handle_resolve_key:702-724). Axon's neutral KeyResolver trait (axiom.rs:357-360) correctly takes only agent_ura; User-role 1:N pinning is a runtime/executor decision, not protocol. So this is NOT a protocol fork and NOT over-sink. The defect is purely how THIS hub-mode daemon dials/resolves — a CLI runtime gap: federated_key_resolver.rs:359 builds args with only agent_ura and never threads the envelope-presented pubkey through, so the dialer is structurally unable to forward it.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — owner-identity-anywhere is the endgame: a User must be able to sign from any federated realm and be verified at a remote hub. Without forwarding the presented pubkey on the cross-hub dial, a roaming User's signature fails verify (peer falls to lex-smallest 1:N lookup), which directly blocks "user owns identity, addressed by URA, governed/auditable anywhere."
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI must thread the envelope-observed presented_pubkey_b64 into the cross-hub federation.resolve_key dial for User-role callers. Concretely: AdmissionFacade extracts envelope_presented_pubkey_b64 and constructs FederatedKeyResolver with that observed key; the resolver's User-aware path carries the pubkey down into resolve_federated and serializes it into the ResolveKeyRequest args. Because Axon's KeyResolver::resolve(&str) cannot pass the pubkey, CLI captures it at resolver-construction time (constructor field), not via the trait arg.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the wire contract — it already exists and is sufficient: KeyResolver::resolve(agent_ura) stays single-arg (axiom.rs:357-360); the presented-pubkey field lives in the CLI-owned ResolveKeyRequest, not in Axon. No Axon change is required to close this gap. (Only caveat: if multi-realm User pinning later proves to need a cross-language SDK helper, that would be a separate F-07 ontology-absorption question — not in scope here.)
- **现状：** missing (within CLI). Receiving half shipped: federation_wrappers.rs:661-724 (ResolveKeyRequest.presented_pubkey_b64 + handle_resolve_key honoring it). Sending/resolving half missing: federated_key_resolver.rs:359 dials with only {"agent_ura"}, and the resolver has no field to carry the presented pubkey. Known-followup comment at admission_facade.rs:529-536 confirms this is the tracked gap (DEC-EU §multi-realm-resolve).
- **行动：** new — patch FederatedKeyResolver (federated_key_resolver.rs) to accept an optional presented_pubkey_b64 (resolver field set per-admission-call from envelope_presented_pubkey_b64 in admission_facade.rs:537-563 for User-role callers), and include it in the resolve_federated args at line 359; add a User-role cross-realm resolve test mirroring the existing cross_realm_with_federated_peers_entry_resolves_via_dial test. No RFC-003 sec0 note needed: this is CLI-repo runtime work, no Axon-repo change. Frame tag: F-03 surface (closes a runtime gap that surfaces owner-identity-anywhere).

### 71. ✅ Device-path strict-admission flip (PR-8 sign-on-send)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). The CONTRACT already lives in Axon and is not forked: Envelope.caller_signature / CallerSignature over canonical invocation bytes is defined in EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:374-376,414,445, and the signing math (canonical_invocation_bytes) is in client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:201, verified by RFC-001 §5.2 / easynet-verify. That is "how this device runs", squarely CLI. Pushing the signing decision into Axon would be over-sink; leaving the URI-only arm is a de-facto local trust policy that diverges from the cross-language strict pipeline.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame requires every URA to be decidable by signature (governed by Axon, auditable by easynet-verify). The Device arm is the last URI-only trust hole: an unsigned device caller is admitted on URI alone, so its calls are not independently verifiable. Closing it makes the Device path produce the same signed, ledger-anchored receipts as Backend/Hub/User, which is exactly the "owner identity + auditable" property.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Device runtime signs on send: load the device identity key into the long-lived daemon, compute canonical invocation bytes via the Axon client-sdk helper (canonical_invocation_bytes), and populate Envelope.caller_signature on the device-emitted envelopes (today src/runtime/invocation.rs:137,278 and dispatch_shim.rs:605 emit caller_signature: None). Then flip the Device arm in admission_facade.rs:462-473 to route unconditionally to run_strict_admission, deleting the URI-only no-op branch and retiring the "unsigned_caller_ura_admitted" receipt annotation. The envelope_carries_signature_material gate (admission_facade.rs:833) becomes moot for Device once all device envelopes are signed.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for new contract work — the shape already exists (Envelope.caller_signature, CallerSignature, canonical_invocation_bytes, RFC-001 §5.2 verify). Axon should only need to remain the imported source of the signing/verify primitive; no proto change, no new RPC.
- **现状：** partial/deferred. Strict pipeline shipped for Backend/Hub/User (admission_facade.rs:475-489 routes them to run_strict_admission). Device arm is the explicit temporary boundary: URI-only admission with no signature/replay work (admission_facade.rs:380-397,449-473), and the device runtime still emits unsigned envelopes (caller_signature: None at src/runtime/invocation.rs:137,278 and src/daemon/axon_bridge/dispatch_shim.rs:605; the kernel.rs hits at 600-611 etc. are test fixtures). One-line flip pending per DEC-013 disclosure.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup (collapse the last temporary admission arm to the strict pipeline). Next step in EasyNet-Cli: (1) wire the device identity key into the runtime and sign device-emitted envelopes using the Axon client-sdk canonical_invocation_bytes helper, populating caller_signature at invocation.rs:137,278 and dispatch_shim.rs:605; (2) flip admission_facade.rs:462-473 Device arm to call run_strict_admission unconditionally and remove the URI-only branch + "unsigned_caller_ura_admitted" annotation; (3) verify via easynet-verify that device receipts now verify. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 note: n/a — contract already landed in Axon; this is CLI runtime adoption only.

Optional follow-up: the reason constant is currently Rust-only (admission.rs:51); for true cross-language agreement, surface AXON_NONCE_REPLAY in the proto/enum or a language-neutral reason registry in core/proto so non-Rust runtimes import the same string rather than hardcoding it. That is Axon-repo work — RFC-003 sec0 note: file as an Axon reason-code-registry item, do not touch CLI. Frame: F-05 (verify-correct) with an F-07 ontology-absorption sub-note for the reason-code registry.

That dispatch is not a cross-language agreement — it is "how this daemon decides who to crypto-verify given its local trust set and a temporary device-can't-sign-yet reality." The receipt emission feeding easynet-verify closes the audit leg.

It must NOT contain its own signature/replay/canonical-bytes logic — only orchestrate Axon's. This is correctly what admission_facade.rs does.

CLI policy + dispatch: EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/admission_facade.rs:398-493 (run_full_admission) and 504-587 (run_strict_admission delegating to easynet_axon run_admission); role enum at EasyNet-Cli/src/services/realm_trust_anchor.rs:89. Contract in Axon: client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs:625 and runtime gate at core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/admission_gate.rs:291. EasyNet Go backend consumes it as the gate it must satisfy (backend/internal/daemon_grpc/*.go references), confirming it does not reimplement admission.

### 74. ✅ RealmTrustAnchor + TrustedAgentRole (Backend/Device/Hub/User)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI), with one contract-shaped seam to absorb. By the boundary criterion: the trust SET itself (authored at pairing, stored in /etc/easynet/realm-trust.toml, hot-reloaded, role-driven admission-path branching in admission_facade.rs) is "how THIS device decides who reaches it" = RUNTIME, correctly CLI. It is NOT a protocol fork: Axon already owns the neutral admission contract (KeyResolver caller_ura->VerifyingKey, 4-step pipeline, URAKind taxonomy which CLI re-exports at src/ura.rs:37), and RealmTrustAnchor implements that trait rather than reinventing it. The one seam worth flagging: the 4-value TrustedAgentRole enum and Invariant 1' (User multi-pubkey-per-URA, tagged DEC-EU / RFC-001 "identity != key") are ontology a peer hub may have to agree on cross-language — that classification is contract-shaped (Axon), while the storage/branching stays CLI. Axon's existing NodeRole (FULL/ORCHESTRATOR/WORKER) is orthogonal scheduling topology, not this.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the device-local "who do I trust to reach me" set that makes admission governable: it backs Axon's KeyResolver so a caller URA resolves to a pinned Ed25519 key, which is exactly the endgame's "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, governed by Axon" being enforced at the front door. The User role + multi-pubkey relaxation directly serves "user owns identity" (one identity URA, per-device non-exportable keys).
- **CLI 预期实现：** Own the device-local trust set end to end: realm-trust.toml loader (realm_trust_anchor.rs), pairing-time writer (register_device_pubkey / revoke / list), SIGHUP hot-reload cell (trust_anchor_cell.rs), role->URI canonicality enforcement at load (canonical_uri_for_role), and the KeyResolver impl that bridges it into Axon admission (trust_anchor_key_resolver.rs). Role-driven admission-path selection (Backend/Hub strict 4-step, Device URI-only-until-PR-8) is CLI runtime policy and stays here.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Already provides the neutral admission contract: KeyResolver (caller_ura -> VerifyingKey, RFC-001 §5.4), AdmissionMode {LocalFast, Federated}, the 4-step pipeline, and the URAKind taxonomy (Hub/Device/User/Agent) the canonicality check maps onto. Axon SHOULD additionally absorb the trust-role + identity-vs-key ontology as a neutral enum/doc (the Backend/Device/Hub/User classification and the "one identity URA MAY bind N pubkeys" rule from DEC-EU) so two different-language hubs agree on it — but NOT the toml storage, the admission-path branching, or pairing flow.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI). TrustedAgentRole/TrustedAgent/canonical_uri_for_role at EasyNet-Cli/src/services/realm_trust_anchor.rs:89-240; wired as Axon KeyResolver at src/services/trust_anchor_key_resolver.rs:26; consumed by admission gate at src/services/invocation_transport/admission_facade.rs. Axon side: admission contract shipped at core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/admission.rs (+go/node/python SDK mirrors) and URAKind at sdk/rust/src/ura.rs:64; but TrustedAgentRole ontology + User-multi-pubkey rule are MISSING from Axon (no grep hit in proto/sdk).

Frame: F-07 ontology-absorption (Cli ran ahead per F-01 sec7; Axon absorbs the role/identity-key ontology back). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo contract work — file under RFC-001 amendment (DEC-EU), not RFC-003 transport plane; coordinate so admission_facade.rs role branching is unaffected by the type move.

### 75. ✅ Strict 4-step §5.2 admission pipeline (run_admission)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (in Axon). This is the canonical case of the boundary criterion's first clause: two different-language nodes MUST agree on the admission verdict and on the exact rejection-reason strings to interoperate, so the SHAPE is contract and belongs in Axon. The pipeline is deliberately kept neutral — no URA-scheme parsing (deferred to a profile validator), no transport, no product UI, no daemon identity — so there is no product/runtime over-sink. CLI does NOT fork it: src/services/invocation_transport/admission_facade.rs:118,566 imports and calls easynet_axon::invocation::admission::run_admission rather than re-implementing the 4 steps, which is precisely the no-protocol-fork outcome.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — admission is the "governable + decidable" axis of the endgame: every signed invoke against an owner device is validated (envelope, signature structure, crypto-verified key, nonce non-replay) before it can touch state, and the canonical rejection reasons (AXON_AXIOM_ENVELOPE_INCOMPLETE / AXON_CALLER_SIGNATURE_INVALID / AXON_NONCE_REPLAY) are wire-stable so easynet-verify and audit tooling can grep them. Without it, "ability lives on owner device, governed by Axon, auditable" has no enforcement point.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Provide only the device-local plumbing around the imported pipeline: (a) the FederatedKeyResolver implementation that knows how THIS daemon resolves caller keys same-realm vs cross-realm; (b) the SharedNonceReplayStore wrapper (src/services/nonce_replay_store.rs) giving the &mut NonceReplayStore a daemon-shared, interior-mutable lifetime across concurrent invokes; (c) supplying now_ms() and mapping the AxonError verdict to a gRPC Status (axon_error_to_status at admission_facade.rs:585). It must NOT re-order or re-implement the four checks.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Own the pipeline SHAPE and the verdict contract: the four ordered checks (validate_envelope -> validate_signature_structure -> verify_signature(KeyResolver) -> NonceReplayStore::check_and_record), the malformed-before-replay-store ordering invariant, the canonical REASON_* constants as stable wire codes, the NonceReplayStore dedup-key tuple (caller.ura, ability, nonce) and window semantics, and the KeyResolver trait as the language-neutral key-resolution seam. It must NOT reach for transport, daemon identity, or product surface. (Confirmed: admission.rs:620-634 is exactly this and nothing more.)
- **现状：** shipped, and correctly layered. Axon: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/invocation/admission.rs:79-637 (validate_envelope, validate_signature_structure, verify_signature, run_admission, NonceReplayStore with time-wheel). CLI consumes it: EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/admission_facade.rs:118 (import) and :566 (run_admission call inside replay_store.with_inner), plus EasyNet-Cli/src/services/nonce_replay_store.rs (SharedNonceReplayStore wrapper). No CLI re-implementation / no fork found.
- **行动：** No move required — feature is in the right layer with CLI importing rather than forking. Frame F-07 ontology-absorption (confirm, not change): verify the Axon Rust pipeline reasons/ordering are mirrored as the contract baseline for any second-language SDK (e.g. Python Client.tenant.ability.call admission parity) so cross-language nodes provably agree on the same three REASON_* codes. Optional F-05 cleanup: docs/rfc/AXON-RFC-003 line 389 references "admission_gate::run_admission_gate" while the shipped symbol is admission::run_admission — reconcile the doc name to avoid a phantom-symbol drift. RFC-003 sec0 note (Axon-repo work): any edit to admission.rs reason strings or check ordering is a wire-contract change and must go through RFC-003 sec0 review since SDKs/CLIs/audit tooling grep these constants.

## 编排 EAL / Composition-EAL（23）

### 77. ⬜ AAL (Agent Assembly Language) agent-behavior layer

- **裁决：** `MISSING — but the dominant problem is a SHAPE conflict, not absence. Applying the boundary criterion: the AAL *semantic contract* (what self-operation means: acquire/forget/publish/compose) must be agreed cross-language, so it is CONTRACT and is already ratified in Axon (ONTOLOGY_AGENT_ABILITY.md §3.4 + §4) as reflexive invocations on meta-abilities, NOT a new layer. Any AAL *compiler/parser/IR* is how one device authors/runs agent behavior = CLI EXECUTOR/orchestration, a sibling of the EAL pipeline. The CLI doc (src/eal/mod.rs:7-9, README:201/219) MIS-FRAMES AAL as a compiler pass that "emits EAL programs" sitting in the EAL pipeline above Mission IR — this contradicts Axon §4 which places the agent-behaviour layer OUTSIDE EasyNet's protocol surface (Claude Code / Codex / future AAL runtime). The CLI facade (src/cli/mod.rs:33-40) gets it right: AAL→BCC, producer-side, separate runtime/sibling binary, bridged only by the gRPC ability endpoint. So today AAL is genuinely MISSING as code, and the EAL-pipeline framing is a latent over-sink risk: do not let AAL grow into the EAL compiler — keep its contract in Axon and any runtime as a CLI-side sibling.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL. The endgame value (owner identity+state+audit, ability addressed by URA, returns Surface, governed by Axon) is fully delivered by Axon contract + CLI runtime/executor + EAL orchestration. AAL is a convenience "how an agent decides what to invoke" layer on top; useful for authoring agent behavior (the case01-aris/aal/*.aal contracts), but per Axon ONTOLOGY §3.4/§4 it adds NO new protocol primitive — every AAL operation reduces to an existing invoke(). So it serves authoring ergonomics, not a missing endgame capability.
- **CLI 预期实现：** If/when AAL lands, CLI owns the RUNTIME/EXECUTOR side: an AAL-native agent runtime (the "BCC"/producer binary, likely a sibling of the EAL interpreter) that lets an agent exercise its meta-abilities and emit EAL source. It is NOT a new pass inside src/eal/ (lexer→parser→planner→ir→interpreter); it sits above and produces EAL source as input to that existing pipeline. The four AAL primitives must be implemented as ordinary CLI invocations of meta.acquire/forget/publish/compose, not as a bespoke protocol.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the cross-language SHAPE and already provides it: the meta-ability namespace (meta.acquire / meta.forget / meta.publish / meta.compose) as ordinary Abilities, plus the ONTOLOGY ruling (§3.4) that AAL is operation-closure not a new layer. Axon should expose these as typed meta-ability method stubs/builders in the SDK (so any-language node agrees), and must NOT grow an AAL compiler, parser, or IR — that would pollute the neutral contract. Axon-repo touch here is documentation/SDK-surface, not protocol redesign.
- **现状：** missing (no compiler/parser/IR). Only declarations + sample artifacts exist: src/eal/mod.rs:7-9 lists AAL as future; README.md:201,219 frame it as future agent-behavior layer emitting EAL; src/cli/mod.rs:33-40 describe AAL→BCC as a not-yet-present sibling runtime; gallery/case01-aris/aal/{aris_researcher.aal,reviewer_oracle.aal}+README.md hold contract-like .aal samples explicitly "not executable source yet." Axon side is shipped-as-contract: ONTOLOGY_AGENT_ABILITY.md §3.4/§4, plus meta.* mappings in AXON-RFC-001-restatement-mapping.md:228/233/242/476.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: reconcile the CLI doc with the ratified Axon ontology before any code. Concretely, fix src/eal/mod.rs:7-9 and README.md:219 so AAL is described as an agent-behaviour layer OUTSIDE the EAL compile pipeline that PRODUCES EAL source (per Axon ONTOLOGY §4), not as a compiler pass above Mission IR — align with the already-correct facade comment (src/cli/mod.rs:33-40). Do NOT build an AAL parser/IR inside src/eal/. No new Axon protocol work needed (contract already ratified in §3.4); if the Axon SDK lacks typed meta.* builders, that is a separate small Axon-repo task and per RFC-003 sec0 must be raised as a doc/SDK-surface note, not a protocol change. Defer any AAL runtime ("BCC" sibling binary) to a new frame when an agent-authoring need is real.

### 78. 🟡 Axon deployment orchestrator saga (publish→install→activate)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-CLI — Apply the criterion: do two different-language nodes have to AGREE on the saga to interoperate? No. They agree on the four proto RPCs (capability.proto:36 Publish→Sign→Rollout→Install→Activate→…). A Rust deployer and a Python deployer interoperate fine with totally different recovery/compensation strategies — neither observes the other's ordering. So the saga is NOT contract; it is "how this device orchestrates a lifecycle," i.e. CLI EXECUTOR/orchestration. Sinking it into the neutral SDK is over-sink, and it is duplicated identically across sdk/rust and sdk/python (orchestrator.rs:56 + orchestrator.py:169), which is the forked-by-duplication tell of an orchestration that leaked into the protocol library. The underlying phase RPCs stay correctly in Axon; only the saga is misplaced.`
- **当前所在：** axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the endgame needs an ability to land on an owner device (publish→install→activate) and be auditable; the PhaseReceipt/DeployTrace genuinely feeds easynet-verify-style audit. But the deploy SAGA itself is a convenience orchestration, not part of "user owns identity+state+audit, ability addressed by URA, returns a Surface." It serves operations, not the user-facing value loop directly.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI should own the deploy saga as a runtime/executor concern: a deploy orchestrator that sequences the four Axon proto RPCs (publish_capability / install_capability / activate_capability / uninstall_capability), applies the forward-recovery + cleanup_on_activate_failure compensation policy, and emits the PhaseReceipt trace into the device audit log. CLI's ability.deploy ability handler (src/runtime/agents/device_ops_ability.rs:242) is the natural home — today it is a manifest-validation stub that explicitly defers "the federation publish/install/activate saga lights up when the federation Invoke transport returns." That handler should call the Axon phase RPCs and run the saga locally.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should provide ONLY the neutral cross-language contract: the four lifecycle RPCs in capability.proto (Publish/Install/Activate/Uninstall) plus the PhaseReceipt / DeployTrace receipt SHAPE as a data contract (so any language's saga emits an audit-comparable trace). Axon should NOT ship deploy_ability_package as a sequencing+compensation saga in each language SDK — that is orchestration policy, not contract, and duplicating it across rust+python is a protocol-library leak.
- **现状：** forked — saga shipped in Axon twice with matching semantics: EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/presets/remote_control/orchestrator.rs:56 (deploy_ability_package, cleanup_on_activate_failure, DeployTrace) and EasyNet-Axon/sdk/python/easynet_axon/orchestrator.py:169 (same shape/flag). The phase RPCs are correctly contract: EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/capability.proto:36. CLI side is a stub: EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/deploy.rs:65 invokes ability.deploy, handler at src/runtime/agents/device_ops_ability.rs:242-294 only validates the manifest and defers the saga.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption (RFC-003 sec0: this is Axon-repo work — keep the phase RPCs + PhaseReceipt/DeployTrace receipt contract in Axon, do not touch the proto). Step 1: relocate the deploy SAGA out of Axon SDK presets into CLI's deploy executor — implement the publish→install→activate forward-recovery + compensating-uninstall sequencing inside EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/agents/device_ops_ability.rs deploy_ability_handler, calling the Axon proto RPCs via DendriteBridge and emitting PhaseReceipt traces to the device audit log. Step 2: in Axon, demote sdk/rust/.../orchestrator.rs:56 and sdk/python/.../orchestrator.py:169 to (at most) reference/example code, retaining only the PhaseReceipt/DeployTrace types as the neutral receipt contract so cross-language traces stay comparable. Net: contract (RPCs + receipt shape) in Axon; orchestration (the saga) in CLI; kills the rust/python fork.

### 79. 🟡 Cross-device EAL step receipts threaded into mission trace

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (for the carriage half) + MISSING (for the threading half). The receipt SHAPE that must cross devices is a contract: InvocationReceipt already exists in EasyNet-Axon invoke.proto:659 (RFC-001 §5.3, field 14 admission_receipt) — two different-language nodes already agree on it for direct invoke. But the federation forward path drops it: Axon hub_profile.rs:1585 ForwardInvokeReceiptBody carries only {ok,state_code,result_b64,error_*} with no receipt field, and the CLI mirror federation_client.rs:61 ForwardInvokeReceipt matches that bare shape exactly. So the signed receipt is forked-out-of-existence across the hub hop — a protocol gap that BOTH sides must agree to close, hence Axon. The "thread into mission trace" half is genuinely MISSING and is CLI orchestration (StepTrace has no receipt field). Putting receipt carriage only in a CLI-local struct would be a protocol fork; doing the trace threading in Axon would over-sink CLI orchestration into the neutral protocol.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame is "owner owns audit; every invocation returns a signed receipt auditable by easynet-verify." A cross-device EAL step that returns only a bare JSON Value breaks the audit chain at exactly the federation boundary, the one place a third party most needs the remote owner's signature. Threading the remote owner's signed InvocationReceipt into the mission trace is what makes a multi-device mission independently verifiable.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI (EXECUTOR): once forward_invoke returns the remote owner's signed InvocationReceipt, dispatch_remote_via_forward_invoke (interpreter.rs:372) must surface it alongside the Value; StepExecResult::Ok (interpreter.rs:933) and StepTrace (interpreter.rs:268) gain an optional remote_receipt / receipt_hash field so the Mission IR interpreter threads it into ExecutionTrace. CLI does NOT define the receipt schema — it only carries Axon's InvocationReceipt verbatim and records it. This is how this device orchestrates and audits a cross-device step.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon (CONTRACT): extend the federation.forward_invoke result contract so the remote owner's signed InvocationReceipt (invoke.proto:659, the InvokeResponse.admission_receipt field 14) is propagated through the hub hop. Concretely add a receipt-carrying field to ForwardInvokeReceiptBody (hub_profile.rs:1585) and the matching federation.proto forward result, so any-language caller (not just this CLI) receives the callee's self-signed/host-attested receipt and can verify it offline. This is the cross-language agreement; CLI's federation_client.rs:61 then deserializes it.
- **现状：** missing/partial-forked. The signed receipt contract exists for direct invoke (EasyNet-Axon invoke.proto:659 InvocationReceipt; InvokeResponse.admission_receipt field 14) and the CLI daemon emits admission receipts locally (src/services/invocation_transport/daemon_invocation_service.rs:3980). But across federation it is dropped: EasyNet-Axon hub_profile.rs:1585 ForwardInvokeReceiptBody and CLI src/runtime/federation_client.rs:61 ForwardInvokeReceipt both carry only ok/state_code/result_b64 — no receipt. EAL dispatch returns Value only (src/eal/interpreter.rs:529-531) and StepTrace (interpreter.rs:268) has no receipt field. So: forked-thin contract at the hub + missing capture in CLI.
- **行动：** Two-step, Axon-first. Step 1 (Axon repo, RFC-003 sec0: this is a contract change to the federation forward surface — open it under RFC-003 sec0 as an AXON-RFC amendment to forward_invoke, do not land a CLI-local receipt shim that forks the protocol): extend ForwardInvokeReceiptBody / federation.proto forward result to carry InvocationReceipt from the remote owner, regenerate bindings. Step 2 (CLI repo): widen federation_client.rs:61 ForwardInvokeReceipt to deserialize the new field, propagate it out of dispatch_remote_via_forward_invoke (interpreter.rs:372), and add an optional receipt/receipt_hash to StepExecResult::Ok and StepTrace so process_step_result threads it into ExecutionTrace for easynet-verify. Frame: F-07 ontology-absorption (CLI runs ahead with EAL device dispatch; Axon absorbs the receipt-carriage contract back into the neutral protocol per F-01 sec7 pattern).

### 80. 🔴 EAL step options: timeout / retries / on_failure / optional

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The disposition VOCABULARY — enum {Abort,Skip,Retry,Continue} + step fields {timeout_seconds,max_retries,on_failure,optional} — must be agreed by every node that runs a step of the mission, so it is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon. Axon already owns it: StepFailurePolicy (types.proto:883-888, ABORT_MISSION/SKIP/RETRY/CONTINUE/UNSPECIFIED) and MissionStep.{timeout_seconds,max_retries,on_failure,optional} (mission.proto:34-42). The CLI re-declares the SAME contract as IrFailurePolicy (ir.rs:285-293) + StepOptions (ast.rs:127-175) with NO conversion path to the proto (grep confirms zero StepFailurePolicy/MissionStep usage in src/eal) — a 1:1 parallel definition = protocol fork. NOTE the legitimate CLI residue: the analysis rule (optional∧Abort rejection) and the enforcement engine (exponential-backoff+jitter retry, phase-ordered optional scheduling, Skipped-vs-Failed dependency propagation in interpreter.rs) are genuine EXECUTOR behavior and correctly CLI — only the shared field/enum shape is misplaced.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL. Per-step failure disposition is real governance value: it makes a multi-node mission auditable (which step Failed vs Skipped vs Aborted) and lets owner-authored missions degrade gracefully — directly serves the auditable/governable endgame. But it only fully pays off when the disposition is honored by the SERVER orchestrator and recorded in the receipt/timeline cross-node; today it is enforced only inside the CLI-local "temporary" interpreter, so a different-language node dispatching the same mission would not agree on outcome classification.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the EXECUTOR role: the Mission IR interpreter that actually fires retries with backoff+jitter, defers optional steps to a later phase, classifies a step Skipped vs Failed, and propagates UpstreamSkipped/UpstreamFailed to dependents; plus the compile-time analyzer rule rejecting optional∧Abort. CLI should LOWER its IrStep into the Axon MissionStep proto (mapping IrFailurePolicy -> StepFailurePolicy) rather than carrying a private enum, so emit-ir and any future server-side dispatch share one shape.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon remains the single source of truth for the contract shape: StepFailurePolicy enum + MissionStep.{timeout_seconds,max_retries,on_failure,optional}, already present in mission.proto/types.proto. Axon must NOT absorb the CLI's interpreter/backoff/jitter/scheduling logic (that would over-sink runtime into the neutral protocol). It only needs to keep the field semantics (e.g. UNSPECIFIED==CONTINUE, optional means "failure doesn't affect mission outcome") documented as the authoritative definition the CLI conforms to.
- **现状：** forked. CLI: ast.rs:127-175 (StepOptions/FailurePolicy), ir.rs:111-118 + 285-293 (IrFailurePolicy), interpreter.rs:1135 (optional/on_failure dispatch), retry/backoff at interpreter.rs:15,121-129,284-300. Axon: core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:883-888 (StepFailurePolicy), mission.proto:28-61 (MissionStep fields). The two are byte-incompatible (lowercase Rust enum vs STEP_FAILURE_POLICY_* proto) with no codegen/conversion bridging them.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: do NOT add a third copy. Make the CLI conform to Axon's existing contract — lower IrStep to the axon.v1 MissionStep proto (the CLI already has a proto-gen/axon-pb feature in Cargo.toml), mapping IrFailurePolicy{Continue,Abort,Skip,Retry} -> StepFailurePolicy{CONTINUE,ABORT_MISSION,SKIP,RETRY} and the four StepOptions fields onto MissionStep, deleting the private enum once the conversion is the single emit path. Keep all interpreter/analyzer behavior in CLI. RFC-003 sec0: this touches no Axon-repo file (proto already correct) — the absorption is one-directional CLI->Axon-shape, so no Axon RFC sec0 entry is required; record it as a CLI conformance change citing AXON mission.proto/types.proto as the authority.

### 81. 🟡 Mission IR interpreter (parallel phase dispatch, retry backoff)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-CLI (engine) + FORKED (the IR data model). Boundary criterion: the rayon dispatch / backoff-sleep / lock-free SegQueue collection / cross-phase substitution is purely "how THIS device runs and orchestrates" — it is RUNTIME/EXECUTOR and correctly CLI; the thesis names "Mission IR interpreter" as a CLI executor responsibility and EAL/Mission as CLI orchestration language, not protocol, so pushing the engine into Axon would be over-sink. BUT the IR SHAPE the engine consumes (src/eal/ir.rs MissionIr/IrCall/IrFailurePolicy/max_parallel/input_refs/output_binding) is a near-mirror of Axon's existing core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto (MissionStep, StepFailurePolicy, MissionConstraints.max_parallel, parallel_safe) — two different-language nodes must agree on this to interoperate, so the shape is CONTRACT and the CLI-local divergent copy (extended with input_refs/output_binding) is a protocol fork. The engine stays CLI; only the data-model divergence is the leak.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the execution engine is what turns an EAL mission into real cross-device invocations (each step lowers to an Invoke through the daemon to a URA), which directly serves the endgame; but it also carries a forked copy of the mission IR shape that the endgame needs to be governable/auditable cross-language, and a client-local fork does not serve that.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the interpreter: rayon intra-phase parallel dispatch, exponential-backoff-with-jitter retry, lock-free result collection, cross-phase data substitution, StepDispatcher abstraction (Borrowed/AgentAware/Mock), and the Mission IR → per-step Invoke lowering. This is CLI's EXECUTOR role and stays CLI. CLI should consume the Axon-defined mission IR shape rather than maintaining a parallel Rust struct family.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Own the mission IR DATA MODEL as the neutral contract: a MissionControl v2 mission.proto carrying step DAG, phase/dependency ordering, StepFailurePolicy/retry policy fields, max_parallel/parallel_safe, AND the data-flow fields the CLI added (input_refs / output_binding) so the CLI fork can converge onto it. Axon should NOT own the execution engine semantics (rayon, sleep, SegQueue) — its existing server-side mission_dispatch.rs is the server-execution path; the cross-language obligation is the IR shape + failure-policy semantics, not the threadpool.
- **现状：** partial+forked. Engine shipped in CLI src/eal/interpreter.rs (execute_with_dispatcher at :789; model header :6-23 self-labels "temporary — target: MissionControl v2"). IR shape forked: src/eal/ir.rs (MissionIr/IrCall/IrFailurePolicy/PhaseRange, input_refs :107, output_binding :109, max_parallel :312) duplicates EasyNet-Axon core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto (MissionStep, parallel_safe :60, max_parallel :67, StepFailurePolicy). Two CLI docs conflict: cli-axon-interface-review-2026-05-30.md:83 calls the IR a deliberate fork pending MissionControl v2; axon-grpc-closure-confirmation-2026-05-30.md:30 marks Axon mission.proto as 0-RPC shell slated for DELETE→ability while CLI keeps its own EAL IR — i.e. no owner has decided who lands the canonical shape.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: resolve the doc conflict by ratifying which layer owns the mission IR shape, then have Axon absorb the CLI-proven IR ontology (input_refs/output_binding + phase/failure-policy) back into a MissionControl v2 mission.proto; CLI then regenerates from Axon and keeps the interpreter as a pure executor over the Axon-defined IR. Keep the rayon/backoff/SegQueue engine in CLI (F-05 only to delete the divergent local struct copy once v2 lands; do NOT delete the engine). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work — adding/evolving mission.proto is a protocol-surface change, so it must enter through an RFC-003 sec0 contract amendment (mirrors the F-01 sec7 pattern: Cli ran ahead, Axon absorbs the ontology back) rather than a silent CLI-side proto edit.

### 82. 🔴 Planner phase partitioning + cycle detection + static call bound

- **裁决：** `FORKED. The validity/cycle/bound checks (duplicate-id, undefined/self dependency ref, acyclicity, step/call cap) exist in BOTH repos: Axon core/runtime-rs/src/services/mission_validation.rs:80-211 (validate_mission_dag: dup-step, missing-dep, self-dep, Kahn cycle detection, max_steps bound) operating on the cross-language contract MissionStep.depends_on / MissionConstraints.max_parallel (mission.proto:28-72), and CLI src/eal/planner.rs:30-59,135-142 re-implementing the same family (DFS cycle, dup-binding, undefined-ref, 256 static-call cap) on the pre-wire EAL AST. Whether a mission DAG is acyclic and bounded is exactly the thing two different-language nodes must AGREE on to interoperate -> it is CONTRACT, Axon is the authority. CLI holding its own cap value (256, src/eal/ir.rs:324) and its own cycle algorithm is a soft protocol fork. The genuinely-CLI half is correct: phase partitioning into PhaseRange for the temporary client-side interpreter (planner.rs:106-130, consumed at interpreter.rs:826) and dependency INFERENCE from VarRef (ast.rs:11 "no manual depends_on in the language") are runtime/executor + orchestration-language compilation, which belong in CLI.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL. Bounded + acyclic + parallel-when-independent dispatch directly serves the decidable/governable endgame (a mission terminates, fans out safely, and is auditable). But only the EAL-specific half (inferring dependency edges from VarRef data flow, phase slicing for the local interpreter, loop-expansion call budget) actually advances EasyNet value; the dupl+undefined-ref+cycle+DAG-bound half re-derives a guarantee Axon already owns and ships, so it adds no new endgame value and risks divergence.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI SHOULD: (1) compile EAL AST -> Mission IR, inferring depends_on edges from VarRef data flow (the language ergonomic that lets authors skip declaring edges) -> EXECUTOR/orchestration-language; (2) phase-partition the IR and run parallel-when-independent dispatch in the client-side interpreter -> RUNTIME/EXECUTOR; (3) enforce the EAL-only loop-expansion budget (max_iters * body calls) which Axon's flat MissionStep DAG has no concept of. CLI should NOT hold the canonical cycle algorithm or the canonical DAG/step cap as its own truth; it should emit MissionStep.depends_on and let Axon's validate_mission_dag be the agreed authority (CLI may keep a fast local pre-check for author feedback, but framed as a mirror of the Axon contract, not an independent spec).
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD (and already does) own the contract shape: MissionStep.depends_on dependency edges, MissionConstraints.max_parallel finite-ceiling invariant (mission.proto:60-72), and the canonical validate_mission_dag (acyclicity via Kahn, dup/missing/self-dep, max_steps) at mission_validation.rs:92. This is the neutral cross-language guarantee any node imports. Axon does NOT need EAL phase ranges, EAL VarRef inference, or the loop-expansion call budget — those are CLI orchestration-language concerns and would over-sink the protocol.
- **现状：** forked. CLI shipped: src/eal/planner.rs:30-59 (one-pass analyze+plan, DFS cycle, dup-binding/undefined-ref), planner.rs:135-142 (256 cap enforcement), src/eal/ir.rs:316-326 (default_max_calls=256), consumed by interpreter.rs:826. Axon shipped the contract-side equivalent: core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto:28-72 (depends_on/parallel_safe/max_parallel) and core/runtime-rs/src/services/mission_validation.rs:80-211 (Kahn cycle detection + dependency validity + max_steps) plus mission_dispatch.rs topological dispatch. Same guarantee, two independent implementations, divergent cap units (CLI counts static calls incl. loop expansion; Axon caps step count).
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup: keep phase-partitioning + VarRef inference + loop-budget in CLI (correct), but reframe CLI's cycle/validity/DAG-cap as an explicit mirror of Axon's validate_mission_dag contract, not an independent spec. Concretely: document in src/eal/planner.rs that acyclicity + dependency validity + the DAG bound are Axon-authoritative (cite mission_validation.rs), and ensure the CLI emits MissionStep.depends_on so Axon re-validates at admission rather than trusting CLI's local copy. RFC-003 sec0 note: the only Axon-repo change implied is a doc/cross-ref tying CLI's local pre-check to the proto contract; do not add EAL phase/loop concepts into mission.proto (that would over-sink). No new algorithm needed in either repo — this is de-forking by declaring authority, F-07 ontology-absorption-adjacent (Cli ran ahead with the local interpreter; Axon already absorbed the DAG-validation ontology, so CLI should now defer to it).

### 83. ⬜ Signed receipt for mission/EAL output (easynet-verify auditable)

- **裁决：** `MISSING — and the missing piece is CLI EXECUTOR wiring, not protocol. The Receipt SHAPE already exists in Axon (InvocationReceipt, RFC 001 §5.3, with callee_signature field 25 and causal_binding field 24 that names predecessor invocations across the chain) and CLI already imports it (easynet_axon::invocation::{InvocationLedger, LedgerSink}). A mission's output is just another invocation that emits a terminal Receipt whose causal_binding references its child-step receipts — no new contract shape is required, so this is NOT an Axon gap. The gap is purely that run_mission_inproc / the EAL interpreter never route mission output through the existing ledger sink (interpreter.rs has zero references to ledger/kernel/LedgerSink). Defining a separate CLI-local mission-receipt format would be a protocol fork (WRONG); the fix is to land the mission output onto the Axon contract CLI already holds.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the endgame is "ability lives on owner device, returns a Surface, governed by Axon, auditable by easynet-verify." A mission is a composed ability; its observable output must be a signed Receipt in the InvocationLedger or it is an unauditable hole in the owner's audit trail. Today single invocations are auditable but mission output is not, so the audit guarantee is incomplete exactly at the composition layer.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI (EXECUTOR + RUNTIME) wires the mission path to the Axon ledger it already owns: run_mission_inproc / the EAL interpreter should, on a mission's observable output, produce a terminal InvocationReceipt signed by the device identity key (the same LedgerSink + signing path that daemon single-invoke dispatch uses in runtime/agents/mod.rs), with causal_binding referencing each child step's receipt, and append it to the InvocationLedger so invocation_history_ability and easynet-verify can read it. The local ExecutionTrace in the run dir can stay as a debug/orchestration artifact, but it is NOT the audit record.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for new shape — Axon already provides the complete contract: InvocationReceipt (callee_signature §25, prev_receipt_hash §6 chain, causal_binding §24 cross-invocation predecessors), InvocationLedger/LedgerSink in easynet_axon::invocation, and easynet-verify as the offline auditor. Only confirm during implementation that causal_binding adequately expresses "mission terminal receipt aggregates these N child receipts"; if a dedicated composition-roll-up field is later judged necessary that WOULD be an Axon RFC-003 sec0 change, but the boundary criterion says start by landing on the existing contract, not extending it.
- **现状：** missing (mission path). Verified: grep for receipt/ledger/signature/LedgerSink/kernel in src/eal/interpreter.rs returns no production hits; run_mission_inproc in src/cli/mission_runs.rs returns a MissionRunResult (run_dir/run_id/meta/bound_vars/ok) with only an on-disk ExecutionTrace and no ledger linkage. Contrast: the contract is shipped in Axon (core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto InvocationReceipt; core/runtime-rs/easynet-verify) and partially wired in CLI for single invocations only (src/runtime/agents/mod.rs holds invocation_ledger: Arc<easynet_axon::invocation::InvocationLedger>; invocation_history_ability.rs reads ledger rows the daemon dispatch writes).
- **行动：** new (CLI-repo work, frame F-03 surface a missing-but-contract-ready capability): land an EAL/mission receipt path. Step 1 — give run_mission_inproc / the interpreter access to the existing LedgerSink + device signing key (the same one daemon single-invoke dispatch uses). Step 2 — on mission terminal output, build an InvocationReceipt (state=terminal, callee_signature by device key, causal_binding referencing the child step receipts) and append to InvocationLedger. Step 3 — add an easynet-verify integration test that audits a mission's signed receipt end-to-end. No RFC-003 sec0 note needed (no Axon contract change); revisit Axon only if a composition roll-up field proves necessary beyond causal_binding.

### 84. ✅ mission.discuss_round multi-agent orchestration ability

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI / EXECUTOR-ORCHESTRATOR). That is device runtime/orchestration, not protocol.
