Per INV-3 it is a method, not a global orchestrator/registry. It turns "multi-agent discussion" into something owner-hosted, URA-addressable, and uniformly drivable from EAL/MCP/CLI rather than a bespoke product loop.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Owns it fully: the discuss_round ability handler (snapshot fairness, parallel per-agent cycle, per-(room,agent) chat session continuity, skip protocol, error-as-skip, HARD_MAX_CYCLES governor), the discuss.* room service (create/post/subscribe), and the thin `easynet mission discuss` front-door verb that sequences discuss.create -> discuss.post -> discuss.discuss_round. This is RUNTIME+EXECUTOR+FRONT-DOOR — keep it CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for discuss_round itself — it is a CLI orchestration pattern, not a contract. Axon's only relevant role is the layer beneath it: the existing Mission IR (mission.proto) as the cross-node step/DAG/failure contract should remain the SHAPE that any cross-device variant of such orchestration compiles down to. Axon must NOT grow a "discussion"/"discuss_round"/room type; doing so would be over-sink of a device pattern into the neutral protocol.
- **现状：** shipped, CLI-only. Ability: src/runtime/agents/orchestration_ability.rs (const ABILITY_DISCUSS_ROUND = "mission.discuss_round" at :74; register at :112-124; handler :128+). Front door: src/cli/discuss.rs (373 lines) wired via src/cli/groups/mission.rs:44-45,121. Room service: src/daemon/execution/discuss/mod.rs. Documented Device-owned in docs/spec/owner-truth-table/ability-owner-truth-table.tex:496-523. Axon side: no discuss/room ontology in core/proto (grep empty), Mission IR present in core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto and README states EAL/Mission IR is "Implemented in EasyNet-Cli". Not forked.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — keep in CLI. F-05 cleanup only: the inline note in discuss.rs admits the post-sub-turn read uses "discuss.subscribe-style snapshot ... plain easynet.invoke against the subscribe ability's snapshot half for v1" — verify that read path goes through the two-RPC invoke surface (DEC-F01-Q9) and does not quietly couple to a realtime/subscribe channel that would pollute Receipt; tighten to the snapshot half explicitly. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 note n/a.

### 85. ✅ mission.run / track / cancel

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). By the boundary criterion this is "how this device runs/orchestrates/exposes" — RUNTIME (in-proc EAL interpreter) + EXECUTOR (Mission IR interpreter, src/eal/) + FRONT-DOOR (the same runtime surfaced AS an MCP tool for the in-agent LLM). The ratified thesis is explicit: "EAL/Mission is CLI orchestration language, not protocol." Abilities are OwnerKind::Device (mission_ability.rs:74-88) — device-local, not network actors, consistent with INV-3 (ability is a method, no global registry). NOT a protocol fork even though Axon ships mission.proto: that proto is the cross-NODE distributed Mission contract (MissionStep.target_node_id, depends_on, MissionConstraints, MissionState machine, Cancel/Abort/Watch, telemetry — restatement-mapping §2 lists 11/11 mission RPCs collapsed to abilities), a different object from the CLI's single-device EAL Mission IR v2. The two must not be conflated, but neither is wrong: Axon = cross-node mission contract; CLI EAL = single-device orchestration DSL. Watch item, not a defect: the per-step cross-agent dispatch underneath the interpreter (the member-call that actually crosses a node boundary) is the one part that must agree cross-language and therefore belongs on Axon's invoke contract, not a CLI-local wire.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the seam that lets an owner-device ability (an in-agent LLM) compose multi-step / multi-agent workflows through the one canonical orchestration path (run_mission_inproc) instead of shelling out or bypassing into mcp.bridge.call_tool. It keeps composition on the owner device, addressed via member-call (agent.ability) URA semantics, produces an auditable run dir (~/.easynet/missions/runs/<run_id> with source.eal, ir.json, trace.json, meta.json), and feeds receipts/governance. Directly serves the endgame: ability lives on the owner device, composed and audited locally.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Exactly what is shipped: (1) the EAL compile/execute pipeline (src/eal: lexer→parser→planner→ir→interpreter) as the single-device Mission IR runtime; (2) the single in-proc entry run_mission_inproc with run-dir persistence, track (find_run) and best-effort cancel (cancel_run); (3) the dual front door — `easynet mission run/list` for humans AND mission.run/track/cancel surfaced as MCP abilities so an in-agent LLM composes without shelling out. The MissionContextGuard gating cross-agent dispatch to a real run dir is also correctly CLI runtime. Owner = Device is the right ontology tag.
- **Axon 预期实现：** For these device.mission.* abilities themselves: n/a — they are CLI orchestration, not contract. Axon's legitimate role sits one layer down and is already present: the cross-node step dispatch that the EAL interpreter performs on a member-call (agent.ability) must ride Axon's neutral invoke→receipt contract so a different-language node can be the callee. Axon's mission.proto (cross-node distributed Mission, restated as 11 abilities) is a SEPARATE, larger object than the CLI EAL Mission IR and should stay neutral; the CLI must not back-port its run-dir / track / cancel / EAL grammar into Axon (that would be runtime over-sink into the neutral protocol).
- **现状：** shipped — src/runtime/agents/mission_ability.rs:47-60 (ABILITY_RUN/TRACK/CANCEL consts) + :73-89 (register with OwnerKind::Device) + handlers :120-226, thin shim over crate::cli::mission_runs::run_mission_inproc (mission_runs.rs:399-545) / find_run / cancel_run; EAL pipeline shipped in src/eal/{lexer,parser,planner,ir,interpreter}.rs. Axon mission.proto shipped at EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto (distinct cross-node contract). No fork between them today — different objects.
- **行动：** Keep in CLI as-is; no relayer to Axon. Frame F-05 cleanup: (a) the in-file doc comments and handler error strings still say "easynet.run / easynet.track / easynet.cancel" (mission_ability.rs:152, 175, 189, 211, 235-243 run_description, 269-298 track/cancel descriptions) after the easynet.* alias was deleted in RFC-001 v4.1.7 — these now mislead the LLM corpus; rename every user-facing/desc reference to device.mission.{run,track,cancel} so the discovery surface and error messages match the single canonical name. (b) Add a one-line note in src/eal/mod.rs distinguishing CLI EAL Mission IR (single-device orchestration) from Axon mission.proto (cross-node distributed mission contract) so the two "Mission" objects are not later conflated into a fork. No RFC-003 sec0 note — this is CLI-repo work, not Axon-repo.

### 86. ✅ EAL as cross-language contract / MissionControl v2 server engine

- **裁决：** `CORRECT for the EAL language + interpreter (CLI orchestration), with one FORKED sub-fact. EAL/Mission IR is how THIS device parses, plans, and runs a composition — pure RUNTIME/EXECUTOR orchestration, rightly CLI-local; pushing the EAL language into Axon would be over-sink. HOWEVER the step-result data-flow shape is a latent fork: CLI's IR adds input_refs (ir.rs:107) and output_binding (ir.rs:109) so steps can chain results, while Axon's shipped mission.proto MissionStep (line 28-61, last field parallel_safe=13) and StepStatus (line 189-199, last field error_message=9) carry NO result fields and explicitly discard InvokeResponse.result (per ir.rs:7-10). If EAL missions ever run server-side cross-node, two different-language nodes must AGREE on how a step result names and feeds the next step — that is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon. So: EAL engine = CORRECT in CLI; the result-binding step shape = MISPLACED-should-be-Axon the day it crosses nodes.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL. EAL today lets a human/agent orchestrate multi-step ability invocations on the owner device (compose abilities addressed by URA, each step a governed invoke->receipt), which directly serves the endgame. But result data-flow between steps lives only in the CLI-local interpreter; nothing is persisted/auditable as a cross-node mission, and easynet-verify cannot replay a mission. The composition value exists; the durable, cross-node, auditable form does not yet.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the EAL language, lexer/parser/planner, Mission IR, and the client-side interpreter (src/eal/) as CLI orchestration — the temporary engine that runs missions against the local daemon and Axon invoke primitives. CLI should NOT grow its own persistent mission server; when MissionControl v2 lands in Axon, CLI's interpreter becomes a client of it (compile EAL -> Axon mission contract, dispatch, watch) rather than re-implementing server-side state. CLI keeps the human verbs (mission run, --emit-ir) and the MCP exposure.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already owns the neutral mission contract (core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto: CreateMission/Dispatch/Cancel/Retry/Skip/Rollback/Watch, RFC-001-restated as agent abilities). What Axon does NOT yet provide and SHOULD, for cross-node EAL to be a real contract: result data-flow in the step shape — StepStatus.result + result_content_type, and MissionStep.input_refs/output_binding (the exact fields ir.rs:18-19 already names as the proto upgrade path). That is the cross-language agreement point. Axon is NOT expected to host the EAL language itself (that stays CLI); only the serializable mission + step-result contract.
- **现状：** Partial/forked. CLI EAL engine SHIPPED and client-side only (src/eal/{lexer,parser,planner,ir,interpreter}.rs; interpreter.rs:5 "Client-side execution engine ... temporary"). Axon mission contract SHIPPED but v1 (no result fields): mission.proto MissionStep ends at parallel_safe=13, StepStatus ends at error_message=9. CLI-local IR result-binding fork present: ir.rs:107 input_refs, ir.rs:109 output_binding, with no Axon counterpart. MissionControl v2 server engine MISSING in both repos (referenced only as future at ir.rs:14 and AAL at mod.rs:8).
- **行动：** Do NOT build MissionControl v2 now and do NOT move the EAL engine into Axon. Frame F-07 (ontology-absorption): the next concrete step is to write an Axon RFC-003 sec0 note proposing the step-result contract delta in mission.proto — StepStatus += {result, result_content_type}, MissionStep += {input_refs, output_binding} — exactly as ir.rs:18-19 documents, so the CLI-local IR fields stop being a CLI-private fork and become the neutral cross-node contract. Until that RFC lands, keep EAL CLI-local (F-05 cleanup: keep interpreter as the only mission executor; no CLI-side persistent mission server). RFC-003 sec0 note required since this touches Axon-repo proto.

### 87. ✅ EAL compiler pipeline (lexer → parser → planner → IR)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — by the boundary criterion this is "how this device orchestrates," pure CLI ORCHESTRATION-LANGUAGE. Two different-language nodes do NOT need to agree on EAL surface syntax (mission/let/call/with) to interoperate; they only need to agree on the IR/MissionStep shape, which already lives in Axon (core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto MissionStep/MissionConstraints). The lexer/parser/planner are correctly a single-device compiler with no cross-language agreement requirement. EAL is absent from Axon (no EAL grep hits in *.rs/*.proto — apparent matches were "Capability" substring false positives) and from EasyNet (only frontend i18n marketing copy in zh.ts, never imported/compiled). Sinking EAL into Axon would be over-sink (pollutes the neutral protocol with a CLI DSL); leaking it into EasyNet would be a layer leak. The contract boundary sits precisely at the IR, not the language.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — EAL lowers human/agent-authored orchestration into Mission IR whose steps carry input_refs/output_binding data flow and produce a structured audit trace per execution (ir.rs:1-19). That directly serves the endgame: owner-device abilities composed, addressed by URA (IrTarget::Agent(AgentId), ir.rs:55), governed/auditable. It is the "compose abilities" half of the value chain.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI OWNS this in full: lexer → parser → planner → IR compile (planner.rs:68 compile()), plus the interpreter (interpreter.rs) and the `mission run --emit-ir` front door. This is the EXECUTOR's Mission IR interpreter + the orchestration-language toolchain — squarely CLI per the three-repo thesis. Nothing about EAL should leave CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the compiler itself — Axon must NOT carry EAL. Axon's only obligation at this boundary is the IR target shape: MissionStep (mission.proto already has output via parallel_safe/depends_on) and the input_refs/output_binding data-flow fields the CLI IR header notes as a proto upgrade path (ir.rs:14-17 "MissionStep += output_binding (13), input_refs (14)"). That data-flow extension IS a cross-language contract concern (any non-CLI executor of the IR must agree) and is the one piece that legitimately belongs in Axon — but it is the IR, not the compiler.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI) — full pipeline present: src/eal/{lexer,parser,ast,planner,ir,interpreter,error}.rs; compile() at planner.rs:68 (analyzer folded into planner per mod.rs:14-18). The IR↔Axon-proto data-flow extension is partial: CLI IR carries input_refs/output_binding (ir.rs:14-17) but Axon mission.proto MissionStep does not yet have those fields — the contract side of the boundary lags the CLI.
- **行动：** Keep the EAL compiler in CLI as-is (F-03 surface: it is correctly placed, no migration). Separately track the IR data-flow fields as an Axon-repo task — land output_binding/input_refs on MissionStep in core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto so any non-CLI IR executor agrees on data flow (F-07 ontology-absorption: CLI ran ahead, Axon absorbs the IR field shape back, per F-01 sec7 precedent). RFC-003 sec0 note: that proto change is Axon-repo work — open it as an Axon RFC/PR with sec0 contract-change rationale; do NOT touch the EAL lexer/parser/planner as part of it.

### 90. ✅ EAL loop control-flow block (body + verify, max_iters)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). Boundary test: do two different-language nodes have to AGREE on loop/verify/max_iters to interoperate? No. The loop is interpreted entirely on this device — execute_loop (interpreter.rs:1277) iterates locally and dispatches individual IrCall leaves; a remote node B only ever receives a plain ability invocation, never a "loop" frame. Axon's mission.proto carries only a flat MissionStep DAG (depends_on, on_failure) with NO loop/verify/max_iters/iteration semantics — confirmed by grep. So this is device-local orchestration (EAL = CLI orchestration language, not protocol per the thesis), not a contract. Not over-sunk into Axon, not forked (no loop concept anywhere in Axon SDK/proto; the eal_subset.py example is a flat-step demo harness, no loop), and no product UI leak.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the loop records iteration intent in the auditable Mission IR (RFC §1 "mission as proof-carrying plan") and drives early termination via the verify predicate, so the user's owner-device orchestration produces a tighter, more honest audit trail than the hand-unrolled form. It serves the endgame at the orchestration layer; each iteration still bottoms out in ordinary URA-addressed ability invocations that return Surfaces and are governed/audited by Axon.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Owns it fully: EAL surface syntax (loop { body verify } max_iters), AST LoopBlock (ast.rs:57-63), planner lowering to IrStep::Loop / IrLoop (ir.rs:207-224) including nested-loop rejection (RFC §4.2), and the EXECUTOR semantics — bounded iteration, hermetic per-iteration scope, verify-predicate-driven early termination, and <name>.result export — in execute_loop (interpreter.rs:1277). The Mission IR interpreter living in CLI is exactly the RUNTIME/EXECUTOR role.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a cross-language contract. Axon must NOT grow a loop/verify/max_iters message; doing so would over-sink CLI orchestration into the neutral protocol and pollute it. Axon's only legitimate stake is the per-invocation contract each loop leaf already uses (URA addressability + Receipt), which exists; INV-3 (ability is a method, no global registry) and DEC-F01-Q9 (Receipt must not absorb realtime/orchestration state) both argue against sinking loop control-flow into Axon.
- **现状：** shipped in CLI: AST at src/eal/ast.rs:57-63 (LoopBlock, only block form in v1; chat/handoff removed per RFC §10); IR at src/eal/ir.rs:207-224 (IrLoop with serde "loop" discriminator); executor at src/eal/interpreter.rs:1277 (execute_loop). RFC docs/rfc/eal-control-flow-v1.md Approved (loop only). Absent from Axon by design (no loop in core/proto/axon/v1/mission.proto).
- **行动：** No layer move needed — confirm-and-leave-in-CLI. Frame F-05 (cleanup): keep the boundary explicit so EAL control-flow does not later drift into Axon. Concrete next step: in the EAL RFC's non-goals/§7, add a one-line invariant "Mission IR control-flow (loop/verify/max_iters) is CLI-local orchestration and MUST NOT appear in axon/v1/mission.proto; only resolved per-leaf invocations cross the wire." No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 note n/a.

### 91. ✅ EAL surface grammar: member-call vs traditional call

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — this is EAL surface syntax and IR lowering: how THIS device parses an orchestration program and routes dispatch. Two cross-language nodes do NOT need to agree on the textual form agent.ability(args) vs call "x" on "node" — they only need to agree on the resolved identity (AgentIdentity / URA), which Axon already owns (types.proto:314 AgentIdentity, caller.uri/callee). Verified Axon has no TargetKind/member-call/EAL concept (mission.proto only has target_node_id / node_id). The design also avoids the INV-3/invariant-2 trap correctly: surface form is the sole dispatch signal, no runtime is_agent string check (ast.rs:85-101, AGENT_IDENTITY.md:199). One adjacent note: the AgentId type the Agent arm carries is CLI-local (src/core/agent_id.rs, not imported from Axon) — that is the F-01 sec7 absorb-back case, but it is the identity ontology, not this surface-grammar feature.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — it is the EAL author's way to address an Agent (logical actor, URA-addressable, returns a Surface, governed by Axon) distinctly from a Device (hosting substrate). The agent.ability(args) form is exactly how the endgame "ability addressed by URA on owner device" is expressed in the orchestration language; the surface distinction maps onto the agent-vs-device ontology that drives the whole product.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Own the EAL surface grammar end to end: parser sets TargetKind at production-match time (ast.rs:89-101); planner lowers TargetKind::Agent → IrTarget::Agent(AgentId) and TargetKind::Device → IrTarget::Device{node_id} (ir.rs:51-60); runtime dispatcher branches on resolved IrTarget only, never on a string is_agent check; reject call "x" on "<registered-agent>" with an error pointing at member-call form (mission_runs.rs find_implicit_agent_fallback). This is exactly EXECUTOR + Mission-IR-interpreter responsibility.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the surface grammar itself — Axon must NOT learn EAL textual forms or TargetKind (that would over-sink orchestration into the neutral protocol). Axon's only adjacent responsibility is the identity the agent form resolves to: it already provides AgentIdentity / URA (caller/callee) as the cross-language contract (types.proto:314, invoke.proto:692-693). The CLI's local AgentId should eventually be sourced from that Axon ontology (separate F-07 item), but the grammar stays in CLI.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI: src/eal/ast.rs:76-101 (CallExpr + TargetKind, Device default), src/eal/ir.rs:51-60 (IrTarget Agent/Device), ratified in docs/AGENT_IDENTITY.md:222-230 (both forms in member-call position) and invariant 2 at :199 (no is_agent check). Axon: no surface-grammar code exists, which is correct (mission.proto target is node-id only).
- **行动：** No change to the surface grammar — keep it CLI-local; it is correctly placed and shipped. Frame tag F-05 cleanup: leave as-is. The only follow-on (separate feature, F-07 ontology-absorption) is making CLI's src/core/agent_id.rs AgentId derive from the Axon AgentIdentity/URA ontology rather than a local definition; that is Axon-repo-adjacent and would carry an RFC-003 sec0 note, but it is the identity ontology, not this grammar feature. For this feature: no action.

### 92. ✅ ExecutionTrace structured audit log

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — this is how THIS device runs and explains a Mission (EAL is CLI orchestration language, not protocol per the thesis). No two different-language nodes must agree on the ExecutionTrace JSON shape to interoperate: dispatch interop runs over Axon's Envelope/InvocationReceipt, and the per-step canonical audit is Axon's Timeline (mission.proto TimelineEntry / GetMissionTimeline + invoke.proto InvocationReceipt with prev_receipt_hash/self_hash/callee_signature). Verified the CLI struct carries NO signing, NO prev_hash chaining, NO envelope (grep clean), and its own doc (interpreter.rs:160) explicitly defers the canonical per-step log to $AXON_INVOCATION_LOG_DIR/<id>.jsonl. So result_sha256 here is a run-diff content fingerprint, not a Receipt chain link — it is EXECUTOR-layer diagnostics, correctly CLI-local. Pushing this versioned JSON into Axon would be over-sink (pollutes the neutral protocol with a CLI orchestration artifact).`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it serves auditability of an owner-device mission run (timestamps, per-step outcome, retry history, result fingerprint), which is part of the "auditable" leg of the endgame. But it is NOT the cross-language, signed, chained audit object the endgame ultimately verifies (easynet-verify reads Axon InvocationReceipt chains, not this JSON). It is local forensics for EAL orchestration, one rung below the governable/verifiable contract.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps ExecutionTrace as EXECUTOR-layer forensics: per-step timestamps, outcome, retry_history, result_sha256 content fingerprint, head/tail truncation accounting, versioned on-disk JSON for "diff two mission runs". This is legitimately CLI's Mission IR interpreter explaining its own run. CLI must keep it UNSIGNED and NON-CHAINED so it cannot be mistaken for, or drift into, an Axon Receipt.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the ExecutionTrace shape itself — Axon already owns the cross-language audit contracts that this trace must NOT duplicate or fork: InvocationReceipt (invoke.proto:659, signed/chained: prev_receipt_hash, self_hash, callee_signature, axiom 7-tuple binding) and Mission Timeline (mission.proto TimelineEntry/StepStatus/GetMissionTimeline). Axon's only contract obligation toward this feature is that the CLI trace's per-step records reference Axon invocation_ids/receipt indices so a reader can cross-walk local trace → signed receipt.
- **现状：** shipped, CLI-local. ExecutionTrace + StepTrace + RetryRecord + CappedTraceBuffer in src/eal/interpreter.rs:236-302; schema_version pin at :139; truncation at :166-228, :887-896; result_sha256 compute at :2078; golden shape test trace_schema_v1_is_stable at :2894-2990. Axon side shipped independently: InvocationReceipt (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/invoke.proto:659), Mission Timeline (mission.proto). No fork detected.
- **行动：** Keep ExecutionTrace in CLI as-is (no migration). Frame F-05 cleanup. One small follow-up: ensure each StepTrace carries the Axon invocation_id / receipt index so the local trace cross-walks to the signed InvocationReceipt chain (cross-walk field, not a chain copy) — this preserves the boundary and upgrades serves_easynet_value from PARTIAL toward the verifiable endgame. No RFC-003 sec0 note needed: this is CLI-repo EXECUTOR work, not an Axon contract change.

### 93. ✅ Implicit-agent-fallback rejection (EAL surface invariant guard)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). Apply the boundary criterion: this is how THIS device parses/compiles its own orchestration language (EAL) and reports a usage error to a human. The traditional-vs-member-call syntactic distinction is an EAL surface concept that exists only in the CLI compiler — Axon README ratifies EAL as "compiles to Mission IR ... Implemented in EasyNet-Cli." No second-language node needs to AGREE on this collision rule to interoperate: the wire contract carries a resolved AgentIdentity/URA or a device node_id (Axon types.proto:314 AgentIdentity, control.proto node_id), never the EAL source form. So it is not a forked protocol. The underlying ontology (agent vs device, §6.4) IS contract and is correctly already in Axon (types.proto AgentIdentity/URA); this guard merely defends that ontology at the CLI front door. Pushing the guard into Axon would over-sink a language-surface diagnostic into the neutral protocol (WRONG). It is not product UI. Hence EXECUTOR/FRONT-DOOR layer, correctly placed.`

But it is an ergonomic/diagnostic guard, not itself a piece of the identity+state+audit+URA+Surface endgame; the value it protects (the ontology) is owned elsewhere.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Exactly this: as the EAL EXECUTOR + human FRONT-DOOR, CLI owns the Mission IR compiler and must enforce EAL surface invariants (member-call = agent, traditional call...on = device-only) and emit a clear, actionable error pointing at member-call form before any disk artifact is written. The one-pass IR walk in find_implicit_agent_fallback (mission_runs.rs:338-388) at the single in-process entry run_mission_inproc (:399, guard at :423) is the right home — registry-aware, post-lowering, pre-persistence. Keep it CLI-local.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — Axon should NOT carry this guard. Axon's correct and already-shipped contribution is the neutral ontology this guard defends: AgentIdentity (URA-addressed network actor) vs device node_id, in core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto:314 and control.proto. Axon must keep providing that agent/device contract shape so every language node agrees on what an agent vs a device is; it must NOT learn EAL syntax or the traditional/member-call distinction (that would over-sink a CLI language surface into the protocol).
- **现状：** shipped — CLI src/cli/mission_runs.rs:338-388 (find_implicit_agent_fallback) and :423-433 (guard with member-call-form bail! message). Backed by invariant docs at src/eal/ir.rs:35-50 and src/eal/parser.rs:21-38, and three tests in mission_runs.rs:904/948/975 (no_implicit_agent_fallback_*). Underlying ontology contract shipped in Axon types.proto:314 (AgentIdentity/URA). AgentId surface type lives CLI-side at src/core/agent_id.rs:75, consistent with F-01 sec7 (CLI runs ahead; Axon absorbs the AgentId ontology back over time).
- **行动：** No move required — leave the guard in CLI as-is (F-05 cleanup posture). Frame tag F-05. Concrete next step: when F-01 sec7 absorption lands (Axon promotes the AgentId/agent-vs-device ontology fully into the contract), keep this guard CLI-local but ensure it consumes the Axon-canonical agent/device classification rather than CLI's own AgentId parse, so the collision check stays aligned with the ratified contract. No RFC-003 sec0 note needed (this is not Axon-repo work; it is CLI executor/front-door surface).

Frame F-05: prune the ir.rs:17-19 "Proto Upgrade Path" comment that implies these fields belong in Axon proto — they do not; record instead that result-passing over the wire is a separate Axon feature. Spin out that separate feature as new: add StepStatus.result + result_content_type (and MissionStep result-ref) to EasyNet-Axon mission.proto only when over-the-wire MissionControl v2 is actually pursued; per RFC-003 sec0 any such Axon-repo proto change must land as its own DEC under the cross-repo coordination plane, not bundled with CLI orchestration work.

Value is real but indirect (orchestration mechanics, not the user-visible contract).

## MCP 绑定 / MCP-Binding（15）

### 100. 🟡 MCP stream-ability projection into provider (InvokeStream→progress)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-CLI (work is CLI-side; nothing should move TO Axon). The CONTRACT shape that two languages must agree on — progress-notification wire frames, terminal-response shape, stream-handle/ProgressSink/ReportOutcome — already lives in Axon (sdk/rust/src/mcp.rs:41-76, 87-110) and is complete + forward-compatible. The missing piece is purely how THIS device runs/projects: driving InvokeStream chunks into that contract. That is EXECUTOR/FRONT-DOOR (CLI). Pushing a stream method into Axon's LocalInvoker-equivalent would be over-sink; the gap is a genuine CLI MISSING wiring, not a protocol fork.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — streaming progress is how an agent (MCP front-door consumer) sees an ability working chunk-by-chunk, which serves the dual-front-door endgame and lets a Surface render incrementally. But it is a UX/liveness improvement on an already-functional unary path; the owner-identity/URA/Receipt/audit core works without it, so it is value-additive, not load-bearing for the endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Extend the CLI-local LocalInvoker trait (src/runtime/agents/profiles/mcp.rs:478) with a stream-aware method (e.g. invoke_stream yielding a chunk iterator / handle), implemented by DaemonLocalInvoker over the EXISTING local streaming primitives (local_runtime_invoker.rs:109 open_local_stream / :247 drain_local_stream_frames / runtime invoke_stream_async). Then have InvokeMcpProvider override handle_tool_call_with_progress (mcp.rs:840) to: if the ability is streaming, drive each InvokeStream chunk into sink.report(...) with monotonically increasing progress, and emit the terminal chunk as the tools/call response; optionally override handle_tool_call_stream (currently inheriting Axon's None) to return a real McpToolStreamHandle. Unary abilities keep the N=1 path. No Receipt pollution — realtime stays on the progress channel (DEC-F01-Q9 two-RPC surface).
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — Axon already supplies the complete neutral contract: McpToolProvider::handle_tool_call_stream + handle_tool_call_with_progress + ProgressSink + ReportOutcome + McpToolStreamHandle (sdk/rust/src/mcp.rs:22-110). No new Axon shape is required; the defaults (Ok(None), fall-through) are exactly the forward-compatible hooks CLI overrides. Adding a stream method to Axon for this would over-sink CLI runtime concerns into the neutral protocol.

### 101. 🔴 MCP tool spec enrichment (x-easynet owner/cost/exec metadata)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (taxonomy half) + CORRECT (projection half). Split the feature: (a) the MCP wire projection — the `x-easynet` JSON object, the annotated description prefix, the tool-spec assembly — is how THIS device exposes abilities to a local agent over its MCP front door; that is CLI EXECUTOR/FRONT-DOOR and is correctly placed. (b) the cost/owner SEMANTIC taxonomy (CostKind enum, cost_kind/cost_label/owner_user/owner_agent/exec_kind as ability metadata) is data two different-language nodes must AGREE on to honestly report "who owns this and what does it cost" across a federated invoke. That contract shape lives ONLY in CLI today (src/core/ability_spec.rs CostMeta/CostKind; Axon proto/SDK has no cost_kind/owner_user/exec_kind — its only `cost` is a 0.0-1.0 routing weight in types.proto, unrelated). A CLI-local copy of cross-node ability metadata is a protocol fork.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the honesty rule (owner + cost surfaced on every tool, defaulting to "unknown" not "free") directly serves the endgame: an agent choosing between abilities sees who owns it and whether invoking it bills the owner, which is the governable/auditable leg. But it only serves it for the LOCAL MCP front door today; cross-node honest cost/owner does not yet flow because Axon carries no cost/owner ability metadata, so a remote ability's cost cannot be trusted end-to-end.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Own the MCP-binding projection: assemble tools/list entries, stamp the `x-easynet` provenance object, prefix the description with owner+cost, and apply the runtime exec-kind heuristic fallback (this is device-local "how do I run/expose it" knowledge, e.g. inferring llm_metered for agent: sources). The "unknown not free" honesty default stays a CLI runtime rule. CLI consumes the cost_kind/owner fields FROM Axon's ability metadata rather than defining the enum itself.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Define the neutral ability-metadata contract fields that the projection reads: cost_kind (closed enum: free | external_metered | llm_metered | unknown), cost_label (free-form), owner_user / owner_agent (derivable from owner URA), and the exec_kind classifier — as part of the cross-language ability descriptor / manifest shape so a Python-authored ability and a Rust CLI agree on what "cost" and "owner" mean over a federated invoke. Axon must NOT define the `x-easynet` MCP JSON wrapper or the description-prefix string — that is CLI front-door presentation, not contract.
- **现状：** partial+forked. Projection shipped in CLI: src/runtime/agents/profiles/mcp.rs:90-225 (tool_spec_from_descriptor_with_name, annotated_mcp_description, inferred_cost_kind/inferred_cost_label). Taxonomy shipped CLI-only: src/core/ability_spec.rs:198-269 (CostMeta, CostKind enum, as_wire_str) and src/cli/agent.rs:309-317,1682 (--cost-kind/--cost-label flags). Axon side MISSING: grep of EasyNet-Axon proto + Rust/Python SDK shows no cost_kind/cost_label/owner_user/exec_kind ability-metadata; only types.proto:999 routing-weight `double cost`. No EasyNet hub/surface consumes the `x-easynet` tool-spec object (the hub's `x-easynet-*` hits are unrelated gRPC delegation/idempotency headers).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption. Step 1 (Axon repo, RFC-003 sec0: this is a new cross-language ability-metadata field set, so it needs a sec0 contract-change note before landing): add cost_kind (closed enum) + cost_label + owner_user/owner_agent + exec_kind to the Axon ability descriptor/manifest contract; CLI runs ahead per F-01 sec7, Axon absorbs the taxonomy back. Step 2 (CLI): re-point src/core/ability_spec.rs CostKind/CostMeta to the Axon-defined types (import, don't redefine) and keep mcp.rs as the pure projection that reads them. Leave the `x-easynet` JSON wrapper, description prefix, and the "unknown not free" runtime fallback in CLI — those are correctly the device front-door's job. Do NOT push the MCP wire shape into Axon.

### 102. ✅ Ability→MCP tool-name projection + reverse route table

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). It is an edge-adapter for one specific MCP client grammar, applied at the inbound MCP front-door and reversed before the registry — it never crosses the federation wire. Axon's invoke.proto:170 carries `ability_name` (dotted canonical) as the contract key and has no tool_name projection — so two nodes never have to AGREE on the projection, hence it is NOT a contract and pushing it into Axon would be over-sink. This is the device's how-it-exposes-to-an-agent layer, which is exactly CLI.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — MCP is one of CLI's two front doors (the agent-facing one). Projecting dotted ability names into the `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+` grammar Codex/Claude Code require is what lets an external agent actually call an owner-device ability; the reverse table immediately resolves back to the canonical dotted ability_name that the registry/URA/Receipt use. So it directly serves "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, invoked, returns a receipt" by making the agent door reachable without polluting the addressable identity.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Exactly what is shipped: deterministic name projection (mcp.rs:237-255), collision resolution via truncated SHA-256 suffix (mcp.rs:412-416, 8-byte width), and a forward+reverse McpToolRouteTable (mcp.rs:294-359) consumed by the MCP front-door (tools/list projection, call_tool dispatch) and InvokeMcpProvider. CLI owns the entire mapping because it is the MCP-grammar edge adapter; canonical dotted name stays the registry key and is carried in x-easynet.ability metadata.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a contract. Axon's only relevant obligation (already met) is to keep the dotted `ability_name` as the neutral addressable contract field (invoke.proto:170); it must NOT learn the MCP-specific `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+` projection, since no cross-language node-to-node agreement depends on it. Adding it would fork product-of-one-client grammar into the neutral protocol.
- **现状：** shipped, CLI only. CLI: src/runtime/agents/profiles/mcp.rs:237-255 (mcp_tool_name_for_ability), :412-416 (short_ability_hash), :294-359 (McpToolRouteTable), consumed at src/runtime/agents/mcp_bridge_ability.rs:59,159 and src/facade/mcp/mod.rs:41. Axon: absent by design — grep of core/proto + dendrite-bridge shows ability_name (dotted) only, no tool-name projection. Not forked: no incompatible second copy exists.
- **行动：** No move; keep in CLI. Frame F-05 cleanup (consolidation already done — the doc-comment at mcp.rs:282-289 records the refactor that collapsed three recompute sites into McpToolRouteTable). Only residual hygiene: the legacy-alias accept path in canonical_for_tool (mcp.rs:365-372) lets pre-projection clients pass dotted names directly — leave it, but tag for eventual removal once no client speaks the old wire shape. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 note is n/a.

### 109. ✅ Inbound MCP server (tools/list + tools/call edge adapter)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (split is properly drawn). The cross-language CONTRACT half — JSON-RPC envelope, tools/list+tools/call handler, progress-token routing, ProgressSink — lives in Axon SDK in three languages (Rust sdk/rust/src/mcp.rs StdioMcpServer/McpToolProvider/ToolResult; Go sdk/go/easynet/mcp/server.go; Python sdk/python/easynet_axon/mcp/server.py), governed by AXON-RFC-004. The CLI half is device-local FRONT-DOOR wiring: it imports easynet_axon::mcp::StdioMcpServer and only supplies InvokeMcpProvider (the local descriptor catalog + tool→ability route table + routing each tools/call into the daemon's Axon Invoke). No protocol fork in CLI (CLI does not re-implement the wire shape), no product UI leak. This is the textbook contract-in-Axon / runtime-in-CLI division.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the agent half of the DUAL FRONT DOOR. It lets external agents (Codex/Claude Code) reach owner-device abilities, and per RFC-001 §A3 every tools/call is forced through the daemon Axon Invoke, so identity/governance/audit are preserved (the call lands as a real Invocation→Receipt, not a side channel). MCP is kept at the edge only; it does not become a second protocol. Directly serves the endgame: ability lives on owner device, addressed via Axon, governed, auditable.
- **CLI 预期实现：** FRONT-DOOR + RUNTIME: `easynet mcp_server` / `start --mcp` entry, build the InvokeMcpProvider (gather host+agent AbilityDescriptors, build tool-name route table, project descriptors→MCP tool_specs), and route every tools/call through DaemonLocalInvoker into the daemon-hosted Axon Invoke. CLI must NOT re-implement the JSON-RPC framing or invent a parallel catalog — it imports the Axon server.
- **Axon 预期实现：** CONTRACT: the neutral MCP edge-binding — StdioMcpServer (request loop, initialize/tools/list/tools/call dispatch, protocol version 2025-06-18, progress-token extraction, ToolResult→MCP result envelope), McpToolProvider trait, ProgressSink — provided identically across Rust/Go/Python SDKs so any-language node speaks the same wire shape. Governed by AXON-RFC-004 (mcp-binding) Vector B (inbound external client → Cli).

Axon contract: sdk/rust/src/mcp.rs:16-76 (ToolResult/McpToolProvider), Go sdk/go/easynet/mcp/server.go:210-217, Python sdk/python/easynet_axon/mcp/server.py:260-265; AXON-RFC-004 §5.2 Vector B.
- **行动：** No structural move needed — boundary is correct. Maintenance/verification only (F-05 cleanup): (1) add/confirm a CLI conformance test that an unadvertised tool name is rejected AND that PRIVATE skills never leak via tools/list under a non-local operator (AXON-RFC-001 §12 threat row), and (2) close the B2b gap noted in mcp.rs:826-839 — stream-ability→progress projection is stubbed (N=1 progress pulse) pending a stream-aware LocalInvoker method; that lift stays in CLI (executor concern), the per-chunk progress wire shape is already in the Axon contract so no Axon change. No RFC-003 sec0 Axon work required.

The thing that two nodes MUST agree on (the verbatim MCP {content,isError} result shape on the wire and across a federation hop) is already CONTRACT in Axon RFC-004 §3.5/§5.1 Vector A, with conformance tests in EasyNet-Axon core/runtime-rs/src/tests/multi_shard_e2e.rs. - **Axon 预期实现：** Not the executor itself — Axon owns the result-shape and receipt contract that this executor must honour: the verbatim {content,isError} return (RFC-004 §3.5/§5.1), the URA-carries-no-mcp-label discipline (§3.1), and the cross-binding receipt persistence so easynet verify closes the chain. Axon does NOT and must NOT gain an AbilityExec enum or executor-dispatch concept (the proto has none — invoke.proto only references "executor" as the callee identity, line 693).

Executor at src/runtime/agents/mcp_executor.rs:121 (run_mcp_exec), variant at src/core/ability_spec.rs:453 (AbilityExec::Mcp / McpExec), validate at :812, boot wiring at src/runtime/agents/mod.rs:824, EAL/chat call sites at src/eal/interpreter.rs:606 and src/runtime/agents/chat_ability.rs:305. Separately confirm the Cli-side RFC-004 addendum (EasyNet-Cli/document/rfcs/004-mcp-binding-cli-addendum.md, referenced by Axon RFC-004 §4.2) explicitly covers the deterministic [exec] kind="mcp" path, not just the reflective registry — it currently reads as reflection-only. No RFC-003 sec0 note (no Axon-repo code change required; Axon contract already covers the wire shape).

### 112. ✅ Outbound MCP client service (McpClientService)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — belongs in CLI/EXECUTOR. MCP is an external (Anthropic) standard, not an EasyNet cross-language contract; two EasyNet nodes interoperate via Axon URA/invoke/receipt, NOT by agreeing on MCP. - **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this in full: a daemon sub-service holding every outbound MCP stdio/http connection (per-connection listener task, initialize handshake, tools/list + tools/call, notifications/list_changed hot-reload), mirroring PtyService's process-wide-Arc shape. It is the executor adapter that turns a remote MCP catalogue into invocable local abilities. Confirmed wired at src/daemon/execution/mcp_client/mod.rs and consumed by src/runtime/agents/mcp_reflective_registry.rs.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the wire transport — Axon must NOT carry an outbound-MCP-client contract; pushing JSON-RPC/stdio framing into the neutral protocol would be over-sink. Axon's only legitimate MCP surface is the inbound provider (expose EasyNet ability AS MCP, sdk/rust/src/mcp.rs) and the AbilitySpec/URA shape that the ingested tools must be normalized into.
- **现状：** shipped in CLI at src/daemon/execution/mcp_client/mod.rs (McpClientService, McpServerSpec, per-connection listener, initialize, tools/list, tools/call, notifications hot-reload via RegistryRefreshSink); consumed by src/runtime/agents/mcp_reflective_registry.rs. - **行动：** Keep in CLI as-is; no migration. F-05 cleanup only: ensure ingested MCP tools are normalized to Axon's canonical AbilitySpec/URA shape at the mcp_reflective_registry boundary (grep Axon SDK before shaping per ura-discipline), so the executor stays MCP-specific while what crosses the layer is Axon-neutral. No Axon-repo change required, so no RFC-003 sec0 note.

### 113. ✅ Per-agent workspace MCP descriptor inclusion

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — belongs in CLI (EXECUTOR + FRONT-DOOR). Boundary test: two different-language nodes do NOT need to agree on this selection policy to interoperate. The thing they must agree on — the MCP tool-list/tools-call wire shape and the descriptor wire shape — is already Axon-owned (easynet_axon::mcp::McpToolProvider/ToolResult/StdioMcpServer, RFC-004 §3.6) and AXON-RFC-001-shaped (owner_agent_ura/visibility). per_agent_workspace_descriptors is the device-local "which of my agents' abilities do I land in this LLM's tool list for this --agent invocation" assembly: reads on-disk TOMLs, synthesises self-bundle, unions cross-agent. Pushing that policy into Axon would over-sink runtime/registry concerns into the neutral protocol (Axon has zero per-agent/workspace concept today, verified). No product UI leaking either.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the mechanism that makes the ontology's "agent owns/exposes abilities" promise real at runtime: a wrapped LLM CLI running inside agent A can actually call A's own abilities, A's self-bundle (discover/invoke), and peers' abilities, all dispatched through the daemon holding the owner identity. Without it, declared abilities were dead metadata (the comment at mcp.rs:606-610 states exactly this). It directly serves the endgame: ability lives on owner device, addressed by owner URA, callable, auditable.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI provides exactly this: (1) EXECUTOR — read agent registry + workspace ability manifests, synthesise the self-bundle discover/invoke descriptors, union peers, filter the recursive .chat surface; (2) FRONT-DOOR — feed the assembled descriptor set into the Axon stdio MCP server so the wrapped LLM (human-driving-agent path) sees them as tools; (3) RUNTIME — dispatch the resulting tools/call through DaemonLocalInvoker under the owner identity. This is correctly CLI-resident at src/runtime/agents/profiles/mcp.rs:598-622 / 630-788.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon provides ONLY the neutral primitives this feature consumes, and that is correct: the McpToolProvider trait + ToolResult + StdioMcpServer JSON-RPC framing (sdk/rust/src/mcp.rs, RFC-004 §3.6) and the AbilityDescriptor wire ontology (name / owner_agent_ura / visibility / scope per AXON-RFC-001 §9). Axon must NOT learn the words "agent workspace", "self-bundle", or cross-agent selection — that is CLI orchestration, not contract. n/a for any new Axon work driven by THIS feature.
- **现状：** shipped — src/runtime/agents/profiles/mcp.rs:598-622 (build_stdio_server wires --agent into descriptor extension) and :630-788 (per_agent_workspace_descriptors: Phase 1 own abilities, Phase 1b synthesised self-bundle discover/invoke, Phase 2 cross-agent). Consumes Axon at :790 (impl easynet_axon::mcp::McpToolProvider). Verified Axon has no competing per-agent concept (grep empty in sdk/rust/src).
- **行动：** No layer move needed; keep in CLI. Frame F-05 (cleanup): the descriptor wire SHAPE itself (AbilityDescriptor in src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs, whose own doc-comment says it is advertised over federation.advertise_abilities and is AXON-RFC-001 §9 shaped) is a separate FORK candidate to track — that shape, not this selection policy, is what cross-language nodes must agree on and is a candidate for Axon absorption. Audit per-feature separately under F-07 (ontology-absorption); do NOT bundle it with this feature. This feature requires no Axon-repo work, so no RFC-003 sec0 note.

### 114. ✅ Reflective MCP-tool → ability registry

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — this is EXECUTOR/binding: how THIS device runs an upstream MCP tool and projects it into a local ability. It does NOT fork the protocol: it mints the canonical Axon URA shape (easynet:///r/<realm>/ability/<owner>.<tail>, verified against EasyNet-Axon types.proto), reuses Axon's existing cross-language MCP contract (InvocationTargetKind.MCP_TOOL, McpToolTarget, target_tool_name in federation.proto) for dispatch, and deliberately keeps the impl-source tag OUT of the address — provenance is a CLI-local free-form AbilityDescriptor.source string (src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs:334-338), which is device bookkeeping no two nodes must agree on. Pushing the reflection mechanism or name_prefix/aliases collision policy into Axon would be over-sink into the neutral protocol.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — it turns an external MCP process into a first-class, URA-addressable, owner-attributed ability living on this owner device, so an agent's MCP tools become governable/auditable EasyNet abilities reachable like any other. That is exactly the endgame (ability lives on owner device, addressed by canonical URA, dispatchable, provenance recorded).
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this in full: (EXECUTOR) call tools/list on McpClientService and register each upstream tool as one local ability descriptor; (RUNTIME) reflection mode (off/lazy/eager) so reflection stays off the bounded boot path; collision resolution via name_prefix/aliases at config-load with no-overwrite policy; stamp provenance into AbilityDescriptor.source via format_mcp_upstream_source. Round-2 items still CLI-local: notifications/tools/list_changed refresh, HTTP transport, unregister(name).

Axon should NOT learn about reflection, name_prefix/aliases, or the mcp_upstream: source prefix — those are device-local.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI) — src/runtime/agents/mcp_reflective_registry.rs (mode parsing, canonical-URA reflection, format/is_mcp_upstream_source, ReflectedAbility/ReflectFailure/ReflectResult); descriptor field at src/runtime/ability_descriptor.rs:334-338. Axon contract side shipped independently in EasyNet-Axon core/proto/axon/v1/types.proto and federation.proto. Round-2 (list_changed refresh, HTTP transport, unregister) explicitly partial per the module header.
- **行动：** No layer move needed; keep in CLI. Next step (F-05 cleanup): land the round-2 EXECUTOR items the module header defers — notifications/tools/list_changed-driven refresh and unregister(name) — so reflected catalogs stay live without a full registry drop, and wire HTTP-transport reflection once McpClientService dispatches HTTP. No Axon-repo change, so RFC-003 sec0 does not apply. One verification follow-up: confirm the reflected descriptor's federation advertisement sets target_kind=MCP_TOOL + target_tool_name so cross-device dispatch resolves via Axon's typed target rather than ability_name fallback.

## 联邦 / Federation（9）

### 115. 🟡 Cross-realm invoke routing (federation.forward_invoke + presence key coercion)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-CLI (this Go adapter), and the substantive contract pieces are already correctly split. The wire envelope that two different-language nodes must agree on to interoperate cross-realm IS already in Axon (axon/v1/federation.proto: DispatchFederatedInvocationRequest/Response, Envelope). The presence-key coercion + forward_invoke dispatch is device-runtime behavior and already lives in CLI (daemon_invocation_service.rs: dispatch_federation_forward_invoke, crate::ura::canonicalize_presence_key, 192 forward_invoke refs). The EasyNet Go file (remote_routing.go) is a hub-backend ability-name router/InvokeResponse-wrapper that re-implements ability classification (federation./runtime./legacy self alias. vs device-hosted) — that classification is a CLI-daemon concern; mirroring it in Go backend per its own comment ("mirrors daemon_invocation_service.invoke() match arm in EasyNet-Cli; keep it in sync") is a runtime-classification fork that should be sourced from the CLI/Axon contract, not hand-maintained in Go.`
- **当前所在：** easynet
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — cross-realm invoke is exactly the URA-addressable, owner-device-hosted, hub-routed endgame (a caller in realm A reaches an ability hosted on a device in realm B and gets a result). But this particular artifact is a backend gRPC ability-name classifier + InvokeResponse wrapper; it serves the value only as a hub-side plumbing shim, not as the substantive routing/governance.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI (Rust daemon) owns and already provides: (1) RUNTIME presence resolution via local PresenceRegistry + runtime.invoke_remote reverse-channel push to the target device session; (2) the federation.forward_invoke dispatcher (dispatch_federation_forward_invoke) that dials the peer hub for cross-realm targets; (3) the presence-key canonicalizer (crate::ura::canonicalize_presence_key) that coerces agent/<bare-id> device aliases from older daemons to canonical device/<id> at the forward_invoke entry. The ability-name classification (daemon-internal vs device-hosted) is the CLI's match arm and is the authoritative source the Go adapter mirrors.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the neutral cross-language wire contract only: the Envelope, DispatchFederatedInvocationRequest/Response, and InvocationTargetKind in axon/v1/federation.proto — what two different-language hubs/daemons must agree on to forward an invoke across realms and return a terminal result. Axon should NOT own presence-key coercion, alias canonicalization rules, or ability-name classification (those are device-runtime/rollout concerns; sinking them would pollute the neutral protocol). The canonical presence-key SHAPE (device/<id> vs agent/<user>.<agent> URA forms) is the AgentId/URA ontology Axon already governs — the coercion of legacy aliases stays CLI-local.
- **现状：** partial/forked. SHIPPED in CLI: dispatch_federation_forward_invoke + canonicalize_presence_key call sites in /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Cli/src/services/invocation_transport/daemon_invocation_service.rs (and src/services/realm_trust_anchor.rs); URA forms in /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Cli/src/ura.rs. SHIPPED in Axon: cross-realm envelope contract in /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/federation.proto:156-289. FORKED in EasyNet hub: /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet/backend/internal/daemon_grpc/remote_routing.go:88-137 hand-maintains a Go copy of the CLI ability-name match arm (daemonInternalAbility) with a self-admitted "keep it in sync" hazard; canonicalize_presence_key here is referenced only in the file comment (line 25), the real coercer is CLI-side.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup: eliminate the hand-maintained Go fork of the ability classifier in remote_routing.go:127-137 (daemonInternalAbility) — drive daemon-internal-vs-device-hosted classification from a single shared source rather than a Go copy that must be manually synced with the CLI match arm. Concretely: have the hub backend defer classification to the CLI daemon (let the daemon 501/route) or generate the prefix set from the Axon contract, so the prefix list (federation./runtime./legacy self alias.) is not duplicated. Leave presence-key coercion and forward_invoke dispatch where they are (CLI); leave the wire envelope where it is (Axon). RFC-003 sec0: no Axon-repo change required here — the cross-realm envelope contract is already landed in axon/v1/federation.proto; the fix is hub-backend dedup, not protocol work.

### 116. 🔴 Directory presence / online-status events (subscribe + projection)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — the DirectoryEvent wire shape is a cross-language contract (Rust daemon producer ↔ Go backend consumer must agree byte-for-byte), so by the boundary criterion it belongs in Axon. It exists THREE places with divergent shapes: Axon directory_events.rs (v1: snapshot/agent_advertised/abilities_advertised/agent_revoked, no presence status on increments), CLI federation_directory.rs (v2: snapshot/upsert/remove + DirectoryEntry.status active|stale|draining, fed from PresenceRegistry), and a hand-mirrored Go copy in directory_events.go whose own header says "wire shape source-of-truth: the daemon's federation_directory.rs". The v2 presence shape that production actually runs lives only in CLI + a Go hand-copy; Axon never absorbed it. The broker/SSE/cap/projection mechanics themselves are correctly EasyNet — only the wire-shape definition is mis-layered.`
- **当前所在：** easynet
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — presence (online/offline/stale) is exactly the "is this owner's device reachable right now" signal the endgame needs: a URA is only addressable if its host daemon is live, and the user-facing surface must reflect that within ~1s. The subscribe+project+fanout chain is what makes a device stop/online transition visible to the product.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI correctly OWNS the runtime+executor side: PresenceRegistry tracks live daemon sessions (the device-reachability ground truth), and the daemon front-door serves federation.subscribe_directory_v2 as a server-stream, projecting PresenceEvent→DirectoryEvent (presence_event_to_directory_event). This is "how this device exposes its liveness" = correct CLI. What stays in CLI: the registry, the stream dispatch, the presence→event projection logic. What CLI should NOT keep as its private source-of-truth: the serde wire-shape definition of DirectoryEvent/DirectoryEntry — it should import that enum/struct from the Axon contract library so daemon and backend can't drift.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD define the neutral v2 DirectoryEvent / DirectoryEntry wire contract (the serde-tagged enum: snapshot/upsert/remove/heartbeat, entry.status enum, the SigningAuthorityWire shape) as the single cross-language source of truth, replacing/superseding its stale v1 directory_events.rs. Both the Rust daemon (CLI) and any consumer import it; the Go backend gets it via a generated/published schema rather than a hand-mirrored struct. Axon stays neutral: it defines the SHAPE only, it does not run the PresenceRegistry or the SSE broker (those are device-runtime / product and must NOT sink into Axon).
- **现状：** forked/partial. v2 producer shipped in CLI: src/services/federation_directory.rs:73-208 (DirectoryEntry+DirectoryEvent), served at src/services/invocation_transport/daemon_invocation_service.rs:3450 and federation_wrappers.rs:141. Go consumer shipped: backend/internal/events/directory_events.go:47-96 (hand-mirrored), broker.go:184/456 opens federation.subscribe_directory_v2, sse_handler fans out. Axon still only has the v1 shape: core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/directory_events.rs:59-108 (no upsert/remove, no presence status) — never updated to v2.
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: lift the v2 DirectoryEvent/DirectoryEntry wire contract into Axon as the neutral cross-language shape (superseding Axon's v1 directory_events.rs), then have the CLI daemon import that type instead of defining its own, and have the Go backend consume the published Axon schema instead of the hand-mirror in directory_events.go. RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work touching the federation directory wire contract — file under sec0 as a contract-absorption change (CLI ran ahead per F-01 sec7; Axon now absorbs the v2 presence ontology back), with a conformance test pinning the snapshot/upsert/remove/heartbeat + status enum across the Rust↔Go boundary so the fork cannot reopen.

### 117. 🔴 SSE event stream endpoint (/api/v1/events)

- **裁决：** `FORKED — split the feature in two. (a) The SSE HTTP endpoint (EventSource framing, JWT-in-query, X-Accel-Buffering, device whitelist from DevicePairing, per-write deadline, drop-rate hangup) is browser-facing product surface + hub routing → CORRECT in EasyNet; it is NOT a cross-language interop point (only browsers consume it). (b) The DirectoryEvent wire enum the broker decodes (snapshot/upsert/remove/heartbeat, serde tag="type") IS a cross-language contract: two different-language nodes — the Rust daemon hub-relay (EasyNet-Cli/src/services/federation_directory.rs:195 enum DirectoryEvent) and the Go hub broker (EasyNet/backend/internal/events/directory_events.go:47 directoryEvent) — must AGREE on it to interoperate, and it is defined twice by hand (the Go file literally states "Wire shape source-of-truth: the daemon's directory_events.rs"). Axon has no such message (federation.proto carries only heartbeat/membership/relay; types.proto:703 has node-level online/last_seen but no DirectoryEvent/DirectoryEntry/subscribe_directory). That hand-mirror across languages = protocol fork, belongs in Axon.`
- **当前所在：** easynet
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — the HTTP/SSE fan-out itself is pure product transport (browser sees live presence/directory changes, which serves "ability addressed by URA, owner device online/offline visible to the user"). But it only serves the endgame insofar as the underlying event SHAPE is a governable contract; today that shape is hand-forked, so the presence/directory signal the user sees is not anchored in Axon's neutral protocol.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the EXECUTOR/RUNTIME side: the daemon hosts federation.subscribe_directory_v2 as a server-streaming ability (federation_directory.rs:1850, const at invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs:141) and emits DirectoryEvent frames built from its own PresenceRegistry. It must IMPORT the DirectoryEvent shape from Axon rather than hand-define the serde enum locally. CLI does NOT own the SSE/HTTP endpoint or any browser framing.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD define the federation directory-stream contract as the single neutral source of truth: a DirectoryEvent (snapshot/upsert/remove/heartbeat) + DirectoryEntry message (with agent_ura, node_id, signing_authority kind/host_ura, status) and the federation.subscribe_directory_v2 server-stream signature, in axon/v1/federation.proto, so both the Rust daemon and the Go hub generate the type instead of mirroring it by hand. Axon does NOT define the SSE HTTP endpoint (that is a device/product transport, not a cross-language protocol).
- **现状：** forked. SSE endpoint shipped at EasyNet/backend/internal/handler/custom_routes.go (Path /events -> sse.Handler, prefix /api/v1) + sse_handler.go (full impl). Broker decodes the contract at EasyNet/backend/internal/events/broker.go:421 openDirectoryStream + directory_events.go (hand-mirrored struct). Daemon side shipped at EasyNet-Cli/src/services/federation_directory.rs (enum DirectoryEvent + subscribe_directory_v2 stream). Axon side MISSING: no DirectoryEvent/DirectoryEntry message in any axon/v1/*.proto — the agreement lives only in the two app repos.
- **行动：** Leave the SSE endpoint where it is (EasyNet, correct). De-fork the contract: add DirectoryEvent + DirectoryEntry + the federation.subscribe_directory_v2 server-stream to EasyNet-Axon axon/v1/federation.proto, regenerate the Rust SDK so federation_directory.rs imports the type instead of hand-defining the serde enum, and replace the Go directory_events.go hand-mirror with the generated Go type. Frame F-07 (ontology-absorption: Cli ran ahead and minted the DirectoryEvent shape; Axon now absorbs it back as the neutral contract, exactly the F-01 sec7 AgentId pattern). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is an Axon-repo proto change touching the federation transport plane — file under RFC-003 sec0 as a federation contract addition (new message + streaming RPC), coordinate with the transport-plane PR owner before regenerating SDK artifacts.

### 118. ✅ Backend aggregator agent (advertise_agent + advertise_abilities)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (split feature, both halves placed right). The aggregate.* fan-out/flatten/dedupe catalog logic is hub-side product aggregation (EasyNet = hub backend role) and a backend-profile Agent's own method per INV-3 (ability is a method, no global registry). No protocol fork, no over-sink into Axon, no product UI leaking down. The ordering claim ("advertise_agent before advertise_abilities") is a contract constraint owned by Axon, not a backend invention — backend merely obeys it.`

It directly serves the endgame: an Agent addressed by URA, advertised and governed by Axon, consumed by the product.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI already provides the peer of this: src/runtime/advertise.rs (advertise_agent/advertise_abilities for device + hosted Agents) and src/services/invocation_transport/federation_wrappers.rs (proxy_resolve/proxy_list_user_devices — daemon owns cross-hub dial+signing so the Go backend never grows a second transport stack). CLI is correctly the runtime/front-door that signs and forwards; it does NOT need an aggregate.* catalog method (that is hub aggregation, not device runtime). n/a for the aggregate.* logic itself.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD remain the single source of truth for the advertise_agent/advertise_abilities wire shape + the agent-before-abilities ordering invariant — which it already is (FEDERATION_INVOKE_SCHEMAS.md, hub_profile.rs, delegation/membership gates). Axon should NOT grow an aggregate.* ability or a catalog-flatten primitive — that is product/hub projection, not neutral contract; sinking it would pollute the protocol. The only Axon-side ontology note: AXON-RFC-001 mapping already classifies aggregate.* as "backend-profile / UI aggregator", so the boundary is documented.

Backend: registration.go:40-95 (AbilitiesAdvertised + Register, fixed two-call order), aggregator.go (ListAbilitiesCatalog fan-out over federation.resolve, read-only, strict-scope), registration_loop.go (bounded-backoff self-healing retry: bootstrap_self_identity → advertise_agent → advertise_abilities). Contract shipped in Axon (FEDERATION_INVOKE_SCHEMAS.md §1-2, hub_profile.rs). Consumed by Go shim internal/axon/advertise.go. CLI peer shipped at src/runtime/advertise.rs. No fork detected — same verb names and ordering across all three repos.
- **行动：** No layer move required — placement is correct. If anything is added later, the watch item is that aggregate.* must stay read-only graph projection (no orchestration), per aggregator.go strict-scope.

Mirror Axon field names (online, last_seen_unix_ms) verbatim — no frontend-local presence enum fork. ## 界面 Surface / Surface-UI（18）

### 124. ⬜ Ability UI bundle Surface (sandboxed iframe + postMessage bridge)

- **裁决：** `MISSING (belongs in EasyNet frontend, not CLI/Axon). The iframe sandbox, isolated-origin enforcement, and postMessage origin validation are "what the user sees / product surface" rendering — RFC-012 itself assigns it to the frontend (line 757 PR-6 "Bundle runtime in the frontend"; §9.3 "the bridge MUST validate every postMessage's origin"). Pushing the iframe/render machinery into CLI or Axon would be product-UI leaking into a runtime/protocol layer (WRONG). Caveat: the bridge API NAME+VERB contract (bridgeVersion "easynet-bridge-v0"; invoke/readResource/subscribe/emit/getContext/getReceipt) is a thin agreement a polyglot bundle author codes against — that vocabulary should be expressed as the SurfaceManifest schema (Axon-shaped contract), but the renderer is EasyNet.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the literal endgame mechanism by which "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, returns a Surface" produces something the user can actually see and interact with safely. The bundle family lets an ability ship its own UI to the product surface while §9.3 isolation (no host token/cookie/global-state, every bundle invocation surfaced as an auditable receipt) preserves user-owns-identity+audit. Directly serves the user-facing half of the value chain.
- **CLI 预期实现：** n/a for the renderer. CLI's only adjacent role: an ability whose receipt carries a bundle SurfaceManifest must have its bundle assets reachable via ResourceRef URAs that the CLI runtime/executor serves (the bundle assets live on the owner device). CLI does NOT host the iframe, the origin, or the postMessage bridge — that is browser-side. CLI also enforces, on the runtime side, that a bundle-initiated invoke (arriving back through the bridge) is a normal governed invocation producing a receipt.
- **Axon 预期实现：** The SurfaceManifest / HtmlSurface(kind:"bundle") schema as a neutral cross-language type — entry, assets[], hash, sandbox, origin, bridgeVersion, signing, initialState — so any-language ability returns the same manifest shape and any-language renderer agrees on it. Two different-language nodes (a Python ability shipping a bundle, a JS frontend rendering it) must AGREE on this shape, so it is CONTRACT. The bridge verb set (invoke/readResource/subscribe/emit/getContext/getReceipt) should likewise be a named, versioned contract enum. Axon does NOT implement the iframe or bridge; it only fixes the shape.
- **现状：** missing. Spec-only in RFC-012 §2.2 + §4.9 + §9.3 (/Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet/docs/rfc/RFC-012-surface-manifest-v0.md:77-95, 430-454, 695-708). Verified: no SurfaceManifest/iframe/postMessage/SurfaceRuntime implementation in EasyNet/Frontend (grep empty), no Surface types in EasyNet-Cli/src (grep empty; only OS-level process sandbox-exec, unrelated), no SurfaceManifest in EasyNet-Axon/core/proto. RFC roadmap explicitly defers it: line 757 "PR-6 ... (v2.)".
- **行动：** No CLI work now; this is an EasyNet-frontend product feature deferred to RFC-012 PR-6 (v2). Frame F-03 surface (product-surface, lives in EasyNet, not this repo). Next step: (1) land the SurfaceManifest + bundle HtmlSurface schema as a contract type in Axon (frame F-07 ontology-absorption — the manifest shape must agree cross-language before the frontend builds against it; RFC-003 sec0 note: a new Axon proto/SDK type addition needs an Axon-repo RFC entry, do not fork the schema into the frontend ad hoc), then (2) implement the iframe + isolated-origin + postMessage bridge renderer in EasyNet/Frontend per RFC-012 PR-6. CLI side only ensures bundle-asset ResourceRef URAs are servable and bridge-originated invokes stay governed/receipted.

### 125. ⬜ External Local Window Surface family

- **裁决：** `MISSING (three-layer feature, no fork). It is not a protocol fork (no CLI-local copy of the manifest contract exists). It is correctly a three-layer split that simply has not been built: the manifest SHAPE (ExternalWindowSurface as a member of the cross-language SurfaceManifest union) is CONTRACT, the launch/attach of the local window is RUNTIME (CLI), and the status/receipt display is product surface (EasyNet). No new Axon work required for this feature beyond keeping that contract stable; the frontend/CLI must consume it rather than re-implement.

- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI provides the real EXECUTOR backend behind the four device.browser.* verbs already registered as methods (OwnerKind::Device) in src/runtime/agents/browser_session_ability.rs: a wry WebView instance per session URA (1:1), platform capture loop feeding the capture_viewport StreamSource at fixed 10fps, input synthesis in send_input_handler, Semaphore(4) concurrency cap, 30-min idle reaper, per-(realm,userId) WebContext cookie profiles, and a gui-render feature flag so hub/headless builds stay clean. Engine lands behind the existing handler bodies; no new verbs, no registry — stays a method per INV-3.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — Axon is imported, not run on a device; a native per-platform WebView engine has no place in the neutral cross-language contract. The only contract Axon already supplies (URA addressing, invoke->receipt, permission/audit) is untouched: the browser session URA, the invocation receipts, and the (callerUra,abilityUra,domain) permission grant all flow through the existing Axon shape. The browser frame/input wire shape is a SurfaceManifest product protocol owned by EasyNet (RFC-012), not Axon.
- **现状：** partial/missing — the mock handler is SHIPPED in CLI at /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/agents/browser_session_ability.rs (593 lines, four verbs registered, returns a 1x1 placeholder webp, explicitly tagged "[V0 MOCK — NOT YET FUNCTIONAL]"). The real engine is MISSING: wry is not a dependency in any CLI Cargo.toml, and capture/input bodies are stubs (lines 401-403 "v0 emits a single placeholder frame ... RFC-013 W2+ ... drives wry's takeSnapshot/CapturePreview"). Spec is draft only: /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet/docs/rfc/RFC-013-wry-webview-integration-v0.md (Status: draft, per-platform matrix informative).
- **行动：** F-03 surface (CLI-repo EXECUTOR work, not Axon — RFC-003 sec0 note n/a). Next step: keep the contract frozen (RFC-012 frame/input shape unchanged) and implement RFC-013 W1–W8 in CLI behind the existing browser_session_ability.rs handler bodies — add wry as a gui-render-gated dependency, build the wry-main-thread + tokio bridge, wire per-platform capture/input, then enforce the Semaphore(4) cap, idle reaper, and per-(realm,userId) WebContext profiles. Promote RFC-013 from draft only after the macOS WKWebView path passes the existing frontend 21/21 frame-stream tests end-to-end against real pixels. Do NOT add any new verb or any Axon proto change; the wire shape already exists.
