## 内容楔子 / Content-Wedge（12）

### 142. 🟡 easynet api-key create/list/revoke (OpenAI-compat token wedge)

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (partial) — the create/list/revoke lifecycle, sha256-hash store, one-time-show, and local default-token cache are correctly CLI (device-local admission + front-door; no second node must agree on the TOML/hash format). BUT the load-bearing cross-language fact — Authorization: Bearer <id> renders to a user/<username> URA and a resource/api_key.<id> URA (INV-2) — is a contract two languages must agree on, and the CLI builds it via its own crate::ura::user_ura / resource_dot_ura (api_key_ability.rs:142,160,177,202) rather than an Axon URA builder. That URA-construction is a latent protocol fork; the wedge lifecycle itself is correctly CLI.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it is a real front-door that lets any OpenAI-shape client (Cursor/Continue/openai-python) act as user/<username> with receipts attributed to a key URA (RFC-006-C INV-1/INV-4), which serves owner-identity + auditability. But it is a compat/adoption wedge, not the URA-native endgame: the bearer token is a side-channel admission, not a Surface-returning, Axon-governed ability invocation. It buys reach, not the terminal model.
- **CLI 预期实现：** RUNTIME/EXECUTOR/FRONT-DOOR: keep minting/listing/revoking bearer tokens, the sha256-only store, the one-time-show, the ~/.easynet/api_keys.local.toml default cache, and resolve_token() at the openai_compat HTTP boundary (openai_compat_ability.rs:230). This is how THIS device admits an OpenAI-shape client and acts-as the user — correctly CLI. CLI must construct the user/api_key URAs via the Axon URA builder, not a local crate::ura copy.
- **Axon 预期实现：** The neutral URA shapes the bearer resolves to: user/<username> and resource/api_key.<id> as v4.1.5-conformant URA builders any language imports (so a Go hub or Python client building the same Authorization-to-URA mapping agrees byte-for-byte). Axon should NOT own token minting, the local store, hashing, or the cache — those are device admission, not contract. Axon's existing identity.issue_token (node/user/service admin auth) is a different abstraction and must not be conflated with the OpenAI-compat capability key.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI src/cli/api_key_cli.rs:30-177 (verbs) + src/runtime/agents/api_key_ability.rs:122-244 (mint/list/revoke/resolve, store at ~/.easynet/api_keys.toml). URA built via local crate::ura helpers (api_key_ability.rs:142,160). Axon has NO api_key/capability-key contract (grep core/proto/axon/v1: only identity.proto issue_token for node/user/service admin auth). RFC-006-C v0.1 INV-2 lives in CLI docs/rfc, not ratified into an Axon proto.
- **行动：** Keep the api-key wedge in CLI as-is (correct front-door). Single corrective step: replace the local crate::ura::{user_ura,resource_dot_ura} calls in api_key_ability.rs with the Axon URA SDK builder so the user/<username> and resource/api_key.<id> shapes come from the neutral contract, not a CLI copy. Frame F-07 (ontology-absorption): the URA shape absorbs back into Axon; the lifecycle stays CLI. RFC-003 sec0: this is CLI-repo work (swap to Axon import) with a follow-on Axon-repo note to confirm the api_key resource URA is covered by the Axon URA builder surface; do not add an api_key RPC/registry to Axon (respects INV-3: ability is a method, no global registry).

### 143. 🔴 easynet call create/show/join/leave/end/watch/metrics (voice/video wedge)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The call-signalling SHAPE — call/participant/event/end-reason/metrics/SDP-ICE — must be AGREED cross-language for two nodes to interoperate, so it is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon. Axon already defines it canonically in core/proto/axon/v1/voice.proto (VoiceCallView, VoiceCallState 7-enum, CallEvent, VoiceNetworkMetrics, VoiceSessionDescription/IceCandidate) restated as voice.* abilities by AXON-RFC-001 §2. The CLI keeps a parallel hand-rolled serde_json::Value state machine (states "ringing"|"active"|"ended", custom envelopes) that imports none of those types — a protocol fork. The CLI verb surface itself (verb -> one device.voice.* Invoke, federation-forward) is correct front-door/executor; only the handler payload is forked.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — a call IS an ability living on the owner device, invoked by URA and federation-forwarded when credentials are present, which serves the endgame. But signalling state today lives in an in-process Arc<Mutex<HashMap>> with no receipt/audit binding and an ad-hoc JSON shape, so the governable/auditable-by-easynet-verify leg of the value is not yet realized.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the dual-front-door verbs (easynet call create/show/join/leave/end/watch/metrics -> exactly one device.voice.* Invoke) and the local-vs-federation-forward executor decision in invoke_call_signaling. CLI is the RUNTIME hosting the voice.* ability handler on the device and the EXECUTOR that wires SDP/ICE/media path. CLI should NOT define the call/participant/event field shapes or state enum — it should construct and read Axon voice.proto types. Realtime media stays out of the Invoke->Receipt path (DEC-F01-Q9), only signalling rides Invoke.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the neutral contract and already ships it: voice.proto VoiceCallView/CallParticipant/CallEvent/VoiceCallState/VoiceEndReason/VoiceNetworkMetrics/VoiceSessionDescription/VoiceIceCandidate plus the Create/Join/Leave/End/ReportMetrics/WatchCallEvents and transport-session request/response messages, restated as voice.* abilities (AXON-RFC-001 §2). Axon must expose these as generated builder/types the CLI imports so the state enum and envelopes are agreed cross-language, not redefined per node.
- **现状：** forked. Canonical contract shipped at EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/voice.proto:58-469 (and restatement EasyNet-Axon/docs/rfc/AXON-RFC-001-restatement-mapping.md:302-324). CLI fork shipped at EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/agents/voice_call_ability.rs:1-623 (untyped JSON, string states, no voice.proto import) with verbs at src/cli/groups/call.rs:36-322; CLI vendors only schemas/control_plane.proto + common.proto (no voice.proto).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: vendor Axon voice.proto into the CLI proto build (alongside control_plane/common) and rewrite voice_call_ability.rs to construct/read the generated VoiceCallView/VoiceCallState/CallEvent types instead of the hand-rolled JSON state machine, retiring the "ringing|active|ended" string states in favor of VoiceCallState. Keep call.rs verbs and invoke_call_signaling local/forward logic unchanged. Axon-repo side is read-only consume (contract already ratified), so no RFC-003 sec0 reservation needed for Axon; if any voice.proto field gap surfaces during absorption, raise it as an Axon RFC-003 sec0 item before editing the proto.

### 144. 🔴 easynet call signaling content-wedge (create/join/leave/end/watch/metrics)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The call-signaling SHAPE — VoiceCallState transitions, VoiceEndReason, VoiceNetworkMetrics, CallParticipant, SDP/ICE transport negotiation — is a cross-language interop contract and is canonically defined in Axon (core/proto/axon/v1/voice.proto + core/runtime-rs/src/services/voice/* + RFC-001 mapping defining voice.create_call/join_call/etc as abilities). EasyNet's Go SFU consumes exactly that Axon contract (backend/internal/sfuprovider/axon_ability.go via the axon.VoiceSignaller facade). The CLI's src/runtime/agents/voice_call_ability.rs reimplements the SAME state machine freehand: in-memory HashMap, string states "ringing"/"active"/"ended" (vs Axon's 7-state typed enum), magic end_reason: 1 mirroring VOICE_END_CALLER_HANGUP without importing it, and a parallel device.voice.* namespace vs Axon's voice.*. Two different-language nodes must agree on this to interoperate, so the CLI-local copy is a protocol fork. The CLI VERB layer (call.rs) itself is correctly placed; the forked piece is the ability handler.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — call signaling is a legitimate addressable+governable ability (URA-reachable conference/voice ability on the owner device, returns call state, auditable). It serves the endgame as a deployable ability. But media is peer-to-peer WebRTC (correct — media must not pollute the control plane), and the value is only realized when the signaling state machine is the SHARED Axon contract; the CLI's private in-process reimplementation does not interoperate, so the endgame value is only partially delivered.
- **CLI 预期实现：** KEEP the call.rs verb group (create/show/join/leave/end/watch/metrics) — that is the legitimate human front-door (FRONT-DOOR role) and the federation-forward routing in invoke_call_signaling (RUNTIME role) is correct. CLI SHOULD provide: arg parsing, credential/URA resolution, federation-forward-vs-local dispatch, table/JSON rendering. CLI SHOULD NOT own the call state machine — voice_call_ability.rs should be a thin EXECUTOR that maps verbs onto the Axon voice.* contract types (the typed VoiceCallState/VoiceEndReason/VoiceNetworkMetrics from axon-pb), not a private HashMap with its own string enum.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already provides the canonical contract: voice.proto (VoiceCallState, VoiceTransportKind, VoiceEndReason, VoiceNetworkMetrics, CallParticipant, VoiceCallView, SDP/ICE transport messages) and the runtime-rs voice services, with RFC-001 restatement mapping the legacy RPCs to voice.create_call / join_call / leave_call / end_call / report_metrics / watch (event stream) / get_call abilities. This is the shape both the Go SFU and the CLI must bind to. No new Axon contract is needed; Axon should remain the single owner of the signaling state-machine semantics and event/QoS shapes.
- **现状：** forked. Axon: contract shipped at /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/voice.proto and core/runtime-rs/src/services/voice/rpc_calls_lifecycle.rs (+ peers); RFC-001 mapping docs/rfc/AXON-RFC-001-restatement-mapping.md:302-315. EasyNet product: consumes Axon contract at /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet/backend/internal/sfuprovider/axon_ability.go and backend/internal/axon/voice.go (VoiceSignaller facade). CLI: parallel reimplementation at /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Cli/src/runtime/agents/voice_call_ability.rs (in-memory HashMap, string states, no Axon types) behind verbs at /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Cli/src/cli/groups/call.rs.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup — de-fork: reduce CLI src/runtime/agents/voice_call_ability.rs from a private state machine to an EXECUTOR that binds to the Axon voice.* contract types (axon-pb VoiceCallState/VoiceEndReason/VoiceNetworkMetrics) so CLI, Go SFU, and any future node share one wire shape; align the device.voice.* names with Axon's voice.* namespace per RFC-001 mapping. Keep call.rs verbs as-is. The contract already lives in Axon, so this is CLI-repo runtime work, NOT an Axon-repo change — no RFC-003 sec0 note required. If a single-node local fallback must persist for offline use, it must still emit the Axon-typed shapes (no "ringing"/"active"/"ended" string fork).

### 145. 🔴 Pages content-wedge CLI + daemon ability (publish/list/get/unpublish/fetch/api)

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The CLI verbs + daemon hosting of folder bytes with MIME inference + sandbox are correctly CLI (this is how the device runs/exposes content — EXECUTOR + FRONT-DOOR, keep). However the page.fetch / api.<verb> RESPONSE ENVELOPE — fetch {bytes_b64, content_type, size_bytes, force_attachment, sha256} (fetch.rs:80-86) and api {status, body, content_type} (api.rs §, serve.go:212-237) — is a cross-language wire contract: the Rust daemon emits it and the Go hub must decode it byte-for-byte to serve. It is hand-rolled independently on both sides (and a THIRD time in backend/internal/handler/openai/files.go:141-168), with no shared Axon definition. By the boundary criterion, two different-language nodes must AGREE on this shape to interoperate, so it is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon; the per-repo copies are a protocol fork. But it is PARTIAL not YES because the thing actually delivered to the user is product surface served at https://realm/web/<user>/<project>/ by EasyNet, and that loop currently rides an unsigned hub-as-Caller carve-out (serve.go:156-172 DEC-EU wire-break) and a fetch/api response envelope that is NOT in any neutral contract — so the "governed by Axon, auditable" half of the value is not yet real.

CLI should CONSUME an Axon-defined content-envelope type for the fetch/api return instead of constructing the json! map by hand, so its emitted shape is the contract, not a local convention.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should define the neutral content-fetch envelope as a contract type (proto/SDK): the read-projection shape {bytes_b64, content_type, size_bytes, force_attachment, sha256} and the dynamic-api shape {status, body, content_type}, alongside the already-present resource URA grammar. This is the shape both the Rust daemon (producer) and the Go hub (consumer) — plus openai/files.go — import rather than re-type. Axon does NOT host the folder, run the sandbox, infer MIME, or expose CLI verbs; those stay CLI. Axon only owns the agreed-upon byte-envelope shape.
- **现状：** partial/forked. Shipped CLI verbs: src/cli/pages.rs:44-113. Shipped daemon abilities: src/runtime/agents/pages/fetch.rs:52-106 (fetch), api.rs:69-115 (api manifests), publish.rs, list_get_unpublish.rs, state.rs, sandbox.rs, mime.rs. Shipped hub consumer: EasyNet backend/internal/handler/pages_public/serve.go:57-256. Forked seam: the fetch/api response envelope is duplicated across fetch.rs:80-86, serve.go:212-237, and backend/internal/handler/openai/files.go:141-168 with no Axon source-of-truth. URA contract is correctly shared (Axon proto + backend/internal/axon/urns.go round-trips it).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: lift the page.fetch read-projection envelope {bytes_b64, content_type, size_bytes, force_attachment, sha256} and the api envelope {status, body, content_type} into an Axon content-envelope contract type (proto + Rust/Go SDK), then have fetch.rs, api.rs, serve.go, and openai/files.go import it instead of re-typing. Leave all CLI verbs, sandbox, MIME, and daemon hosting in CLI untouched (they are correct EXECUTOR/FRONT-DOOR). RFC-003 sec0 note: this is Axon-repo work — it adds a new shared content-envelope contract to the wire surface, so it must land as an RFC-003 sec0 protocol-addition entry (not a CLI-local change) before the per-repo forks are collapsed onto it.

### 146. ✅ API-key content-wedge (OpenAI-compat bearer token mint/list/revoke)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (split is right, nothing forked into Axon). The two halves each sit in their proper layer: (a) key mint/list/revoke is an ability method on the user daemon — CLI RUNTIME/EXECUTOR, and the CLI verb is a thin FRONT-DOOR over invoke_local_ability; (b) /v1/chat/completions + /v1/models is the product front door the user/Cursor sees — EasyNet hub. The api_key bearer *shape* itself (token entropy, sha256 storage, OpenAI-compat header) does NOT need cross-language agreement — the daemon's local api_key store is deliberately independent of the hub's ent table (each re-validates). So there is no protocol fork: the api_key family is correctly CLI-local, the wedge surface is correctly EasyNet-product. No over-sink into Axon, no product-UI leak into CLI/Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it serves the endgame indirectly: the bearer resolves to a user-rooted URA (easynet:///r/<realm>/resource/api_key.<id>), the ability lives on the owner device (registered as <user>.api_key.* via register_rpc_with_owner), and dispatch goes owner-side through the Axon invoker. But the OpenAI-compat /v1 surface is a content-wedge / adoption ramp (talk-to-Cursor/sandbox), not the SurfaceManifest+URA+easynet-verify endgame. It funds adoption rather than expressing the owned-identity thesis directly — hence PARTIAL not YES.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Provide the ability methods <user>.api_key.{create,list,revoke} on the daemon (RUNTIME), the local sha256-hashed bearer store + atomic 0600 TOML, the daemon-side adapter ability that the hub's Axon invoke targets (Subject = api_key URA), and the `easynet api-key` verb as a thin human FRONT-DOOR. Shipped at src/cli/api_key_cli.rs and src/runtime/agents/api_key_ability.rs.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the api_key bearer shape — it is intentionally CLI-local and need not be a neutral cross-language contract. Axon's only relevant contribution is the URA grammar the bearer resolves into (easynet:///r/<realm>/resource/api_key.<id>), which already exists in Axon SDK (sdk/rust/src/ura.rs, sdk/go/easynet/ura.go). Do NOT sink api_key minting/storage into Axon — that would pollute the neutral protocol with a product-adoption concern.
- **现状：** shipped on both ends. CLI: api_key_cli.rs:90-177 (verbs) + api_key_ability.rs:290-308 (register_rpc_with_owner for create/list/revoke), hashed-token TOML store at ~/.easynet/api_keys.toml. Hub: custom_routes.go:372-396 (/v1/chat/completions, /v1/models routes) + handler/openai/chat_completions.go (resolveBearer → ent.ApiKey → user_id → Axon invoker → daemon adapter, SSE projection). Conformance tagged RFC-006-C v0.1 INV-2. No Axon-side api_key code exists outside legacy example prototype (examples/legacy/16-easynet-backend-prototype) and node_modules — correctly absent.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — placement is correct as split. F-03 surface: leave the api_key family in CLI and the /v1 wedge in EasyNet. Cleanup-only (F-05): confirm the hub never forwards the raw bearer to the daemon (chat_completions.go comment at :185 claims it does not — verify the InvokeRequest envelope carries only the api_key URA Subject + signed Delegation, not the bearer) so the CLI-local store stays the sole token authority. RFC-003 sec0: n/a — no Axon-repo work; do not open an Axon change for api_key (would be over-sink).

### 148. ✅ device.skill.* (skill content abilities: install/list/publish/unpublish/read_file/write_file/tree/remove/upgrade)

This is the content-wedge realization of "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, governed by Axon, auditable." It directly advances the endgame: a skill is owned content on the user's device, surfaced and audited like any other ability.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI SHOULD provide (and does): the device-local skill byte store, the install/publish/tree/read_file/write_file/upgrade/remove/list executors, agent-scoped path resolution (.claude/skills vs .agents/skills vs legacy skills/), install provenance (.easynet/install.json), and install-time skill_tree_hash for upgrade-diff/integrity. These are RUNTIME/EXECUTOR concerns. Implemented: src/runtime/skill_store.rs (1083 lines), skill_publish_ability.rs (1362), skill_install_ability.rs (333), skill_ability.rs (273), with tests in real_invoke_tests.rs.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon SHOULD provide (and does) ONLY the neutral contract pieces these abilities ride on: the resource URA grammar (resource_dot_ura / resource_ura, parse_ura) so any-language node addresses a skill package identically, plus the receipt/invocation envelope and the AXIOM §6.1 Q6 ability_snapshot.content_hash (invocation-time callee attestation). Axon must NOT grow a "skill" type, skill registry, or content-store API — skills are CLI content, not protocol. The store's content_hash is correctly documented as NOT Q6 (skill_store.rs:45-60).
- **现状：** shipped — 9 device.skill.*.ability.toml in abilities/system/ (evidence said 12; actual count is 9 device.skill.* plus a2a.bridge.list_skills which is a separate a2a ability). Backed by ~3050 lines of runtime impl (src/runtime/skill_store.rs, src/runtime/agents/skill_{ability,publish_ability,install_ability}.rs) and routed/tested in src/runtime/agents/real_invoke_tests.rs. URA built via Axon SDK (verified in EasyNet-Axon ura.rs). docs/cli-command-review-2026-05-30.md:765 marks skill install/list/upgrade/remove SHIPPED.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — placement is correct and shipped. (F-03 surface) Two light follow-ups, both CLI-local, neither re-litigates ratified anchors: (1) reconcile the accounting count — evidence claims 12 device.skill.* entries but the directory has 9 (the other is a2a.bridge.list_skills); update the wedge ledger to 9 to avoid phantom inventory. (2) The review doc (docs/cli-command-review-2026-05-30.md:770) flags an asymmetry: install is a one-way github: fetch with no matching self-serve publish loop, and (:503,:578) notes Skill=PRIVATE visibility is an honor system because visibility/authorized_agents are not in proto/runtime — that governance gap IS a genuine cross-language contract item and belongs to Axon (RFC-003 sec0: file as an Axon-repo ontology item to add ability visibility PUBLIC/PRIVATE/SCOPED + authorized_agents to the capability proto, so the Skill=private-ability encapsulation is structurally enforced rather than CLI-honor-system). The byte-handling abilities themselves stay CLI.

### 149. ✅ easynet llm-api OpenAI-shape chat client

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI) for the verb, with a FORKED contract dependency underneath. The `easynet llm-api` verb is exactly a DUAL-FRONT-DOOR concern: a human CLI verb that wraps a device-local ability invoke (pick_model -> list_models, build request -> chat_completions). That is how this device exposes a runtime capability to a human; keeping it in CLI is right and sinking it into Axon would over-sink product/runtime. HOWEVER the OpenAI `chat.completion` wire envelope it depends on (openai.chat_completions, RFC-006-C v0.1) is a cross-language contract that external nodes (Cursor/Continue/any client) must AGREE on, and it exists ONLY in CLI (openai_compat_ability.rs, mod.rs) with no neutral Axon definition (Axon has zero chat_completion SDK/proto; only opaque `hub.openai.chat_completions` name strings in hub-profile tests). That adapter shape is a protocol fork living in CLI. The verb stays CLI; the wire-shape contract underneath should be anchored in Axon.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it makes a device-owned chat ability reachable from any terminal via the same OpenAI path Cursor/Continue use, which serves the "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA" endgame. But the verb itself returns raw OpenAI text/JSON, not a governed Surface + Receipt, and resolves keys from env/local cache rather than through identity/audit — so it does not yet deliver the owns-identity/audit/Surface part of the endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the `easynet llm-api` verb as a thin human front-door: parse args, resolve model via list_models, resolve key, invoke the device-local OpenAI executor adapter, print text/JSON. CLI also legitimately owns the EXECUTOR that maps an EasyNet chat-base ability onto the OpenAI wire shape (openai_compat_ability.rs is correct as a device-local executor/adapter). CLI should NOT be the place that defines the canonical OpenAI envelope schema other languages must match.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon should provide the neutral, cross-language definition of the OpenAI-compat chat contract shape — the request/response envelope (messages, model, choices[].message.content, usage) as an importable contract/builder + the ability-name convention (`*.openai.chat_completions` / `list_models`) — so the CLI executor and any other-language node project the SAME shape instead of each forking RFC-006-C locally. Axon does NOT run the call; it only fixes the shape both ends agree on.
- **现状：** shipped in CLI: verb at src/cli/llm_api.rs:54-134; device-local executor abilities at src/runtime/agents/openai_compat_ability.rs:467-472 and src/runtime/agents/mod.rs:1282-1289,1435-1449; tests at src/runtime/agents/real_invoke_tests.rs:2620-2653. forked contract: OpenAI wire shape defined only in CLI (RFC-006-C in docs/), MISSING in Axon — Axon has no chat_completion proto/SDK builder, only opaque `hub.openai.chat_completions` name strings in core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/hub_profile.rs:1825 and state/hub_published_abilities.rs:220.
- **行动：** Leave the `easynet llm-api` verb and the device-local OpenAI executor in CLI as-is (F-03 surface: correct front-door, no move). Open an Axon-repo item to absorb the OpenAI-compat chat envelope + ability-name convention from RFC-006-C into a neutral Axon contract (builder/schema), then have openai_compat_ability.rs consume it instead of defining the shape locally (F-07 ontology-absorption, mirrors F-01 sec7 Cli-runs-ahead / Axon-absorbs-back). RFC-003 sec0 note: the Axon-side work is a new neutral-contract definition (adapter wire shape), must not pollute Receipt and must not introduce provider-matching/registry (INV-3) — it is a shape contract only, not a runtime.

### 150. ✅ easynet pages create/list/show/delete/url (content wedge)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (split is clean across all three layers, with one absorption gap). Boundary check: the bits two different-language nodes must AGREE on — the resource-URA grammar (resource/<owner-id>, types.proto:52-57, README.md:169) and the user/resource owner-kind ontology (ONTOLOGY_AGENT_ABILITY.md:156-187) — already live in Axon as neutral contract, NOT forked into CLI. The bits that are how-this-device-runs-and-exposes (open folder fd, in-process HTTP listener, hot-register page.fetch, the <user>.pages.{publish,list,get,unpublish} executor abilities, the human CLI verbs) correctly sit in CLI. The bit the user sees (browser fetch routed by the hub) correctly sits in EasyNet (serve.go forward_invokes the ability — consumes CLI, not Axon). No product UI leaks into CLI/Axon. One real gap, not a fork: CLI's OwnerKind enum (src/runtime/ability_dispatch.rs:560-583) is missing the Resource variant that Axon already names; CLI registers pages under OwnerKind::User as a stand-in, so the catalog cannot yet represent the resource-rooted owner of page.fetch.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — it is the endgame in miniature: static bytes live on the owner's device (folder fd opened by the daemon, src/runtime/agents/pages/publish.rs), get a resource URA (easynet:///r/<realm>/resource/<user>.<project_id>/), become reachable via a hot-registered <user>.<project_id>.page.fetch ability, and EasyNet's Hub returns the bytes as a browser surface at /web/<user>/<project_id>/ — owner identity + URA addressing + ability-on-owner-device + product surface, all wired.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this feature: RUNTIME (daemon opens the folder fd + runs the in-process listener that makes the page.fetch URA reachable), EXECUTOR (the <user>.pages.{publish,list,get,unpublish} ability handlers and per-project page.fetch/api hot-registration in src/runtime/agents/pages/), and FRONT DOOR (the human `easynet pages create/list/show/delete/url` verbs in src/cli/pages.rs). CLI should additionally carry the Resource OwnerKind variant once Axon's owner-kind ontology is mirrored, and register page.fetch under it instead of borrowing OwnerKind::User.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon provides the contract SHAPE only: (1) the resource-URA grammar easynet:///r/<realm>/resource/<owner-id>/... (types.proto, README.md, URAKind::Resource in sdk/rust/src/ura.rs:687); (2) the owner-kind ontology that names `user` and `resource` as first-class ability owners (document/concepts/ONTOLOGY_AGENT_ABILITY.md:156-187). Axon must NOT contain a pages registry, folder serving, or publish logic — those are device runtime, correctly absent from Axon (grep confirms no pages.publish/page.fetch handlers in Axon, only ontology docs).
- **现状：** shipped in CLI: src/cli/pages.rs:44-303 (verbs), src/runtime/agents/pages/{mod,publish,fetch,api,list_get_unpublish,state,sandbox,mime}.rs (runtime/executor). shipped in EasyNet: backend/internal/handler/pages_public/serve.go (hub surface forward_invokes page.fetch). contract shipped in Axon as ontology+grammar docs (ONTOLOGY_AGENT_ABILITY.md:156-187, types.proto:52-57) and URAKind::Resource (ura.rs:687). partial gap: CLI OwnerKind enum (ability_dispatch.rs:560-583) lacks the Resource variant Axon already defines — pages registers under User as a stand-in (mod.rs:131).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: add the `Resource(String)` variant to CLI's OwnerKind (src/runtime/ability_dispatch.rs:560-583) mirroring Axon's owner-kind ontology, and re-register the per-project page.fetch/api abilities under OwnerKind::Resource(format!("{user}.{project_id}")) in src/runtime/agents/pages/{fetch,api}.rs instead of inheriting the management abilities' OwnerKind::User. This absorbs back the ontology Axon already ratified (ONTOLOGY_AGENT_ABILITY.md §resource) and removes the User stand-in. No Axon-repo change needed (contract already complete), so no RFC-003 sec0 note required; this is CLI-local absorption work.

### 151. ✅ easynet skill-install (skill content wedge)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). Apply the boundary criterion: (a) Cross-language agreement? No — the SKILL.md files are host-client prompt templates (Claude Code / Codex format), not a wire contract; no second-language node must agree on them to interoperate, so this is NOT a protocol-fork-into-CLI case. (b) Product UI? No — it writes dotfiles, renders no Surface, no hub routing, so it does not belong in EasyNet. (c) It is precisely how THIS device exposes EasyNet to the host AI agent — front-door enrollment. That is CLI FRONT-DOOR. Note the distinct namesake `skill.install` ability (src/runtime/skill_store.rs, src/runtime/agents/skill_install_ability.rs) is the contract-governed ability-bundle installer EasyNet backend invokes — different feature; do not conflate. This facade verb is the local template-copy and is correctly CLI-local.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it serves the endgame indirectly: copying SKILL.md prompt templates into ~/.claude/skills and ~/.agents/skills is what makes the host AI client (Claude Code/Codex) discover EasyNet's MCP front door and reach for the collaborate/invoke abilities. It is an onboarding wedge for the dual front door, not itself any of identity/URA/Surface/receipt/audit. It greases adoption of the value chain rather than delivering a link in it.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI SHOULD own this: it is FRONT-DOOR onboarding — making the host AI client discover EasyNet's MCP server by planting prompt skills on this device. Belongs alongside `mcp serve/install`. Reasonable hardening: have it bundle the skills into the binary (include_dir) rather than relying on find_bundled_skills_dir walking cwd/exe-relative paths (skill_install.rs:149-193 is fragile for an installed binary), and converge its target-resolution with `mcp install` so the two front-door enrollers share one notion of "which host client, where".
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a contract. The SKILL.md templates are host-client (Claude Code/Codex) prompt artifacts, not a neutral cross-language wire shape; no two different-language nodes must agree on them to interoperate. Sinking this into Axon would be over-sink (polluting the neutral protocol with one device's host-client packaging). Axon's only legitimate adjacent concept is A2ASkillSpec in proto/axon/v1/types.proto (abilities auto-exposed as A2A skills) — a different, contract-level notion of "skill" that must not be confused with this prompt-template copier.
- **现状：** shipped — src/cli/skill_install.rs:47-237 (full run/list/install path); wired through src/cli/skill.rs and src/cli/mod.rs; bundled templates at skills/ (easynet-ability-author, easynet-author, easynet-collaborate, easynet-deploy, easynet-pages-author); listed SHIPPED in docs/cli-command-review-2026-05-30.md:785.
- **行动：** Keep in CLI as front-door onboarding; no layer move. Frame F-03 (surface/front-door): (1) embed skills into the binary via include_dir! so installed binaries stop relying on the cwd/exe path walk at skill_install.rs:149-193; (2) unify host-client target resolution with `mcp install` so both front-door enrollers agree on client→dir mapping. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 does not apply.

### 152. ✅ Pages reference HTTP listener (daemon in-process)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — by the boundary criterion this is RUNTIME/transport: how this device exposes its served bytes over a local HTTP socket. It is not a thing two different-language nodes must agree on; the cross-language agreement point is the ability SHAPE (01HUB.pages.serve / <user>.<project>.page.fetch), which both the CLI listener (pages_serve_ability) and the Go backend (pages_public/serve.go forward_invokes page.fetch) speak. The listener is a swappable transport in front of that contract, so it is not a protocol fork. Port-probe + control.json write is pure device-local orchestration. Keeping it in CLI is right; sinking an HTTP listener into Axon (which is imported, never runs on a device) would be wrong, and the product surface correctly lives in EasyNet.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it makes a project's served bytes reachable over HTTP on the owner device (ability lives on owner device, returns a Surface), which serves the endgame; but it is explicitly the dev-local MVP boundary (127.0.0.1, RFC-006-B v0.6 comment at pages_listener.rs:11-16), not the production path users actually see — that is the Go backend wildcard at *.*.pages.easynet.run.
- **CLI 预期实现：** YES — CLI owns this. Daemon spawns the in-process listener, probes EASYNET_PAGES_PORT..+20, writes the chosen port to control.json discovery so the start verb can print the final URL (start.rs:198-223). This is exactly RUNTIME (daemon making a URA/surface reachable) plus EXECUTOR (Host-header → pages.serve ability dispatch). Correctly placed at src/runtime/hub/pages_listener.rs and src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:321-397.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the listener itself — Axon is imported, never runs on a device, so it must not host an HTTP listener. What Axon SHOULD own is the neutral CONTRACT SHAPE the listener fronts: the pages.serve / page.fetch ability signature and its Surface/bytes return shape, so the CLI dev listener and the Go production listener provably return identical bytes (RFC-006-B INV-3 deterministic projection). Today that shape is asserted only by per-repo conformance comments, not a shared Axon definition.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI: spawn at src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:321-360, resolve_pages_start_port at :381-397, DEFAULT_PAGES_LISTENER_PORT=8787 at :67; listener + spawn_first_available + DEFAULT_PORT_PROBE_SPAN=20 at src/runtime/hub/pages_listener.rs:41-103; port persisted via discovery (src/services/control/discovery.rs:96, server.rs:466) and surfaced in start.rs:198-223. Production counterpart shipped separately in EasyNet at backend/internal/handler/pages_public/serve.go.
- **行动：** No move — keep the listener in CLI as-is (F-05 cleanup only). The one real gap is the cross-language contract: lift the pages.serve / page.fetch ability+Surface SHAPE that the comment at pages_listener.rs:13-16 promises ("keeps the same 01HUB.pages.serve ability shape") into a single Axon-defined ability/Surface contract so the dev listener and the Go backend cannot drift (F-07 ontology-absorption). RFC-003 sec0: that Axon-repo work is contract-shape only (ability signature + Surface return), it must not pull any HTTP-listener/transport runtime into Axon.

### 153. ✅ Skill content-wedge (search/installed/tree/file/install/upgrade/remove)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — boundary criterion applied strictly: this is device package-management (clone github source, hash tree, write under <agent>/skills/, list/browse/remove) = EXECUTOR/RUNTIME, so it belongs in CLI. It is NOT a cross-language agreement: nothing in Axon proto defines a device.skill.* message/RPC; the Axon hits are doc comments (rpc_handlers.rs:1092, runtime_admin.rs:66), a URA example string (client-sdk/src/domain/ura.rs:30), an RFC table row, and reference MCP examples — documentation, not a forked contract. Axon's real skill concept (A2ASkillSpec / A2A_SKILL, types.proto:757/804/1135) is a SEPARATE legitimate interop primitive (ability auto-exposed as agent skill card) and must not be conflated with the content-wedge. No protocol fork, no over-sink, no product UI leaking into CLI/Axon. The "cli+axon" label overstates Axon: footprint is doc-only.`
- **当前所在：** cli+axon
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — skills are abilities-as-content that live on the owner's agent device, are addressed by URA (easynet:///.../ability/skill.tree), travel as signed Invoke→Receipt, and surface as a product Surface (SkillsPage). It directly serves "ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, governed/audited." The content the user installs IS the ability inventory of their owned agent.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this fully: (1) EXECUTOR = runtime/skill_store.rs filesystem package store behind device.skill.{install,upgrade,remove,list,tree,read_file,write_file,search} daemon abilities; (2) FRONT-DOOR = cli/skill.rs verbs (--json for machine consumers) plus the same abilities reachable over MCP. CLI maps terminal args / Invoke to the runtime store and renders. This is correct as-is.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the content-wedge itself — Axon should provide ONLY the neutral transport it already does (Invoke→Receipt two-RPC surface, URA addressing, delegation/audit), not a device.skill.* message shape. The doc-comment references in Axon are acceptable as illustrative URA examples. Axon's separate, legitimately-owned skill contract is A2ASkillSpec/A2A_SKILL (ability→agent-skill-card interop); keep that distinct and do not extend it to cover package management.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI: cli/skill.rs:27-100 + skill_install.rs + runtime/skill_store.rs (full store, real_invoke_tests.rs covers install/remove/upgrade/list/tree/read_file). Hub: EasyNet/backend/internal/handler/skill/*.go (8 handlers) wired at custom_routes.go:315-350, logic dispatches signed Invoke skill.install (installSkillLogic.go:91). Product: EasyNet/Frontend/src/pages/easynet/SkillsPage.tsx (+ test). Axon: doc-only references, no contract type.
- **行动：** No layer move required — feature is correctly placed across CLI(executor/front-door)+EasyNet(surface) on Axon's generic transport. F-05 cleanup only: correct the "cli+axon" classification to "cli (+ easynet surface)" in the catalog, and confirm Axon carries no device.skill.* contract obligation (it does not). Optionally add a one-line note in the Axon RFC/URA examples clarifying that device.skill.* is a CLI executor namespace illustrated for URA shape, NOT an Axon-defined contract, to prevent a future over-sink. RFC-003 sec0 note: n/a — no Axon-repo code change needed; this is a catalog reclassification, not transport-plane work.

## 运行时守护进程 / Runtime-Daemon（15）

### 154. 🔴 Identity-key holder / self-identity bootstrap

- **裁决：** `FORKED — the boot orchestration is correctly CLI, but the key-DERIVATION math is duplicated. CLI's derive_subject_keypair (src/runtime/publish.rs:547-566) reimplements Axon SDK's AxonClient::derive_subject_auth (EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/client-sdk/src/domain/easynet/semantic.rs:87-105) byte-for-byte: identical DERIVE_CONTEXT="axon-client-sdk-ed25519-v1", identical SHA256(tenant:subject:context) -> ed25519 seed. The runtime's verify_easynet_subject_key_binding (security.rs:537,931) only admits an Invoke when the registered key equals the key the SDK signs under — so this derivation is a cross-language CONTRACT both sides MUST agree on. A CLI-local copy of it is a protocol fork: if Axon ever rotates the derive context or subject-id grammar, the CLI silently desyncs and every signed Invoke breaks. The bootstrap RPC itself (runtime.bootstrap_self_identity) is correctly in Axon (runtime_admin.rs:190,393-399); only the derivation function is forked.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the gate that makes "user owns identity key on owner device" real. The daemon holding the node identity and minting trusted-key material is precisely what makes a signed Invoke from this device admissible; without it the runtime returns AXON_EASYNET_SUBJECT_KEY_UNREGISTERED (security.rs:606,643) and the device is unaddressable/un-invocable. Identity-bearing daemon is the literal mechanism behind owner-device addressability + auditability.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI keeps the RUNTIME role: boot the node identity from credentials.json (EASYNET_NODE_ID, easynet-daemon.rs:140-144), decide WHEN to bootstrap (once per runtime lifetime, before any signed Invoke — start.rs:510-537, also heartbeat.rs:355, invocation_transport/boot.rs:1191), register both agent and hub subject keys (publish.rs:409-465), and surface failure to the operator. CLI should CALL the Axon SDK's derivation, not own a copy of it. The orchestration/lifecycle is correctly CLI; the algorithm is not.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon owns the derivation contract AND must EXPORT it as a callable SDK function the CLI imports (it already exists as AxonClient::derive_subject_auth / default_auth_for_subject / derive_owner_auth in semantic.rs:87-123, and the runtime.bootstrap_self_identity RPC + verify_easynet_subject_key_binding in runtime_admin.rs:190 / security.rs:537). The gap: the SDK derivation returns EasyNetUserAuth with URL_SAFE_NO_PAD public key (semantic.rs:100) while the runtime admin RPC stores STANDARD base64; Axon should expose a stable accessor that yields the STANDARD-b64 public key + seed so CLI can register and mirror into the keyring without re-deriving. Subject-id grammar for the agent (easynet:prv:reg:agent.<node>) and hub (easynet:prv:hub:<realm>) subjects must also be SDK-owned, not string-built in CLI (publish.rs:503,515).
- **现状：** shipped but forked. Working end-to-end: CLI bootstrap orchestration (src/runtime/publish.rs:403-472; src/cli/start.rs:510-537; src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:140-144), Axon RPC handler (EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/runtime_admin.rs:190,393-399), Axon verification (security.rs:537,606,643). Forked artifact: CLI src/runtime/publish.rs:547-566 duplicates Axon client-sdk semantic.rs:87-105 derivation with the same protocol constant.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup: replace CLI's local derive_subject_keypair (publish.rs:547-566) and the subject-id string builders (publish.rs:503,515) with calls into the Axon client-sdk (depend on easynet_axon::...::AxonClient::derive_subject_auth and a STANDARD-b64 accessor). Step 1 (Axon repo, RFC-003 sec0 note: touches the neutral cross-language identity contract — flag in sec0 before editing client-sdk): add/confirm an SDK accessor that returns (seed, standard_b64_public_key) plus the canonical agent/hub subject-id constructors, with a test pinning byte-equivalence to the existing derive_subject_auth so no trust anchors invalidate. Step 2 (CLI repo): swap publish.rs to consume it, delete the forked copy, keep a characterization test asserting the bootstrapped key still byte-matches the bridge's signing key (the AXON_EASYNET_SUBJECT_KEY_UNREGISTERED guard). Do NOT move the boot orchestration or RPC invocation out of CLI — those are correctly RUNTIME.

### 155. 🟡 runtime-dispatch UDS responder sidecar

- **裁决：** `MISPLACED-should-be-Axon (partial) — the responder ROLE (bind socket, accept loop, run the ability, stale-socket recovery, dual-socket split from control.sock) is correctly CLI EXECUTOR plumbing. But the WIRE FRAME it speaks ({mode,tool_name,function_name,arguments_b64} request, {ok,result_b64,content_type|code,message} RPC reply, and the {kind:snapshot|progress|done|error,frame} stream grammar) is a contract two parties must AGREE on to interoperate, yet it is hand-coded as raw JSON literals on BOTH sides — EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/interop_native/execution.rs:2116 emits it and EasyNet-Cli runtime_dispatch.rs:92-135,400-577 mirrors it — with NO shared proto or SDK type. That is a protocol fork: the agreement shape belongs in Axon (proto/SDK), the CLI keeps only the responder runtime.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it is the wire that makes a daemon-registered ability (runtime_local_tool) actually reachable when axon-runtime resolves an external Invoke to this device, i.e. it makes the URA reachable/executable (endgame: ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA). But it carries no identity/permission/audit of its own — it is pure transport plumbing under the gRPC Invoke that already governs. So it serves the value as a transport substrate, not as a governance-bearing surface.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the responder runtime: bind/own ~/.easynet/runtime-dispatch.sock (separate from control.sock), accept loop, per-connection serve_one, stale-socket recovery, register dispatch_endpoint=ipc://... via runtime.register_local_tool, map AbilityProxy results/errors to the codec, fail-soft (log, never tear down daemon). It should DESERIALIZE/SERIALIZE the dispatch frame via a shared Axon-provided type instead of hand-rolled serde structs + json! literals.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Own the dispatch-frame contract as a neutral, cross-language artifact: a single source-of-truth schema (proto message or versioned SDK struct) for the request {mode,tool_name,function_name,arguments_b64}, the RPC reply {ok,result_b64,content_type|code,message}, and the stream envelope grammar {kind:snapshot|progress|done|error,frame}. axon-runtime's interop_native/execution.rs dialer and any other-language daemon import it instead of the two parties hand-copying JSON literals. (The dial/UDS transport itself stays in axon-runtime, which is the runtime executor side, not the importable SDK.)
- **现状：** shipped but forked — responder shipped at EasyNet-Cli/src/services/control/runtime_dispatch.rs (wired in src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:304-319, fail-soft confirmed), with full RPC+stream tests. Dialer shipped at EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/interop_native/execution.rs:2078-2179. Frame schema exists in NEITHER proto nor SDK — grep for arguments_b64/result_b64 in Axon hits only runtime-rs source/tests + RFC-004 prose (which documents the federation envelope, not the local UDS dispatch frame or the stream kind grammar). No global registry / provider-matching: registration installs concrete <agent>.<verb> methods directly, honoring INV-3.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — making a hot-added agent's <agent>.chat/discover/invoke URA actually reachable on the live daemon (not just on next restart) is exactly the RUNTIME promise: ability lives on owner device, addressed by URA, online without a reboot. The test pins the load-bearing property (after register_agent, runtime.has_ability("liangbing.chat")==true) that the dispatcher Phase-4 arm and session-receive Axon arm gate on, and notes the pre-fix bug where chats bypassed the wired LedgerSink so invocations.redb stayed empty — i.e. this also restores auditability.
- **CLI 预期实现：** The shared late-wired cell + register_agent/unregister_agent: build the canonical <agent>.chat/discover/invoke triple plus TOML-declared <agent>.<verb> handlers, install/replace/remove them in the live LocalRuntime via Axon's CRUD API, manage the boot-order gap (pending construct, set_runtime once), and keep the agents.json disk write authoritative for restart pickup. Exactly what is shipped.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Only the neutral runtime CRUD contract — register_ability / replace_ability (sole upsert path) / unregister_ability_by_prefix / has_ability / subscribe_ability_changes(AbilityChangeEvent) on LocalRuntime. Already provided. Axon must NOT know about agents.json, AgentEntry, boot ordering, or the chat/discover/invoke triple — those are CLI concepts.
- **现状：** shipped — registrar at src/daemon/axon_bridge/hot_agent_registrar.rs (replace-capable register, prefix unregister, runtime_not_ready boot-window handling, 4 invariant tests); cell constructed in src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:213-214 and passed into build_registry_for_daemon at :225; late-wired via registrar.set_runtime in src/services/invocation_transport/boot.rs:469; lifecycle handlers consume it in src/runtime/agents/agent_lifecycle_ability.rs:84-461. Axon contract shipped in EasyNet-Axon sdk/rust/src/invocation/local_runtime.rs with tests under sdk/rust/tests/ability_crud*.rs.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — keep in CLI as-is. F-05 cleanup only: (1) the runtime_not_ready boot-window is a real (brief) hole — an agent started in the gap between build_registry_with_services and set_runtime only lands on disk and is invisible until restart; consider draining a pending-registration queue inside set_runtime so in-window starts auto-land once the runtime attaches, rather than relying solely on restart pickup. (2) Confirm the boot-side set_runtime call (boot.rs:469) cannot race a concurrent agent.start such that an early register no-ops AFTER set_runtime conceptually should have applied. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 n/a.

### 165. ✅ Loop controller install + inflight resume

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (belongs in CLI). By the boundary criterion this is how THIS device runs/orchestrates/recovers an ability: KernelLoopInvocationDriver wires the loop to the local Kernel (RUNTIME/EXECUTOR), and resume_inflight() re-arms Pending/Running loops from the tenant-bound LoopStore (daemon-restart recovery). Nothing here must be agreed cross-language — each iteration consumes an Axon Receipt; the loop itself is single-device iterate-until-verify orchestration, which the thesis names "CLI orchestration language, not protocol." Not Axon's Mission (that is multi-node DAG dispatch). No protocol fork, no over-sink, no UI leak.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — the loop is an owner-device ability (loop.* on the local node) that iterates body/verify through Kernel::invoke and returns Receipts; disk-backed resume keeps the ability reachable/durable across daemon restarts, which is exactly the "ability lives on owner device, survives, auditable" endgame.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this fully: LoopService (cache + disk store + driver slot), KernelLoopInvocationDriver building per-iter Invocations and routing through Kernel::invoke, install_driver() at boot, bind(tenant) for disk persistence, and resume_inflight() to re-arm in-flight loops on restart. This is the RUNTIME+EXECUTOR responsibility — correct here.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the loop machinery itself. Axon's only contract surface touched is the per-iteration boundary: Invocation -> Receipt (TerminalState) and session_events, which already exist (RFC-001). The loop must NOT push its LoopState machine or resume semantics into Axon — that would over-sink CLI orchestration into the neutral protocol.
- **现状：** shipped. Boot wiring at src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:139-156 (install_driver + resume_inflight) with tenant store bind at :131-137; LoopService.install_driver / bind / resume_inflight at src/daemon/execution/loop_instance/mod.rs:169-247; KernelLoopInvocationDriver at :55-110 routing via KernelApi::invoke. Axon has no loop concept (mission.proto is unrelated multi-node DAG) — confirmed by grep, correctly absent.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — keep in CLI. Frame F-05 cleanup: minor hardening only — resume_inflight() errors are swallowed with eprintln at daemon.rs:153-155 (a failed re-arm leaves a loop silently dead after restart); consider surfacing via BootBus.emit_failed or a status flag so a non-resumed in-flight loop is observable. No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 note is n/a.

### 166. ✅ Re-register reconnect hook

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — this is device-local runtime/orchestration policy ("after MY transport reconnects, re-advertise so I'm not a stale phantom"). The two things the nodes must AGREE on are already in Axon: the reconnect machinery (ReconnectHook/ReconnectingBridge/backoff at sdk/rust/src/reconnect.rs) and the federation.advertise_agent ability surface (hub_profile.rs). The CLI hook only composes those primitives into a daemon-lifecycle closure; nothing cross-language-agreement lives in the CLI copy, so it is not a protocol fork.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — keeping the daemon's URA actually reachable/online after a transport drop is the core RUNTIME promise (a URA is only addressable if the node holding the key is registered at the Hub); a phantom-heartbeat rejection would silently take the owner device off-net, breaking ability-on-owner-device addressing.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Build and install the on_reconnect closure on the daemon's ReconnectingBridge (build_reregister_hook at src/cli/heartbeat.rs:270-293): construct caller URA, invoke federation.advertise_agent via the bridge on every successful reconnect, map ability failure to a transport error so the SDK retries. This is RUNTIME daemon lifecycle wiring — correctly CLI.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Provide the neutral primitives the hook rides on, already shipped: ReconnectHook type + ReconnectingBridge.connect/with_bridge + ReconnectConfig backoff (sdk/rust/src/reconnect.rs:52-114) and the federation.advertise_agent ability contract + receipt shape (core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/hub_profile.rs:161-471). Axon must NOT contain the daemon-specific "when/whether to re-advertise" policy — that is over-sink into the neutral protocol.
- **现状：** shipped — CLI hook at src/cli/heartbeat.rs:270-293 (wired in run_daemon, doc at 295-305); Axon reconnect primitives shipped at EasyNet-Axon/sdk/rust/src/reconnect.rs:52-114; advertise ability shipped at EasyNet-Axon/core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/hub_profile.rs:471.
- **行动：** No layer move needed; keep as CLI RUNTIME. F-05 cleanup only: the hook passes public_key_hex: String::new() (heartbeat.rs:280) — verify the Hub-side federation.advertise_agent tolerates an empty key on re-advertise (vs initial advertise) so a reconnect cannot silently re-register with a blank signing key; add an integration test that drops the daemon's bridge past the sweep window and asserts heartbeat is accepted post-reconnect (closes the phantom-heartbeat path the hook exists to fix). No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 N/A.

### 167. ✅ Schedule tick runner

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI RUNTIME) — by the boundary criterion this is "how THIS device runs/orchestrates over time," not a cross-language agreement. Verified: the schedule ontology (ScheduleEntry, MisfirePolicy Skip/FireOnce/CatchUpWindowed, cron_expr, DueFire) lives entirely in CLI src/runtime/domain.rs:161,196 and src/daemon/execution/schedule/mod.rs; Axon core/proto has zero schedule/cron/misfire concept (Axon's "scheduler" at core/runtime-rs/src/runtime/scheduler.rs is node-selection routing, an unrelated concept). No fork: the cross-language objects it emits (Invocation, CausalContext, Receipt) are correctly imported from easynet_axon, not re-declared. Pushing the tick loop / cron parsing / misfire policy into Axon would be over-sink — two nodes never need to agree on when MY device wakes its agent. No product UI leaks here (no rendering of a user surface; it only constructs the Invocation).`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** YES — this is the device that makes a URA reachable in time without a human present. It turns "owner is asleep" into "owner's agent still fires on cron, returns a Receipt, is auditable." Every fire becomes a real Invocation (caller/callee=local node URA, subject=schedule.<id> URA, args={prompt}) routed through Kernel::invoke, so a scheduled fire is indistinguishable from a Client-initiated one to subscribers of session.attach. That is exactly the endgame: ability on owner device, addressed by URA, returns a governed/auditable result.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Own the entire tick runner: the long-lived 15s loop (RUNTIME role), cron evaluation + misfire collapse via ScheduleService.due, prompt-template render, Invocation construction, and Kernel::invoke routing (EXECUTOR role). Should also own durable idempotency: a disk-backed last_fire sidecar so daemon restart does not refire — the due() docstring (mod.rs:200-202) promises "persists this in a sidecar so daemon restart does not duplicate-fire," but the runner actually keeps last_fire only in an in-memory HashMap (easynet-daemon.rs:469), and the spawn doc (lines 461-465) admits restart loses it. That gap is a CLI-layer bug, not a layer-placement issue.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the runner itself — Axon must NOT learn about cron/schedules/ticks. Axon's only correct contribution is the objects already imported: Invocation/CausalContext/Receipt shape (so a scheduled fire produces a contract-shaped, governable, auditable receipt identical to any other invoke). If a future "scheduled cause" needs to be expressible cross-language (e.g. CausalContext citing the prior fire's receipt for v2 catch-up provenance), only that CausalContext linkage is an Axon concern — the cron expression and misfire policy stay CLI.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI). Tick runner live at src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:294-302 (spawn) and 443-566 (spawn_schedule_tick); ScheduleService + DueFire + render_prompt + cron/misfire at src/daemon/execution/schedule/mod.rs (tested, lines 369-552); domain types at src/runtime/domain.rs:161,196. Partial only on durability: idempotency is in-memory (easynet-daemon.rs:469), contradicting the disk-sidecar contract claimed in mod.rs:200-202 — restart can refire per misfire policy.
- **行动：** Keep in CLI as RUNTIME+EXECUTOR; no Axon work, no RFC-003 sec0 note needed (not an Axon-repo change). Single follow-up (F-05 cleanup): make last_fire idempotency disk-backed to honor the mod.rs:200-202 docstring, or correct the docstring to match the in-memory reality and document the per-policy refire-on-restart behavior. Either way reconcile easynet-daemon.rs:461-469 with execution/schedule/mod.rs:198-202 so the persistence contract is not silently false.

### 168. ✅ SIGTERM/Ctrl-C shutdown wait (wait_for_shutdown_signal)

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI/RUNTIME). This is how THIS device's daemon process stays alive and reacts to OS termination — pure device-local runtime lifecycle. Two different-language nodes do NOT need to agree on whether this process installs a SIGTERM vs ctrl_c handler to interoperate, so it is not contract and must not sink into Axon. The SIGTERM tokens that DO live in Axon (invoke.proto:539-545 PtySignal, 769-771 SupervisorSpec cancel_grace) are a different concept entirely: the contract semantics of cancelling an in-flight ability/PTY invocation, not the daemon's own process lifecycle. No fork, no over-sink, no UI leak.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — keeping the daemon alive until a real OS signal is exactly what makes the user's identity key resident and the URA reachable/online, which is the RUNTIME pillar of the endgame. But it is PARTIAL because the graceful side is incomplete: control.json (the front-door descriptor the surface FFI dials) is left stale on SIGTERM (daemon.rs:34-36), so on an unclean exit a consumer can dial a dead UDS. Liveness is served; clean teardown of the addressability artifact is not yet.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this fully: (1) wait_for_shutdown_signal as the daemon's main-loop terminator (daemon.rs:399-420, awaited at :377); (2) the still-missing graceful cleanup — on signal, remove ~/.easynet/control.json and drain the control-plane accept loop before exit so the addressability artifact never outlives the process (gap noted daemon.rs:34-36). This is the RUNTIME pillar's responsibility, not a contract.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a contract. Axon must NOT learn what an OS signal does to a daemon process. Pushing daemon-lifecycle signal handling into Axon would over-sink runtime concerns into the neutral protocol. (Axon legitimately defines invocation-cancel signal semantics via PtySignal / SupervisorSpec.cancel_grace_seconds, but that is the lifecycle of an ABILITY invocation, distinct from the host daemon's own shutdown and not what this feature is.)
- **现状：** partial — signal-wait shipped and correct at src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:399-420 (unix SIGTERM with ctrl_c fallback on install error; ctrl_c-only on non-unix), invoked at :377. Graceful cleanup missing: control.json removal on SIGTERM explicitly NOT implemented, documented at daemon.rs:34-36; only the OS-freed UDS socket file is reclaimed on exit, the JSON descriptor is left for the next start to overwrite.
- **行动：** Implement graceful shutdown cleanup in CLI: after wait_for_shutdown_signal() returns at daemon.rs:377, remove ~/.easynet/control.json and stop the control-plane accept loop before process exit (closes the gap at daemon.rs:34-36). Frame tag F-05 (cleanup — completing the runtime teardown path). No Axon-repo work, so no RFC-003 sec0 note needed; keep this entirely CLI-local.

## 可观测性 / Observability（13）

### 169. 🔴 Call QoS metrics reporting

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly) — the QoS metrics SHAPE must be agreed cross-node: CLI src/cli/groups/call.rs:312 forwards voice.report_metrics over invoke_via_federation_forward (call.rs:170), so a participant node and the call-host node must agree on the metrics field set to interoperate = CONTRACT. Axon already pins it: voice.proto:145 VoiceNetworkMetrics + voice.proto:347 ReportCallMetricsRequest/Response + VoiceAdaptationDecision. But CLI keeps a loose local copy: ability.toml types metrics as bare object (no inner schema), and the handler stores last_metrics as opaque Option<Value> (voice_call_ability.rs:67,348) with the CLI verb hardcoding placeholder concealed_samples=0 / audio_level_dbov=-26.0 (call.rs:309-310). Same contract, two incompatible definitions = protocol fork.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — media-plane QoS feedback (rtt/jitter/loss) feeding VoiceAdaptationDecision is real value for an owner-device call that returns a Surface, but QoS telemetry is media-transport health, adjacent to the identity+state+audit+URA endgame rather than core to it. It serves the voice/conference ability surface, not the ownership/governance spine.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Keep the RUNTIME/EXECUTOR/FRONT-DOOR slice only: the `easynet call metrics` verb (human front door), the federation-forward of the invoke, and the device-side handler that actually samples/stores the snapshot. The metrics field shape itself must be IMPORTED from Axon (typed VoiceNetworkMetrics), not re-declared. CLI should drop the hardcoded placeholder values and the opaque Value blob, binding to the Axon-defined message — the dendrite-bridge mapping (control/voice.rs:58 voice_metrics_from_json) is the correct precedent the CLI ability should mirror.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Already owns it and should remain the single source of truth: VoiceNetworkMetrics (voice.proto:145), CallParticipant.latest_metrics (voice.proto:169), ReportCallMetricsRequest/Response + VoiceAdaptationDecision (voice.proto:153,347-358). Axon is the neutral cross-language contract any node imports so two nodes agree on the QoS field set and the adaptation-decision return shape.
- **现状：** forked — strong typed contract shipped in Axon (EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/voice.proto:145,347; mapped in core/runtime-rs/dendrite-bridge/src/control/voice.rs:58-105,375) AND a loose divergent local copy shipped in CLI (EasyNet-Cli/abilities/system/voice.report_metrics.ability.toml metrics:object; src/runtime/agents/voice_call_ability.rs:67,335-348 opaque Value; src/cli/groups/call.rs:304-321 hardcoded placeholders).
- **行动：** F-07 ontology-absorption: reconcile the fork by making CLI consume Axon's VoiceNetworkMetrics/ReportCallMetricsRequest as the contract rather than re-declaring it — tighten ability.toml input_schema.properties.metrics to the Axon field set, replace voice_call_ability.rs last_metrics Option<Value> with the typed shape, and drop the hardcoded concealed_samples/audio_level_dbov placeholders in call.rs:309-310 (sample or omit). Keep verb + federation-forward + handler in CLI. RFC-003 sec0 note: the Axon-side work is already landed (voice.proto), so this is a CLI-absorbs-Axon-contract change, not new Axon protocol — no new proto, just bind CLI to the existing one.

### 170. 🔴 observe.health smoke probe ability

- **裁决：** `FORKED (exists in two repos incompatibly). The ability NAME, owner (device), and PUBLIC visibility are an Axon-ratified contract: AXON-RFC-001 restatement mapping §2 line 186 (`Health | device | observe.health | any | PUBLIC | {} | {status, details}`) and Axon hub_profile.rs:2183 register `observe.health` PUBLIC. Two different-language nodes must AGREE on this probe's shape to interoperate, so it is CONTRACT. CLI ships the right NAME but a divergent PAYLOAD: ping.rs returns `{echo, replied_at_unix_ms}`, while the Axon-ratified shape is `{status, details}` (proto HealthResponse: status/uptime_ms/version/components). The ROLE (does-my-device-dispatch-work liveness) is legitimately CLI RUNTIME; the RESPONSE SHAPE is a CLI-local protocol fork of an Axon contract.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it is plumbing, not a user-owned ability that returns a Surface. It serves the endgame indirectly: a device-owned PUBLIC liveness probe is exactly how a remote caller (or easynet-verify / network_health fan-out) confirms a URA is actually reachable/online before trusting any real ability. But on its own it carries no identity/state/audit value the user sees; doctor.rs and status.rs use it purely as a daemon-wired smoke target.
- **CLI 预期实现：** RUNTIME concern: keep registering a device-owned `observe.health` handler on the daemon as the smoke target proving the four dispatch stages (IPC envelope, resolver, dispatcher loopback/gateway, handler return) are wired — this is correctly CLI. But the handler must EMIT the Axon-ratified response shape (`status`, plus `details`/`uptime_ms`/`version`/`components`), not a bespoke `{echo, replied_at_unix_ms}`. CLI may add the echo/timestamp as extra fields for its own smoke diagnostics, but the contracted fields must be present so a cross-language caller gets `{status, details}`.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Axon already owns the contract shape: HealthRequest{} / HealthResponse{status, uptime_ms, version, components} in observe.proto, restated as the device ability `observe.health` (PUBLIC) in RFC-001 §2. Axon should be the single source of the request/response shape (proto + any generated/SDK constant for the field set) that the CLI handler conforms to. Axon does NOT run the probe — the device daemon does — it only defines the shape both sides agree on.
- **现状：** forked. CLI: abilities/system/observe.health.ability.toml + src/runtime/agents/ping.rs:57-63 return `{echo, replied_at_unix_ms}`. Axon contract: core/proto/axon/v1/observe.proto:46-54 (HealthRequest/HealthResponse), docs/rfc/AXON-RFC-001-restatement-mapping.md:186 (`observe.health … {status, details}`), core/runtime-rs/src/services/invocation/hub_profile.rs:2183 (registers PUBLIC). Same name, incompatible payloads — a cross-language caller invoking the URA gets a different object shape depending on whether it hits the CLI handler or an Axon-conformant runtime.
- **行动：** F-05 cleanup: reconcile the CLI ping.rs handler response to the Axon-ratified `{status, details}` HealthResponse shape (status="healthy" plus optional uptime_ms/version/components), keeping the existing echo/timestamp only as additive smoke-diagnostic fields; pin the contracted fields in a fixture test so a future rename/reshape trips. The ability stays CLI-owned RUNTIME plumbing — do not move the handler into Axon. RFC-003 sec0 note: the shape source-of-truth edit (if a constant/field-set is added) lands in Axon repo and must cite AXON-RFC-001 §2 observe.proto mapping; the field set itself is already ratified (F-01 sec7 Axon-absorbs-back precedent), so no re-litigation needed — this is conformance, not a new contract.

### 171. ⬜ SLO / health aggregation metrics surface

- **裁决：** `MISSING (with a FORKED-shape risk). Two halves: (a) the SLO/event SCHEMA — indicator names ("invoke_success_rate","p99_latency_ms"), window tokens ("1h"/"24h"/"7d"), SLOIndicatorKind, BurnRate math, EventEntry/WatchEvents filter grammar — is something a Python node and a Rust node must AGREE on to aggregate across a federation, so it is CONTRACT and belongs in Axon (it already exists as proto in observe.proto + RFC-001 mapping rows, but the Observe service was collapsed out and never re-landed, so the contract is dangling/unimplemented). (b) The COMPUTATION of those indicators over a node's own ledger, plus the operator surface, is how this device rolls up its own runtime state = CLI RUNTIME/EXECUTOR. The trap to avoid: CLI inventing its own SLO field names/window math (= protocol fork of a shape that must be cross-language agreed); and Axon trying to compute a node's ledger rollup (= product/runtime over-sink). Net today: neither half is shipped, so MISSING, but the Axon shape must be reaffirmed before CLI computes against it.`
- **当前所在：** none-missing
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — "auditable by easynet-verify" and "observable means operable" are endgame properties, so an SLO/error-budget rollup over the ledger directly serves owner-visible operability. But it is downstream operability polish, not on the critical path of identity+URA+Surface+governance; the owner can own state and invoke abilities without it. High value for trust/operations, not foundational.
- **CLI 预期实现：** A device.observe.slo ability (and a device.observe.events stream ability for general op_events, sibling to the existing op_event emission) that READS this node's local ledger/op_events and COMPUTES the indicators — success rate, p50/p99 latency, error budget / burn rate over a window — emitting values into the Axon-defined SLOIndicator/BurnRateEntry/EventEntry shapes. Plus the human front-door: an `easynet slo` / `doctor`-style verb and the MCP-server exposure. This is RUNTIME (rollup of the device's own ledger) + EXECUTOR + DUAL FRONT DOOR; it must NOT define new indicator names/window tokens of its own — it fills the Axon contract.
- **Axon 预期实现：** Own the neutral SLO/observability SCHEMA only: SLOIndicator (name, target, current, in_compliance, window, kind, threshold_target), GetSLOStatus/GetBurnRate request/response shapes, and EventEntry/WatchEvents filter grammar — already drafted in core/proto/axon/v1/observe.proto + AXON-RFC-001-restatement-mapping.md §observe (rows observe.get_slo_status / get_burn_rate / watch_events, hub-scoped). Axon must NOT compute the rollup or hold ledger state; it is the cross-language agreement on what an indicator/window/event MEANS so federation aggregation is well-defined. The dangling contract (service collapsed in services/mod.rs, tests `// mod observe; // mod policy;` disabled) needs to be re-ratified as the canonical shape, decoupled from the deleted hub Observe service.
- **现状：** missing (no computation anywhere). Axon: proto messages present at /Users/macbook.silan.tech/Documents/GitHub/EasyNet-Axon/core/proto/axon/v1/observe.proto:126-174 (GetSLOStatus/SLOIndicator/GetBurnRate) and state_sync.proto:95-97 (slo_in_compliance/invoke_success_rate_24h/p99_latency_ms), mapped in docs/rfc/AXON-RFC-001-restatement-mapping.md:189-191; BUT the Observe service is removed from core/runtime-rs/src/services/mod.rs (no observe module; only admin/capability/control_plane/federation/identity/invocation/mission_*), and the observe+policy tests are commented out in src/tests/mod.rs:724-725. Only a frozen docker-context snapshot under examples/reference/.../artifacts/ implements GetSloStatus, and those indicators were node-internal (policy_eval_p99, cache_hit_ratio) not ledger-derived invocation SLOs. CLI: only point probes — observe.health (ping.rs) and observe.network_health (src/runtime/agents/network_health_ability.rs, explicitly "without building a second monitoring stack"); no device.observe.slo, no events-stream ability; grep for slo/p99/error-budget computation in CLI src returns only comments (admin_status_ability.rs:28 "not a full SLO", presence_registry.rs:107 "P99 lookups").
- **行动：** new (F-07 ontology-absorption, RFC-003 sec0 applies — Axon-repo work). Step 1 (Axon, RFC-003 sec0): re-ratify the SLO/event SCHEMA as a standalone neutral contract decoupled from the deleted hub Observe service — confirm SLOIndicator/BurnRateEntry/EventEntry shapes, indicator-name registry, and window-token grammar in observe.proto are the canonical cross-language agreement; do NOT re-introduce a hub-side computation service. Step 2 (CLI, F-05 surface): land device.observe.slo + device.observe.events abilities that compute the indicators from this node's own ledger/op_events and fill the Axon shapes, exposed via an `easynet slo` verb and the MCP server. Per INV-3 these are methods on the device Agent, not a global metrics registry. Sequence Axon-first so CLI computes against a ratified shape rather than forking field names.

### 172. ✅ Boot event bus + staged boot timeline

- **裁决：** `CORRECT — belongs in CLI. By the boundary criterion this is RUNTIME-local: it is a process-local in-memory broadcast bus (tokio::sync::broadcast) describing how THIS daemon brings up its own stages (kernel, control-server, tenant-stores, ability-registry, daemon-invocation-transport, pages-listener, accept-invokes). Two different-language nodes never need to AGREE on these stage names to interoperate — the stages ARE the CLI's internal boot sequence, so this is not contract. It is exposed over control.sock via system.watch_boot BEFORE the Axon runtime exists (boot_events.rs:5-9), which is precisely why it cannot be an Axon concept. Sinking it into Axon would be over-sink (pollute the neutral protocol with one runtime's startup internals); it carries no product UI, so no leak the other direction.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it does not directly touch identity/URA/Surface/audit, but it makes the RUNTIME daemon (the thing that makes a URA reachable/online) observable as it comes up; a user owning their own device-resident runtime needs to know when kernel->Ready has happened and which port the local Surface listener chose. It serves the endgame indirectly by making the owner's runtime trustworthy/legible, not by being part of the addressable/decidable/governable contract.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI SHOULD own exactly this: a runtime-local boot progress bus + history replay so the dual-front-door `easynet start` (human) can attach as soon as control.sock accepts and render the kernel->Ready timeline, with PortChosen surfacing fallback ports and each emit dual-logged to easynet-daemon.log for headless runs. This is the EXECUTOR/RUNTIME concern. Shipped at src/services/control/boot_events.rs:35-259, emitted at src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:103-375.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a contract. Axon must NOT gain a boot/stage timeline concept; confirmed absent from Axon proto (core/proto/axon/v1/*.proto) and SDK (only build-artifact false positives). Stage names are CLI runtime internals, not a cross-language interop shape. Keeping it out of Axon respects DEC-F01-Q9 (realtime/progress must not pollute the two-RPC Receipt surface).
- **现状：** shipped — BootBus/BootEvent/BootStageStatus/BootSubscription fully implemented with replay history + dual-logging and unit tests at src/services/control/boot_events.rs:35-363; wired end-to-end through every daemon stage at src/bin/easynet-daemon.rs:102-375; surfaced via system.watch_boot (referenced daemon.rs:368). No fork in Axon.
- **行动：** No layer move needed — leave in CLI as-is (RUNTIME/EXECUTOR observability). Frame tag F-05 cleanup: optional follow-up only — the PortChosen `start: Option<u16>` back-compat default (boot_events.rs:42-49) is the one evolving edge; if the daemon/CLI version skew is closed, drop the Option and make start required to remove the "unknown start" ambiguity. No RFC-003 sec0 note (no Axon-repo work).

### 173. ✅ Boot watcher / staged start UX

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI / RUNTIME + FRONT-DOOR). The only Axon `boot` token is BootstrapConfigEntry in admin.proto (unrelated config reload).`

But staged-start UX is not itself part of the owns-identity / URA-addressed / Surface-returning / Axon-governed loop; it is the device-local "am I online yet" affordance underneath it.
- **CLI 预期实现：** Own the entire feature: daemon spawn + control.sock readiness probe + system.watch_boot subscription over the local control transport, BootEvent IR, StageRenderer presentation, and the stuck-before-stage-1 watchdogs (SOCKET_WAIT_BUDGET, NO_EVENT_WATCHDOG, child try_wait). This is RUNTIME (bringing the URA online) plus FRONT-DOOR (human-facing staged UX). Shipped here today.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a — not a cross-language contract. Daemon local-boot progress must NOT enter Axon proto/SDK; doing so would be over-sink polluting the neutral protocol with a single-device startup concern (parallels DEC-F01-Q9: device-local realtime must not pollute the contract surface).
- **现状：** shipped — src/cli/start_boot_watcher.rs:74-267 (wait_for_daemon_boot, subscribe_boot_events, apply_event); BootEvent/BootBus in src/services/control/boot_events.rs:33-213; WATCH_BOOT_ABILITY + handler in src/services/control/server.rs:68,339-347; presentation in src/cli/presentation/stage.rs. No Axon counterpart (correctly).
- **行动：** No re-layering needed — feature is in the correct layer and shipped. Frame tag F-05 cleanup: keep BootEvent CLI-internal; add a guard/note so daemon boot-stage types are never promoted into Axon proto, and confirm the WATCH_BOOT path stays on control.sock (not the two-RPC Axon surface). No Axon-repo work, so RFC-003 sec0 n/a.

### 174. ✅ Daemon log tail/follow

- **裁决：** `CORRECT (CLI). By the boundary criterion this is "how THIS device's local runtime exposes its own state to a human operator" — no second-language node must agree on a plaintext log file on disk to interoperate, so it is not contract. It is distinct from Axon's observe.proto (WatchEvents/EventEntry/QueryAuditLog/NetworkHealth), which is the cross-node observability contract; this feature does not fork that — it tails a local file (easynet-daemon.log / axon.log) selected by local RuntimeKind. Pushing local file-tail into Axon would be over-sink.`
- **当前所在：** cli
- **符合 EasyNet 价值：** PARTIAL — it serves the operator-of-this-device need (is my runtime alive, what is it doing) which is real for the daemon-holds-identity-key runtime, but raw plaintext file tail is not on the endgame audit path (user-owned, URA-addressed, easynet-verify-auditable). It is dev/ops ergonomics, not the governable audit chain.
- **CLI 预期实现：** CLI owns this (RUNTIME + FRONT-DOOR): resolve the local runtime log path by RuntimeKind, print last N lines, optionally stream new lines for the human operator. This is the correct home. Only refinement: the naive 250ms poll-at-EOF loop never re-opens on rotation/truncation (seek-to-End after rotation strands the reader) — a runtime-hardening detail, not a layer issue.
- **Axon 预期实现：** n/a for the file-tail itself — not a contract. Axon already owns the cross-language observability contract in core/proto/axon/v1/observe.proto (HealthResponse, NetworkHealthSummary, QueryAuditLog, WatchEvents/EventEntry, SLO/BurnRate). If the CLI ever wants to stream structured runtime EVENTS (not raw file lines) to another node or surface, it should consume that WatchEvents contract — but that is a separate feature from local log tail.
- **现状：** shipped (CLI). easynet runtime logs --tail/-f implemented at src/cli/groups/runtime.rs:78-142 (run_logs + resolve_runtime_log_path); RuntimeKind/state_dir at src/persistence/config.rs:185,228. No fork of Axon observe.proto; grep of src/ shows only this site does raw file tail (read_line/SeekFrom).
- **行动：** Keep in CLI; no Axon work. F-05 cleanup: harden the follow loop against log rotation/truncation (detect inode/size shrink, re-open + reset to start) and consider sharing the RuntimeKind->log-path resolution with start/stop/status so the path is defined once. No RFC-003 sec0 note (not Axon-repo work).

### 176. ✅ observe.network_health

Bounded single-shot probe, not a monitoring stack. Belongs in EXECUTOR/RUNTIME layer.
